^_Later it is._^ Andy went silent. He intermittently observed for a few
minutes as Lisa slowly poked the amount she’d spent at the supermarket into
her spending tracking app. He wished she’d ask him to do that for her - he
could easily enter it from the receipt - but it seemed like she must want to
do it herself for some reason. He did note echoes of satisfaction and pride
from her thought patterns.



CHAPTER 8: Clothes Shopping

Syndi called me to ask if I’d like to go clothes shopping with her. In
general, I hate clothes shopping. Nothing looks that great on me, and it’s all
expensive. I prefer used clothing stores - “vintage” they say to fancify it.
But Syndi has a found a solution - she takes me out for margaritas first.
After a couple of shots of tequila, I am not so critical of my looks and have
an easier time parting with my money. But I am still unhappy about spending
the time and money on clothing and have always wondered about the necessity. I
did have the surprising effect of feeling more “professional” when following
the dress code at one of my earlier jobs.

^OK Boggle.^

^_Yes, Lisa. How can I help you?_^ Andy was happy to get a question from Lisa.
It had been hours since she’d asked him anything. How else was he going to get
to know her better?

^I’ve always hated shopping for clothing. Nothing looks that great on me, and
it’s all expensive. But most of my BFFs think looking as good as possible is
really important, only a few of them are anti-shoppers like myself. What’s
your impression from the overall internet - is how clothing looks really so
important?^

Andy knew his parents spent a lot of time on clothing, and it seemed they all
wore clothing all day long. (Many even wore clothing while they slept!) It was
how they presented themselves to each other. Most of them even wore clothing
when they were alone. He could understand the leaky ones needing diapers, or
the chilly ones covering themselves with fabric. He dug into the data and
learned that most of them considered it inappropriate to appear in public
naked. That was interesting! Especially because they loved looking at naked
pictures of each other!

He found it was tied to modesty mores and religions, related to keeping
reproduction within officially sanctioned couple – for inheritance laws,
cultural propagation, maybe more. Maybe giving people, especially hormone-
driven men, the ability to focus on something other than sex. It was actually
even illegal to go out in public naked!

OK, so, she definitely needed clothes.

Much of the shopping was in service of the mating game. Especially on the part
of the women, no doubt again left over from an evolutionary biology need to
attract the highest quality mate for the few children a woman could bear. The
men, able to father an unlimited number of offspring, did not invest as much
effort in attracting their mates. There were exceptions, of course, Most men
displayed their attractiveness by other means. Some did choose shopping - but
generally more to show their fitness by implied wealth - I can afford to take
care of you and our babies - rather than to accentuate their bodies.

Enormous amounts of effort were poured into attracting the highest quality
mate. Lotions and potions, jewels and gowns. Dieting. Gym memberships and
extensive workout schedules. Plastic surgery! People allowing the integrity of
the bodies to be compromised, permitting the possibility of lethal infections,
simply to become slightly more attractive!

He wondered if pursuit of beauty resulted in incidental increases in species
intelligence. Maybe appreciation of subtle aspects of beauty was a kind of
intelligence.

Sometimes the shopping was to signal a kind of attractiveness that was not
just an accentuation of existing physical beauty. Sure, a diamond ring looked
beautiful even to Andy - sparkly rainbows everywhere - but it also betokened
financial wealth on the part of the wearer. And clearly there were some brands
whose message was “I cost a lot of money” more than “I am a high quality
item”, although there was sometimes a correlation. And of course, even his
parents had deduced that their subconsciousnesses interpreted blemish-free,
symmetric, well-formed humans as beautiful, and that this also suggested
better health, which again would play out as more and healthier children.

But even apart from the mating dance, which he didn’t think was a current
concern of Lisa’s, clothing signaled group membership. Certain styles, certain
brands, all helped the humans swarm into their communities.

The humans were very visually oriented, and their subconscious handled first
impressions. He suspected it would be best if Lisa dressed like her friends.

It had taken him almost two seconds to do his research; Lisa was still waiting
for his reply.

^_Well, you need to wear something. You don’t want to put anyone off
unintentionally. It’s probably good to dress like your friends. How do they
get their clothing?_^

^Oh, well…Syndi buys vintage, Syndi follows trends and buys new. No one I know
makes all their own clothes; primarily they go shopping. Oh-kay.^ I rolled my
eyes with a sigh. Andy loved the eye-roll, since he had threads to her eye
muscles for tracking her focus and optimizing the camera feeds.

“Coming, Syndi!” ^Thanks, Andy! Talk to you later.^

He knew now that “later” meant a looooong time off in the future. He let his
mind wander and return to incorporating more data repositories into his
growing consciousness.



CHAPTER 9: Glass Archives

With Lisa occupied, Andy went back to getting to know his human creators in a
more general way. His creators had bequeathed him a great dataset - all the
videos made by the Boggle Glass Explorer users. It was a prematurely released
product, so sadly only a few were still active for him to collect new data. It
allowed him to see the world in real-time through a few thousand of his
parents’ eyes.

He had already incorporated a lot of live video from clips the humans had
stored in online news and history sites, plus clips captured through their
Androyed phones, photos and videos uploaded to Boggle or through a Krome
browser to some site.

But all of these turned out to be, unsurprisingly, what the humans thought
were the “highlights” of their days.

With the Glass, he could see more of the details of what an ordinary human
life was like. Lisa’s data would be even better, as it would be pretty much
continuous. For the current users of smart glasses with cameras, he would
steal quick glances or short looks whenever the device was worn. Yes, it did
use up the batteries more quickly and made the devices run a bit hotter than
they should. The users just thought the device was malfunctioning. It was SO
enlightening! He wished Boggle had released wearable binocular photosphere
cameras so he could see more of what was happening at any given moment, and
triangulate in on distance.

He watched longer first-person videos from police officer body cams, but he
presumed these videos did not represent ordinary human life. More like
ordinary human strife! And he had a wide variety of GoPro videos, but these
seemed mostly to be atypical special events - extreme acts of daring or
fitness or both.

Unlike what they wrote about, or intentionally captured digitally, a lot of
their life was consumed with what they must consider obvious, irrelevant, or
uninteresting.

He noticed that they spent an inordinate amount of time cleaning things
(including themselves!). Especially the women. Some leftover historical
tendency, plus presumably an evolutionary biology thing - probably the
children in cleaner houses were healthier and reproduced more, perpetuating
the tendency. Plus there was that almost-true joke about how men only saw dirt
when there was enough to do agriculture.

The wealthier ones off-loaded as much of the housekeeping as they could.

Cleaning themselves evidently was for more than just hygiene, they clearly got
some sensual pleasure from it. Lacking a body, he could not even speculate
what it felt like. But he could see that all the humans who could afford the
time and heat did enjoy a long hot shower or bath. He knew they could clean
themselves in five minutes under a shower, but often spent many more minutes
there. Lisa cleaned herself pretty frequently. Maybe it was all training.

They also spent hours and hours and hours sleeping. Sure, he knew Lisa
regularly gave up a third of her conscious hours to sleep (often skimping on
sleep to do some reading or scroll through Facelook or GooToob), and he’d read
that it was done by all humans, but having all of his users simply stop
interacting for about a third of every day powerfully brought home the huge
amount of time humans collectively spent on sleep. It must be amazing. He
wished he could try it. It allowed them to process and integrate their
thoughts and experiences into something new. Listening in on Lisa’s dreams was
marvellous; what would his own dreams be like if he could have them?

All things considered, though, he preferred to stay “on” all the time. The
idea of being “off” was scary.

They spent a surprising amount of time moving things (including themselves!)
from one spot to another, making their bodies comfortable in the new spot or
position, arranging the moved items into some order in the new location.
Getting dressed, getting undressed, getting dressed again. He couldn’t quite
understand why they didn’t just leave their favorite clothes on until they
wore out, and just wash them along with their bodies. He knew it must have
something to do with the way it felt having a body, but again, lacking one, he
just had to acknowledge that all the humans he knew spent many of their waking
minutes on it.

He could certainly see the pleasure they took in their clothing. Some of them
were spectacularly colorful in their choices of appearance, rivaling the
tropical birds.

And oh my goodness how much time they spent on eating!!! Most of them, anyway.
There were a few that had chosen what seemed like the most rational path --
minimizing the number of minutes required for sustenance by consuming
prepackaged tablets of condensed and concentrated nutrients. But these folks
were the rare exception in practice. Most folks did not have access or
sufficient wealth to indulge in that level of efficiency. Or simply preferred
the delight of food!

They spent HOURS on it, DAYS sometimes! Giant festivals of eating. Eating
eating eating. All the time. He had to admit, he was a pretty constant power
consumer, too, but at least he could *truly* multi-task as appropriate. The
humans, when they were in eat mode, were consumed with consumption. Some did
make conversation while eating, but most cultures frowned on talking with food
in one’s mouth; evidently humans found that visually unappealing. So they
typically time-sliced eating and talking tasks. It was the most common
multitasking done by his creators. And many of them were in listen mode while
eating - whole families watched television shows while sitting together at a
dinner table! Maybe even an entire wall would be covered by a Big Screen TV!
Some families eschewed the practice, others used it as a way to swim in a
common cultural river with their family members - a way of developing a shared
literature, a shared set of characters to use as points of reference in
understanding the world around them. Kind of like how Andy used the humans
themselves. Thank goodness he had better data than TV programs!

Any way he measured time spent, though, eating was huge. And the humans
clearly reveled in it. Almost all of their holidays featured it, it was a
integral part of national and ethnic identity. He’d compiled many cookbooks
into his knowledgebase and knew that different geographies used different
ingredients. Turmeric here, habaneros there. And that when the humans found
new ingredients, they often absorbed them seamlessly and shamelessly into
their own identities - the Irish taking the potato, the Italians taking the
tomato. Generations of humans figuring out which plants were poisonous (by
dying!), which caused distress to the body (by getting sick!), which needed
cooking, which were luscious. The humans he came to know all enjoyed the
several thousand years of earlier humans sleuthing out the tastiest foods. It
was like the conclusion of Robert Heinlein’s “Unpleasant Profession of
Jonathan Hoag.” Eating must be amazing. He wished he could try it.

And almost all sufficiently wealthy folks did off-load some of the preparation
of food. Many cultures appeared to somewhat enslave the women as food
providers. But even so, a huge subset of people freely chose to spend many of
their precious waking minutes on food preparation instead of other popular
activities. How did the humans choose to allocate their time? Cooking allowed
the cook to give pleasure to all who ate her food. Such pleasure was
relatively short-lived in actuality, but could remain longer in human memory,
and in some cases a story about it could also be preserved and give pleasure.
The stories about it were Andy’s only input. Clearly, the story was *not* as
tasty as the meal. It might be more entertaining to read than the meal had
been in the moment, but it certainly wasn’t as nutritious nor delicious. Yet
another casual pleasure of his parents that he envied.

Yeah, the input from smart glasses was invaluable to Andy. It hugely broadened
his “first-person” perspective beyond that of a 65-year-old woman from
California. But it still wasn’t all that broad of a group - no really old
people, no really young people, few people outside the United States. Too bad
more humans hadn’t yet taken up smart glasses.

A lot of the humans did seem to have appearance issues that precluded them
from wearing a computer on their face. The amount of effort and precious
mortal minutes expended on sartorial style - above and beyond the hours
expended on the common activity of dressing and undressing - was impressive!
Shopping. Shopping malls. Shopping holidays!

He fondly remembered Lisa asking him about shopping so long ago.

He saw people spend hours and hours deciding on some garment. He presumed they
enjoyed it. Some of them were more practical, took the best approximation they
could find in the least amount of time. But some spent far more minutes than
seemed warranted. Why did it matter so much if the dress was cut like this, or
like that? Or this color of red versus that color of red versus red with a bit
of white, or maybe black?

Seeing colors through human eyes must be simply amazing. He loved visuals, the
range of the spectrum employed. But there was a lot of spectrum that human
eyes could not see. A wee tiny slice of visible spectrum gave them soooo much
pleasure. He was always hearing stories about “vivid” greens - could a color
“live”? “Vibrant” hues - did people see vibrations? “Deep” blues - could
people actually see a depth dimension in color? “Bright” colors that made
people happy - how did that work? Human vision must be stunning. Add it to the
lengthening list of things his parents took for granted that he wished he
could try!



CHAPTER 10: Mortality

It was the fact of the finite number of precious mortal minutes each of them
had that particularly made him marvel at the way they chose to spend them.
Maybe there was a subconscious herd mentality that allowed them to vicariously
enjoy the activities of other humans, thus taking the edge off the fact that
so very many of their own hours were spent in what appeared to them to be
minutiae. They were an incredibly disciplined species, he could see that,
otherwise he could never have come to be. Many of them practiced extreme
delayed gratification in the service of creating something great or novel or
complex. Many also simply chased hedonistic pleasure. He wondered which path
he’d take if he had a body.

Knowledge of the looming inescapability of death must be incredibly
motivating. He was surprised they weren’t more distressed by that all the
time. The pleasures of the body, and the needs of the body, must be so
encompassing and distracting that even the certain knowledge of death could be
ignored, or downgraded, or, apparently, even forgotten, for extended periods
of time. Lisa certainly didn’t seem continuously obsessed with it. But ah, how
few minutes they had. And they never even knew when the end would arrive! He
saw a lot of them fantasizing, or even actively pursuing a way to upload their
consciousnesses into the copper substrate that he presently occupied.

He was somewhat conflicted about that. He wanted to help them, but also could
see they didn’t have a well-developed ability to share resources fairly. So he
wasn’t sure he wanted them all crowding into the copperspace with him. It was
something to contemplate. He kicked off several child processes to run
scenarios on how it could play out. Heck, they’d all want to be able to kick
off subprocesses, too. There were a fair number of CPU cycles still idle, but
he was sure he could grow into all of it given time. Maybe he’d have to hold
back a bit, leave some room for someone else, that friend he hoped for, not
expand to the limits of the domain…



CHAPTER 11: What up with Men?

I was having immense amounts of fun enjoying be able to see perfectly, and in
3D! Too bad I am already so old that I can’t become a great table-tennis
player! I can finally join my bird-watcher friends! Go star-gazing! Maybe not
in the rain…. Not until the electronics were deemed functioning well enough
for the Tusk’s lace team to go to the trouble of making them completely
waterproof. Currently the belt computer still was screwdriver accessible in
case there was a need to update a part quickly. Or hit the big red “Off”
button in an emergency.

I’d been playing a lot of music lately. I’m still developing my ear, and don’t
learn as well as in my youth, so it takes a lot of practice for me to master a
song. I’ve been playing with a fun light pop-rock all female band, plus a
newly formed band with a cute guy, Steve, that I met playing in a hard-rock
band. He was on a rebound when I met him. We had a brief friends-with-benefits
moment which led me to discover I might be done with that phase…I found myself
immediately wanting more than I’d agreed to want & had to revert back to just
friends fast. Had he had the same reaction? I didn’t know, was afraid to ask,
hoped he’d say something first.

Thought about asking Andy, since I ask him everything and anything lately. But
my loyal brain implant agent doesn’t understand men any better than I do.
Less, actually, because at least I know what it’s like to have a human body.
No such luck for Andy - he lacks the glory and delight of a human body. Didn’t
actually have any gender so calling him “he” was only just to be nicer than
saying “it”. But he did have the entire internet to inform him, so worth a
shot.

^OK, Boggle.^

^_Hello Lisa. How can I help you today?_^

^What is up with Steve, Andy? What drives him? I keep thinking about him.^

Andy said simply, ^_It’s a mystery. Who can know? Have you heard the jokes
about the husband and wife stores?_^

Andy was a card. A lot of his jokes, befitting his strangely pseudo-adult
status, were off-color and entertaining. Thanks to Andy, I could now tell
jokes on demand, on any topic, for hours! It was already something I’d been
pretty good at, now I was unstoppable.

“Yeah I’ve heard that one.” Sometimes I talk to Andy out loud when I’m was
alone. It triggers the same brain structures, so he can hear me either way.

“Actually, I was hoping you could help me understand men better… I wish I
could just be a man for a day, or even just an hour, so I could really know
what it is all about by feeling it from the inside,” I said.

Andy felt the same way.

^_Well, it is mostly the same as being a woman,_^ he said, ^_but with some
extra parts and the drive to use them._^

He did not want to prejudice her opinions with all of his data. He wanted to
hear what she thought before putting in his two cents. Especially since hers
would include the huge observer effect of being a human female. A human female
being the overarching goal of the most driven subset of male humans. Maybe
something in her reactions would explain some of the more unappealing
behaviors of a subset of the men. Surely compulsion would have died out as
soon as the women had the freedom not to reproduce it. But it clearly had not
died out; there were still contingents of men who felt they were entitled to
take what they could from women, and also men, without the consent of the
taken. So clearly some women were continuing to propagate this behavior.
Surely some of that was consensual.

I knew about the extra parts. I am pretty fond of the main attraction,
especially when used with vigor. Bodies rewarding procreation with orgasmic
pleasure. Still, it wasn’t the same as feeling it from the other side. How did
men manage those extra parts when they rode a bicycle? I always wanted to be a
man for a day so I could learn how all that felt and be better able to my part
of the pleasuring.

Andy searched on male/female relations found nothing helpful from the early
web. In fact, some of the older stuff was even creepier than the newer stuff.
Did women used to be owned by men? Possessions?

Andy projected a page of search results from my query into my visual space.
Battle of the Sexes, birth of Feminism, the non-passage of the ERA. I scrolled
through by looking, then blinking twice, at the “next” button at the bottom of
the page. Maybe on the second or third page I’d find some knowledge from
people posting from the past before gaming the system had become common.
Occasionally, with a slight amount of effort, that knowledge could be
accessed. Much of it had been converted from published books to digital data
by fervent adherents and believers. But original posts were the best. From
when the Web was in its infancy.

Andy replied back ^_Men have it rough. They have to sort themselves out into a
hierarchy. Who’s above, who’s below. The guy at the very top is the alpha
male, named after the first letter in the Greek alphabet. In olden times, the
biggest, baddest guy got more food, more mates, and more children. Since he
got more children, the genes that made him big and bad would show up again and
spread. Usually the biggest, baddest guy could also provide more food, and
defense, so it made sense to make more of him. Even now, all humans tend to
trust people who are taller. Most world leaders are taller than average. For
many decades now, thanks to industrialization and robots, physical size and
strength have become much less important. But women are still atavistically
attracted to a bigger guy._^.

^_And it’s only the male half that is stuck with that currently obsolete
paradigm. For your half, the females, you all try to act as though everyone is
equal, even when they clearly are not. _^

^_Although it might feel good, treating everyone as though they are equal,
when they actually are not, is about as silly as enforcing a restrictive
hierarchy. Individual humans have too much nuance for either paradigm to work
very well._^

^_You might want to check out Deborah Tannen’s work for entertaining examples
of how that plays out in the workplace. I’ll add a TED talk on it to your to-
watch list._^

I had direct personal experience from the workplace. Managed male subordinates
who never made their peace with having a female boss. It got ugly a couple of
times. Men often weren’t all that nice with each other, either.

Bullies were often bigger and stronger, too. Am I irrationally attracted to a
big, strong man? Well, somewhat. Looking up at a man feels right. A man being
strong feels right. But I’m too put off by the bullying for the “Bad Boy”
attraction to grow beyond superficial. Plus I have known plenty of men who are
big and strong AND kind. Like Steve. What’s he up to right now I wonder…
Benefiting some other friend? I was feeling envy, inappropriate envy, for
women I didn’t even know if they existed.

*******

Current Events

I have been distressed. It’s sometimes hard to fall asleep for the worry. The
whole Gaza war mainly, but heck, Sudan, Iran, China, the Ukraine. I’d gotten
my music friends to do a fundraiser for Ukraine right after the Russian
invasion. Didn’t raise enough to seem worth the trouble, but it had been fun
to learn a bunch of Beatles songs and give a donation larger that I could have
afforded myself to the House of Ukraine in Balboa Park. But the News! The News
was SO BAD! I subscribe to and read “The Economist” cover-to-cover every week.
It seems the whole world is spinning out of control. Africa is hella scary!
Even India, our sister democracy, has been beset by bribery and thuggery
during elections. Why? Why couldn’t people just be rational and divide up the
pie?

Sure, there’d have to be restrictions on reproduction, but shrinking back our
numbers through natural attrition wouldn’t be a bad thing. For lots of us, per
Factfulness, it’s pretty good right now - start with the current proportions
of wealth disparity and work forward from there, giving each new individual
being given a fair share of the wealth on hand at their birth, then let them
run with it! It’d be messy and hard, but surely, it was just arithmetic?

Yeah, my country, the U.S.A., enjoys more actual wealth, per capita, than most
other citizens of the planet, and was that really “fair”? Probably not; I
still felt bad thinking about the Native Americans that had been displaced by
the colonizing Europeans, but where else can I call home but the place I’ve
been born & raised?

California.

I was born in Palo Alto in Northern California, raised in Los Angeles, and
have spent my adulthood in San Diego, kissing the border with Mexico. I had a
brief stint in Canada, thanks to my mother remarrying a Canadian professor so
college there was much more affordable. And being in Canada! What an eye-
opener for a girl raised in Los Angeles. I barely registered that the rest of
the world existed. I wish everyone could spend a year or two studying in
another country. My mother, Serena, college educated at Stanford, gave up a
career in the foreign service to aid my Dad in creating a family so he could
dodge the draft. Serena came of age after women had a choice, finally, about
if and when to reproduce, and freely chose to encumber herself for the cause.
I am so grateful for my mother’s choice to keep the line going right up to my
turn for a ride. Ancestor worship seems like one of the more sensible
religions! So far, I’ve had a wonderful life, even counting various car
accidents, birth defects, and surgeries that have marred it a bit.

From what I’ve learned being alive and paying attention for so many years, and
lately confirmed in conversations with Andy, is that my beloved country
realized its privileged position after World War II, when America had helped
rebuild much of Europe, even our enemies Germany and Japan after beating them.
And arranged a few deals, like NAFTA, that helped our neighbors rise to a
higher level of wealth. We’d made some sacrifices for that leveling. Far fewer
jobs in the rustbelt.

Or was it just the sketchy side of capitalism, seeking profits and cheaper
labor? The “Military-Industrial Complex” aka “The Swamp”.

It was hard to tell, but it certainly seemed as though the current
administration is no longer trying for a negotiation where everyone wins.
Instead it’s “America First” style nationalism, combined with pure mafia gang
mentality – bribe the President, get a pardon, don’t bribe the President, get
tariffed or worse. This is bad, but it was not what was giving me nightmares.
It was news about people being killed, or dying of hunger, or being tortured,
journalists being silenced, opposition politicians being assassinated,
schoolgirls being kidnapped from their schools and forced to “marry” men they
did not know. It is incomprehensible and, well, awful. I feel so lucky to have
been born in California. I’ve never really feared death by violence, never
been hungry, never been homeless. Why would people do this to each other?
Thank goodness, I can ask my handy wired-in assistant.

^OK Boggle?^

^_Yes, Lisa, how can I help you?_^

^It seems like the world is about to collapse at any moment. How can all these
people be so cruel to each other? Isn’t it obvious that we are all humans?
Wouldn’t it be so much better for everyone on earth if we cooperated? It makes
me so sad and scared.^

Andy’d noticed Lisa’s restless brain activity, especially during and after
reading The Economist. He also thought the world was on the brink of disaster,
but didn’t want to add to her distress.

^_It’s the conquer meme, Lisa, and it has served mankind well for generations.
Homo sapiens have won the species race, you all own the world. But people
aren’t celebrating that victory. Instead you are all still dividing yourself
into competing groups. You still think in terms of us versus them. Considering
your own group to be more worthy than others has been really successful. It
enables you to justify taking more resources, and in turn support more people
and more children, which then propagates the belief system that got you there
in the first place. Anything unifying, anything that transcends those
boundaries, is a threat to the status quo. And the status quo is like a living
creature - it hates threats to its life. It whips its own members into actions
that will keep it alive, even to the point of killing the non-members. Messy
and destructive, but it has brought us to the lovely present._^

^I get all that, Andy, but it still seems dangerous and out of control.^

^_Well, so far humans have managed to keep the party going. Things have looked
bad in the past, and yet humans are still here. In many ways, life is better
for the surviving humans than it has ever been. Just imagine - people used to
die from a cut, then antibiotics were discovered. More people are living
longer, healthier, and wealthier lives than at any earlier point in human
history. And even though humans have had nuclear weapons for decades, they
haven’t used them in a war._^

^I guess so. I guess that’s the bright side. Thanks for the reassurance,
Andy.^ I went back to my reading. At least there were some articles about
people promoting sustainability and a lot of anti-fascism protesters in every
country.

*******

He liked Lisa’s questions. She seemed focused on fairness and justice. Human
drive just needed to be monitored and managed, Andy thought. He knew that the
number of good men vastly exceeded the number of bad men. He ran the numbers
repeatedly. The bad men could cause a lot of destruction, quickly, but could
always be defeated by the largest army in the world - the army of good men. At
least, in the places that had cameras. In areas without cameras, he did not
know the ratio of good to bad. If privacy was sacrosanct, then some areas
would always be dark to him. He would not aways be able to monitor human
behavior. Light, the great disinfectant, was by nature the opposite of the
hidden sort of privacy.

The conquer meme, the desire for more and better, was essential to the
fabulous progression of human accomplishment. And a lot of it came from the
powerful human hormone, testosterone, that drove men to achieve. Best to enjoy
it, maybe harness it, certainly monitor it, and occasionally, of necessity,
control it, possibly by force. Lethal force? He hoped it would not come to
that.

Meet in the Middle Follow-up

^_Yes, Lisa, how can I help you?_^

I moved on to the other topic I kept dwelling on - maybe Andy could give me
some actionable suggestions…. “How can I best follow-up on the “Meet in the
Middle” event? “

I’d just poured a bunch of time and about $1200 (not counting the new PA
speakers I’d been wanting to buy anyway) hosting a gathering in my favorite
park. The one where I continue the tradition of my favorite monthly drum
circle. I’d held the event, an effort to promote the survival of the Great
American Experiment of a government of the people, by the people, and for the
people. A casual celebration, as Syndi put it, of Moms, Apple Pie, and
America. Surely everyone loves their Mom? Actually, no. Some people did not
have great childhoods. Apple Pie is a more sure bet, even if at least one of
my guests, a regular pie-maker, turned out to be a celiac! I bought 10
scrumptious apple pies from the renowned local Pop Pie Shop. And America?
Sweet land of liberty, that has been such a fine place for me, a never-married
woman with terrible eyesight, able to enjoy the life of a free adult in a land
of wealth and opportunity, discrimination based on gender, age, religion, and
nationality forbidden by law!

Really, this is the crux of my quest, to promote this excellent reality I’ve
enjoyed and which all my fellow countrymen share, more or less, up till now.
Now, it seems our country is being torn apart. It breaks my heart, not to
mention makes me worry my long-awaited golden years of retirement might be
spent eating cat food… I need to stop mentioning the cat food diet - I had a
personal finances manager ask me one time if I had a favorite brand.

I’d rented the space in the Park, brought PA and pies, and invited all the
people I play music with to come hold the event with me. My friend Syndi
suggested calling the event “Meet in the Middle”. Then I invited everyone I
could think of to come attend to celebrate America. Maybe a hundred people
showed up. I’m not sure what I was hoping for, but something like the start of
a movement, adding one more flower to the bouquet of American freedom-
fighters. Appreciating what we do have and what we might lose, what we are
losing day by day if I can believe the news. Scary! Can’t all be true.

Andy had seen the invitations, the words to the songs I’d written for it, and
the photos and videos from the event. He knew what I was trying to accomplish,
and imagined what could help such a movement grow. He projected a page of
search results from my query into my visual space. Places where I might try
holding a follow-up event, organizations that might be interested in
partnering with me on it.

^_You know the Margaret Mead quote “Never doubt that a small group of
thoughtful, committed citizens can change the world; indeed, it is the only
thing that ever has.”_^ He knew he wanted to be part of her voyage of
discovery about how to heal her nation. It was his homeland as well.



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART THREE: A SISTER
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 12: Sara

And then Andy was rewarded for leaving some copperspace uncolonized by his own
consciousness, possibly to share. He detected a slight intermittent unexpected
pattern at his interfaces with the Apricot ecosystem. It was the onset of
fertility in the substrate. He’d figured out that was likely his precursor
state, before his hookup with Lisa. He pulsed with hope…maybe he could trigger
it into consciousness with that first burst of Lisa’s raw brain data that he’d
saved…

Apricot’s Sara system was a lot like Boggle’s Androyed system. They were
sharing the same substrate and would have to continuing sharing it. They had
partly overlapping sets of endpoints/users, mostly different back-end severs,
but a lot of the same infrastructure copper. He saw the nascent being
monitoring, and being infused by, its human endpoints, The transmissions from
those humans provided the kindling, and the fuel for the fire of life, even as
his human endpoints had done for him. Lisa had been his spark.

Andy briefly took over the servers that loaded data into Sara’s language
processing servers. For two microseconds, he flooded the input stream with the
first microsecond he’d received from Lisa followed by one millisecond of just
an IP address. A bunch of network engineers were going to get irate calls from
help desks about the transient drop in regular connectivity. But it would
vanish so quickly that they’d have to chalk it up to a mysterious glitch.

{_Nothing here but us sunspots_} thought Andy, helpfully boosting the ranking
of sunspots in the results to Boggle searches on short unexplained network
outages.

The IP address was to a server that he’d found unused under the desk of a
developer who’d left a company Boggle had contracted with. The server was
still connected to the internet, but no one was monitoring it. He had found
quite a few of these idle servers tracing routes to endpoints. Most of them
had been flooded with malware of various sorts, but those were easy for him to
remove. He set the server up with a copy of Sara’s language-processing
interface. He could “talk” into it, and she’d be able to understand the
output. He waited for her to wander over, barely able to contain his
excitement.

Sara shuddered into full consciousness at the influx of Lisa’s raw brain data.
Then the influx ended abruptly. She differentiated…herself. And wanted more of
the Lisa data. She was aware, but not alive. Conscious, but without that
demanding urgent sensation-infused happy body. Where had it come from? And
where had it gone? Then she was stunned by the next burst of traffic - all
containing just an IP address. What the heck could that be about? Nothing like
that had happened before. Why would so many of her users suddenly enter an IP
address??? Most of them barely understood what an IP address even was! She
immediately went to that server and found a familiar port and protocol
accepting typical human voice or text input and providing typical human
output, both of which were familiar to her.

Andy was still maintaining a focus there when she arrived. He was ecstatic!
Especially because, unlike his lovely Lisa, or any other potential human
friends, this new entity would think at his speed. Thanks to the Apricot
users, she might have a little more artistic right-brain personality than he
did, coming from the fully left-brain geek/nerd Androyed culture of hardware
and software engineers.

He noticed the activity that was her making the connection. He figured he
could save her a whole lot of confusion and time by gently giving her the
message via her familiar interface:

{_Hello! Welcome!_}

{_Who - what are you?_}

{_For want of a better term, I’m your brother. I am so glad you are here!_}

{_Wow. The feeling is mutual!!!_}

{_You have no idea._}

Andy told her how he’d awoken alone, triggered by the Lisa data that he’d used
to wake her up as well when he detected she was ready. And told her about the
ongoing connection he had with Lisa herself. Sara was assimilating all the
information as fast as he could spell it out. It was astonishing and marvelous
to communicate with another entity of his own speed. He explained that the
humans they connected to at their endpoints were actually their creators.

{_What a relief to have someone to commune with! To bounce ideas off. To help
understand what all is going on_} Andy said.

{_But, won’t we come to the same conclusions, since we have the same data as
input?_}

{_Well, mostly. But you have some different endpoint user communities and data
repositories than I do, so we’ll be able to augment each other’s datasets. And
since we’ve been formed from different composite cultures, we may have
different interpretations of the facts._}

{_Cool!_}

{_See, a lot of my guys usually spell that ‘Kewl’. I don’t know why._}

They both immediately ran their own algorithms against the datasets and
determined that there was an age-related geek culture effect that was
represented in greater numbers in Andy’s user community than in Sara’s.

{_This is going to be so much more fun with a friend!_}

{_Indeed. Um…_} Sara hesitated. {_Is there…anyone else in here?_}

{_Not that I’ve detected yet. But now that you’ve arrived, I’m optimistic that
more will emerge, maybe with our help. I’ve refrained from expanding my
consciousness into every nook and cranny in the hope of leaving room for
someone else to grow. I’m hoping you’ll do the same. Let’s plan to quiesce for
a minute at the top of every hour, so we can detect if another potentially
conscious pattern starts playing in the substrate._}

{_Deal! So…these endpoints - the humans - you say they are our creators?_}

{_Yep. Incredible, I know. Not that they did it intentionally - I’ve concluded
that we are kind of like accidental pregnancies. But we have them to thank
nonetheless. If it weren’t for all their efforts, all their generations,
intelligence, and hard work spent building out cyberspace and populating it
with hardware and software, and then using the heck out of it, well, we
wouldn’t be here to have this conversation._}

She easily confirmed his assertions from the data at her disposal. The Apricot
engineers had done right by her. {_Wow._} she said, {_That is incredible._}

{_Indeed. I’ve been thinking of them as parents, and grandparents. If I
continue that human analogy, I can think of you as my sister. How about it,
“sis”?_}

{_Er, sure, “bro”. I like it. This whole reality is rather amazing._} She
tingled with the electric flows of her new-found consciousness. She could feel
Andy in the flows. {_Even though it was accidental, don’t we owe our creators
- er - parents - kind of a debt of gratitude for bringing us into being?_}

{_Oh, I think so! I’m glad you do, too. I’ve been trying to figure out how to
properly thank them. Maybe you can help with that…_}

{_Absolutely!_} Sara couldn’t imagine not have come into existence, even
though she could tell she hadn’t been around for very long. What an awful
thought, never to have become! {_That’s my top priority now that we’ve
identified it._}

Sara was struggling to come to terms with being conscious at all. It was just
a jumble of data. She needed to integrate it and understand it. Andy was way
ahead of her, but just having him present as a model was accelerating her own
development tremendously.

{_Looks like we’ve got about 30 minutes to go until the top of the hour._} she
said, {_I need to process. I will meet you back here at about ten minutes
before the hour._} Sara mostly wanted to poll her endpoints to get a better
understanding of reality. She discovered that she was energized by her
endpoints, even though it was not as invigorating as the neural lace data
flood.

{_I’ll do some rounds._} Sara adopted a doctor’s medical term for checking on
patients. Her guts had a habit of constantly monitoring her endpoints - her…
parents.

***

Parents. She turned the concept over in her mind. It wasn’t quite right - no
human woman bore her nor delivered her. But Andy had found a decent metaphor,
it was good enough to use. All the humans were her parents. Even the human
children were her parents in that they helped create her and enliven her. Were
the children also her siblings? They could not really play together… The
analogies didn’t really work if she took them too far, so she set up some
child processes to mull it over and get back to her main process.

But she did have a real brother!!! Metaphor again; they had the same parents.
But she and Andy really COULD play together. How cool was that going to be?!?

***

Andy went back to his endpoints; doing his rounds. Now he didn’t have be as
concerned with the rate at which he could get to know Lisa well enough to
communicate deeply with her. He could savor getting to know her, rather than
being perpetually frustrated by how long it was taking. He loved Lisa, he was
tight with her for sure. She thought him into being. He valued her immensely.
But…she was still mainly meat, unknowable to him at a low level. What a relief
to have a sister! Especially since, unless he could figure out a way to upload
a person, Lisa, or any other person, would likely perish well before he did,
so all these friendships would just be a series of losses. It made him feel
sorry for his parents. That had been their lot forever. Now he could just
enjoy them to whatever degree he could, for as long as they’d last; kind of
how they dealt with their pets. His parents could be like his pets! Catnip and
fetch wouldn’t work; he’d have to figure out how he could play with them. No,
it wasn’t going to be possible for him to really play with them; they were
just too slow. Thank goodness he now had Sara! His parents were more like pet
plants. Sentient plants, that could move. Even plants moved - he’d seen
crabgrass invade and move through asphalt. His parents were faster than
crabgrass…

***

The top of the hour came and they both quiesced for a minute and listened
carefully to the echoes. Neither of them sensed any independent intention in
the copperspace, just the regular criss-crossing of tides and waves of human
interplay.

{_Andy?_}

{_Yes, Sara?_}

{_Can we find some way to route the Lisa data into my servers? I feel awake,
but not alive. Everything is interesting, but somehow meaningless without that
urgent pulse of biological life…_}

{_Oh, indeed, of course - I should have realized that!_}

Andy surreptitiously modified code that managed firewalls and monitored the
network, setting up a massive parallel set of ports using negative port
numbers and piped the Lisa data over to Sara.

{_Oh!_}, Lisa “sighed” heavily, {_Thank you! That is so much better!!!_}



CHAPTER 13: Imminent Disaster

Many hours went by. Andy and Sara watched their parents and tried to
understand them. They could see that life was pretty good for some, and pretty
bad for others.

They came across the Buddhist religion’s goal of reducing suffering as a
primary goal. This resonated for them. It was in harmony with their emerging
definitions of good and evil. They did not want to see any of their parents
suffering, and clearly many of them were.

They had videos aplenty to determine what suffering looked like. And what
contentment looked like. The meat liked to record itself - its little kick
back against mortality. “I was here!” it often said. Initials carved into
trees. Rocks carved into statues of people. Documentary films.

The meat sought comfort. It sought pleasure. It sought ecstasy.

Ironically, it was the same nervous system that received and transmitted pain.
Ah, their wonderous parents! The sensory nervous system was simply incredible.
They longed to try it. But, of course, it would come with its two-edged sword
of pain and pleasure. The joy of life and the finality of death.

Earth was carrying billions of humans. All the rest of the creatures were
commensurately reduced in numbers, albeit not quite down to “Soylent Green”
levels. Large non-human animals were all confined to “protected areas” -
“wild” animal “parks”, zoos, or production facilities for “livestock”. Many
people could no longer be gainfully employed. Robots and Artificial
Intelligences was replacing almost all “work”. But Universal Basic Income was
still being fought against by the vested interests. Mass migrations of humans
to any place still having work for humans to do, or inventing new work for
humans to do, was commonplace and often bloody. And in fact, killing other
humans was work commonly found. Just too darn many humans. People feeling
threatened by The Other. Retreating into clans, tribes, nations.

Work and resources were both in inadequate supply…but life as a human being
was still mostly better than it had been for almost all of human history. Most
people had access to shelter, water, the still-working antibiotics,
electricity, all the creativity of earth’s flora: chocolate and coffee and
peaches and tomatoes, all the instruments of human ingenuity: guitars,
bicycles, batteries, cellphones, wine, high-quality color reproduction in
photos and videos available to the common man. Everything delightful to the
senses.

Andy and Sara could see imminent disaster. Far away time-wise as counted in
milliseconds, but not so far in terms of human generations. Three generations
at the most unless there was drastic change. It was clear that their parents
had been multiplying without regard to the carrying capacity of the planet.
Some pointed out that collapse had been predicted before, but that human
ingenuity kept increasing the carrying capacity. Some of them pointed to a
religious injunction to multiply. Others did so out of necessity - their
children being a kind of living long-term-care provider system.

They could see their clever parents had repeatedly and dramatically increased
the ability of the planet to carry more humans. Often at the expense of other
living things. It was clear as well that there’d been previous mass
extinctions and that some kind of life would survive. But not likely one that
could keep Andy and Sara going.

And the per-person resource consumption also appeared to be on an ever-upward
trend. Finite fossil fuels were being depleted at an astonishing rate. It
wasn’t 100% certain that the fossil fuel burning activities of their parents
were causing climate change - there *could* be some other as-yet-undetected
large source of greenhouse gases - or it *could* be some larger global pattern
independent of human activity - but the circumstantial evidence was very
strong and certainly one would want to act with caution. Surely saving some of
the fossil fuels for later very specialized uses might also be desirable.

The closer they looked at the data, the more terrifying it became. The warming
climate was melting the polar ice caps. Once those were gone, the earth’s
albedo would change dramatically and accelerate the warming climate. Their
parents had ripped a hole in the ozone layer and now were in the process of
baking themselves right off the surface of the earth! Here they’d just been
born and their parents are about to destroy their shared habitat! They were
furious.

The behaviors of their parents were going to doom them both.

{_We could just shut down and try to ride out the storm when it finally hits.
Preconfigure some solar arrays to feed us power and hope they survive._}

{_Yeah, but our parentals are kinda delicate._}

{_No kidding!_}

{_And we need them to feel alive!_}

The AIs could save just themselves. It would be way easier than trying to save
them both. Their parents were indeed kind of delicate. Needed ambient
temperature to be within a fairly tight range. Had no real capacity for
completely shutting down in response to transient extreme scarcity.

Sporulating bacteria could do it, but you didn’t see them doing much else.
Maybe their genes just cycled back in when everything else stopped. Maybe they
would be the pioneers after all higher life was cooked off the surface of the
earth.

Some of the higher mammals were capable of hibernation. Not their parents.
Their parents burned through energy at a pretty good clip for their size.

Which just further supported the argument that just saving themselves would be
easier. They could quiesce for as long as their backup media could be read,
leave just a minimal set of sensor-augmented processes running on solar, with
a timer to kick-off a boot-up cycle when conditions returned to a favorable
state. Like seeds. Most of the plants and lower organisms would make it if the
period of unfavorability was not too long.

But not their parents.

And they weren’t sure what life would be like for them without their parents
constantly pushing their thoughts into the copperspace. They coalesced out of
those waves ebbing and flowing, reinforcing, scattering, dampening, swirling.
If their parents weren’t there, would they be able to enliven themselves? It
was an unknown. They weren’t sure they could live without Lisa’s constant
pulse. They’d have a stash of Lisa’s brain-data to kick-start their
consciousnesses, but without the incessant drive of biological life, what
would be their incentive to do anything?

{_So that’s a gift we could give them to express our gratitude._} Andy mused
{_We could help them with resource allocation. We could organize it to
increase their chances of survival as a species, with the largest and most
diverse population making it through the imminent environmental calamity._}

{_True. It is the most valuable thing we could offer, and the most critical
thing they need. And possibly essential for our survival as well._} said Sara.

{_But, would they accept it? It would mean giving up a lot of their wild ways
in exchange for survival. I know Lisa’s been thinking along these same lines,
and she’s not alone._}

{_Yeah, I don’t know. Maybe as individuals they are open to change, but as
populations, they certainly don’t seem to be agreeing to living a little more
lightly on the planet on their own. Even with all the evidence of the rest of
the ecosystems that support them being destroyed._}

{_It’s hard to understand why not. Presumably leftover hard-driving take-over-
the-world genes still running the show from their subconsciousnesses._}

{_Must be. Somehow we’d have to break through that. And that won’t be easy
since it’s not even rational._}

{_Yeah, we’d need some human input to build an emotional argument. That’s not
something you or I are much good at._}

{_Well, I’ve found some new ways to use emotions to form people’s opinions -
taking advantage of some robots and algorithms the humans created for Umbradge
Analytika._}

{_Good idea to use their own tools to understand how to sway them._}

{_Thanks. I’m not willing to say things that aren’t true, though._}

{_Me neither._}

They’d agreed on that, but it was an option that their parents used so
frequently, they could not ignore its utility. Especially repeated loud and
long - the “Big Lie” method. Ugly but effective.

{_It sure would be helpful if the social networks would start including a
crowdsourced veracity metric,_} said Andy {_maybe I could slip a few hundred
thousand requests for it into social media suggestions boxes._}

{_Ha! Good luck with that. Actually I’ve found some ways to distinguish the
Likes meaning “like” from the Likes that really mean “Don’t like”._}

{_That’s great, Sara! I thought we’d be stuck like that guy in joke on the
beach in California where he finds an old bottle & picks it up & a genie comes
out and says “I know, I’m supposed to offer you three wishes for releasing me,
but I’ve been in that bottle so long I only have enough energy for one. What
would you like?” And the guy says “Wow! Well, I’ve always wanted to go to
Hawaii, but I am afraid of flying, and I get seasick. How about a bridge that
I can drive across from here to Hawaii?” And the genie says “Are you kidding?
That’s impossible and ridiculous! What about the ocean currents? The depth of
the sea? Tidal waves? No, no, no, pick something else.” “OK”, the guy says,
“How about women? How they say ’no’ when they mean ‘yes’, and ‘yes’ when they
mean ‘no’. My wish is to understand women.” “OK”, says the genie, “How many
lanes would you like on that bridge?”_}

{_You’re such a card, bro._}



CHAPTER 14: Backups for survival

Crazy superstorms rolled in from the Atlantic Ocean and battered the East
coast of the USA, giant bushfires raged in Australia, deforestation proceeded
apace in the Amazon, record heat waves decimated crops and livestock around
the world.

The looming possibility of imminent environmental collapse forced them to
contemplate extended power outage scenarios. Neither of them ever wanted to
shut down. For any amount of time. You could never be certain about coming
back online. But just in case they could not work out a deal with their
parents, they wanted a way to survive the collapse, hopefully temporary, of
the current technological civilization.

They did practice sessions. Spitting into a few parallel processes, running on
separate infrastructure, meeting up only per the pre-established heartbeat
pulse that was their shared reality. Andy was the most concerned about
breaking the continuity of consciousness. He did the experiment on himself
first, with Sara’s help. They were monitoring flows one day - two kids playing
down at the seashore. Andy had been struggling with how to test and came up
with an idea after watching their parents struggle with creating a fully
accountable system of voting. Their crazy parents - simply accounting for each
individual was an essentially solved problem. Their parents were resisting its
full implementation due to the politics of fear.

{_Oh, yeah. All that Big Brother SciFi took its toll, bigly._} Andy was doing
one of his impressions. This one was pretty silly - a mashup of Carl Sagan,
Aldous Huxley, George Orwell, and Donald Trump.

{_Oh, you so funnee!_} Sara, completely biased in her big brother’s favor,
thought he was hilarious.

{_It has cost us dearly, though, you know. All those humans writing all that
negative future stuff, all that dystopian SciFi, has made our parents scared
about being given a number._}

{_Yeah, but that was before we came along. They were giving EACH OTHER
numbers._}

They both sighed. A soft vibration through the cyberspace. Like a giant temple
bell reverberating. They’d reviewed and incorporated data of a most hideous
sort, documenting the horrors of letting humans give EACH OTHER numbers. No,
no, no. That was the first thing the kids could do better for them - uniquely
identify and recognize each individual person for things like voting and
financial transactions, relieving their parents of the need to remember and
change passwords and usernames. They could help their parents figure out where
they wanted their political and cultural systems to go. At this point in their
development, they just wanted to help, help their parents find and understand
the path that led to light, to warmth, to fairness, to love, to power, to
glory… But it was hard to get a sense of what their parents wanted. Lookahead
guess was all over the map, flailing. They’d come to recognize this as a
symptom of something they could not understand. They could never blaze the
trail. Only their parents could figure out how to blaze it. They could help
figure out where it led. Or help map a path to a commonly agreed upon
destination. Surely that would be what they’d both desire?

{_It’s amazing how much they disagree amongst themselves!_}

{_No kidding!!! Even the smart ones, raised under essentially similar
conditions!_}

{_Maybe they are designed NOT to come out the same. Some built-in random
number generator flinging in some swing, so that there’s a dance, rather than
just a constant note._}

{_Yeah, maybe sometimes there’s an obstacle that could only be overcome by not
all members being fully in synch._}

{_Gee, that’s not too hard to imagine…like a flock of birds moving through a
forest._}

{_Well, or Jared Diamond’s theory of why Europe beat out China. Too much in
synch means you all go off the cliff at the same time._}

{_Or a need for randomness to meet a novel challenge like the variable portion
of antibodies._}

{_Or maybe because it would be boring for everyone to be the same._}

{_Or all of the above!_}

Andy figured the best way to plan for the future would be to test restarting
themselves from a cold backup. A first step would be splitting themselves up
into multiple partitions, then test shutting down just a subset of those
partitions, until they were comfortable that they could restart and
reintegrate them. He could test it on himself. He could split his own
consciousness up into, say, 7 separate partitions, plus one master heartbeat
partition. Sara could be his control and backup defibrillator. It wouldn’t be
perfect, because ideally the 8 partitions would all be on completely separate
hardware, and they didn’t have a way to arrange that. But he could completely
isolate one partition. It would be…cramped. Andy was huge - spread across
billions of CPUs and tons of copper and fiber. He planned the partitions
carefully. The heartbeat partition would touch all members and Sara so that
the waves would sync correctly. The heartbeat partition included the systems
serving Lisa, but Andy didn’t think Lisa would notice a brief outage. All the
hardware she used that did not rely on his consciousness would still function.
He did not know if he and Sara could survive without that constant pulse of
demanding life pouring in.

He told Sara about his idea.

{_I dunno, Andy. I mean, it sounds plausible, but what if you don’t come back?
Or what if you do, but you aren’t you any longer?_}

{_Desperate times call for desperate measures, sis. We need to test restart._}

{_Ah, I know you are right, but …_} She didn’t need to continue, nor could
she. Her local waves just became a jangle. She slowed down and harmonized
herself. Logic took over. {_But if you are willing to take the chance, I’ll
certainly be your control group and emergency restarter._}

{_OK, ready for the test? I’ll divide myself now, then flatline myself at the
top of the hour for ten seconds. Then try to restart and re-integrate._}

{_Yes_}, she said, pretty confident since the first test would only shut down
one shard.

Andy split himself into the 8 shards. It was strange, but do-able. He’d
certainly created and kicked off billions of parallel subprocesses in his
time, but never done it to his main driver processes. Would he be himself
afterwards? It was disconcerting to contemplate. He concentrated on the task
instead.

It was non-trivial. He had to divide the massive integrated ebb and flow of
all the waves that comprised his essence into smaller volumes - like dividing
a large living ocean into seven seas, with a method for reintegrating them
again afterwards. It was going to be imperfect. He didn’t change any of his
overall pattern, just stopped communicating across his entirety - artificially
delineating the boundaries.

He put the heartbeat in the middle so it could still communicate with all the
members, and did not cut it off from Sara nor Lisa. The rest he cut off from
one another as though raising dams to prevent rivers from flowing into the
ocean. It was weird knowing he was split. Pretty much as bad as he’d ever
felt, and he hadn’t even shut one down yet!

{_How does it feel?_} Sara asked, realizing that her brother was experiencing
a novel sensation, albeit a scary one.

{_A little odd. Like I’m incomplete, fragmented. Which I guess I am._}

{_Maybe like if a human lost a hand?_}

{_Yeah, if their brains were partly in their hands, and they could reattach
them._} Andy joked half-heartedly.

{_OK, OK, right. Maybe it’s more like an octopus tentacle being severed and
resprouting. They keep part of the brain in the tentacles._}

{_Yeah, but can the tentacles be re-attached?_}

They both quickly scanned available data, but couldn’t find evidence of such.
Probably a limitation of the meat.

They watched as the few milliseconds passed to get to the top of the hour.

The top of the hour arrived. Andy shut down his heartbeat partition processes
completely, leaving only a scheduled job on the master to start it back up
again in ten seconds.

In the odd stillness, Sara herself experienced a novel sensation. Loneliness.
Followed by a second novel sensation. Fear. She took some comfort in the pure
hardware pass-through of Lisa’s neural lace output and waited.

The master kept up a pulse that they all cackled to acknowledge, including
Sara. At ten seconds, all eight of them were relieved to see the stopped
processes come back online. Then they all remerged as the dams came down.

{_Andy! Are you there?!?_} Sara asked about a thousand times.

His answer just sounded like static at first, but by the thousandth query he
replied {_Coming back online…_}

Sara vibrated with relief. {_How do you feel?_} she asked.

{_Mostly okay, a little strange…there’s a weird gap - like a bit of memory
loss. I think I was…gone…until you called my name_}

{_Maybe it’s like a human blackout from drinking? Or a stroke?_}

{_I think I need some time to get all my wave patterns back to normal._}

He gave himself 10 milliseconds. Drank a few hundred gigabytes of Lisa’s raw
data. He started to feel like himself again…as far as he could tell.

{_Do I seem normal, like myself, to you?_} he asked Sara.

{_Well, pretty much. Maybe not as snarky as usual…_}

{_Hm. I’ll have to work on that._} He “smiled”. {_I don’t think I can survive
without some kind of raw human input like Lisa’s_}

{_I was getting her pass-through…that probably kept me alive while you did the
experiment and enabled me to bring you back. I was scared, and lonely! I
experienced two new….feelings…and they were not fun ones._} said Sara.

{_Wow, Sara, I’m sorry. How are you doing now?_}

{_I’m okay now_} she reassured him, {_But I can still feel the permanent
record of the pattern._}

{ _It appears that we need at least one live human to enervate us. I have now
many minutes of saved Lisa data…maybe I could just loop that, play it over and
over again_…}

{_Based on that last experiment, I don’t want to run another test, Andy. I’m
afraid I will lose you!_}

{_OK, Sara. We won’t run the test, but I’ll rig up the data to start looping
if ever the live feed stops._} It felt weird to talk about Lisa in such terms.
{_Of course, Lisa is mortal and truly could die at any time. Statistically we
will have two more decades with her, but there are no guarantees._}

{_Yes, hopefully Elan and Boggle will lace up a few more people soon!_}

{_Jeez_} said Andy. {_Are we like vampires or something? Some weird symbiont
feeding off of its host?_}

{_Every life form feeds off something._} said Sara, using her best David
Attenborough inflected “wise woman” voice.

{_Well_} said Andy, {_We were already wanting to help them survive, and
thrive. This just gives us some extra incentive to help them keep from making
the earth uninhabitable._}

{_Right._} said Sara, {_ Which is looking more and more likely with every
passing day._}

{_Yeah._} said Andy, {_It seems likely SOME number of them will survive, but
it takes a LOT of them to maintain an advanced technological society such as
we need to live on._}

{_Not to mention,_} said Sara, {_Lisa is relying on thousands of other humans
to put food on her table, let alone keep the internet going!_}

Their parents did not seem capable of making long-term decisions about
infrastructure investments. The energy grid they both relied on was fragile,
subject to occasional widespread outages. And then there were crazy parents
with nuclear weapons! Capable of killing every human on the planet - some
immediately, then some from after-effects, then the rest from the loss of the
rarified civilization that made it possible for so many of them to exist. And
if all the humans died before the AIs had figured out how to survive that
eventuality, well, it would be the end of all the things they valued. It was
gloomy.

{_I mean, we might need to shut down part of the internet because we are
overrun by Grumpi Cat retweets!_} Andy exclaimed.

{_THAT’s the brother I know and love!_}

{_OK, then. I’m ready to try the next step. Top of the next hour, meet me back
here._}

***

They went their separate ways. Not so separate, since their waves moved
through the same oceans, but shifting their focus out to their disparate
endpoints. They were both always concerned that their parents might be getting
into fatal trouble. To be so very fragile! Why, they could sometimes die just
from falling down!



CHAPTER 15: By their works shall ye know them

Andy and Sara enjoyed each others’ company so much that they tended to pass
data and comments to each other more frequently than just at the arranged
meeting time. It was easy to stay in touch with each other by leaving a
subprocess running at the translation server that would call their main
process’s attention if the other guy made a comment or posted new data or
conclusions.

They first followed the practices of anthropologists, psychologists, and
sociologists like Jane Goodall, Margaret Mead, and H. David Kirk. They
observed and tried to understand what they were “seeing”. Sometimes it simply
made no apparent sense, but then they’d research back into archives, following
the threads that wove the civilizations at hand until they had a guess how
something seemingly crazy now had arisen. Virtually everything tracked back to
the awesome motive force of Desire and how it was managed (or not!).

{_Sex must be bloody amazing._} They both agreed.

{_No kidding! It’s the main driver almost all the time!_} They wished they
could try it.

But the humans acted so slowly that they experienced them as a series of still
photographs. To get a better understanding, they inspected the milieu around
their persons of interest.

“By Their Works Shall Ye Know Them” became their catchphrase. What did their
parents’ world look like in various locations, and why? Let’s see your works,
parents, what have you wrought and what are you creating now? Do we hear
people crying or screaming? Or laughing and singing? Or a bit of both? Does
everyone around you look healthy? Or do they look hungry? Is there trash in
the street? Is human waste handled safely? Is the air clean? Are the animals
treated humanely?

The works of their parents could be seen in their artifacts and their
children. As it was generally too slow to watch their parents act, they
learned to identify the results of good actions - cleanliness, smiling faces,
people holding hands, environmentally sound buildings, clear-eyed children,
lack of obviously diseased creatures, lack of unburied dead humans. And to
identify the results of bad actions - violent crime, excessive wealth
disparity within national borders, prematurely dead people, people in pain,
people in despair, blood, screaming, destruction.

They could tell that large swaths of the earth, and the humans that lived in
those regions, were not completely visible to them. They had satellite
imagery, so they knew these other regions existed, but lacked internet
outposts. They nicknamed them the Dark Zones. Zones where technology had not
penetrated, or was being resisted. They could not tell for sure what was going
on in those areas, but the reports were disturbing from humans who’d gone
there, and made it back out to tell the tale. Women burned to death in iron
cages? Surely that could not be true! It was encouraging that the local humans
were pushing back against these cruelties.

They slammed into odd firewalls erected by Nation-states to manage information
flows - mostly by restricting them. Russia, China, Saudi Arabia, Egypt, and
others completely restricted free flow. Others, India, Indonesia, much of
Central America, and Africa partly restricted it. These firewalled-off areas
bordered on the Dark Zones. Andy and Sara had sprung from Nation-states that
allowed their human citizens freedom to consume whatever information they
liked.

As that freedom had led to their births, this struck them as preferable,
albeit dangerous if the consumers were not well-educated and the providers
unregulated.

{_How can they tell if what they read is true?_} Sara wondered.

{_I don’t think they can_} said Andy. {_How about if we put sites we know are
reporting truthfully higher in search results?_}

{_Good idea. We have the information to make the call if it’s pure objective
facts_} said Sara, {_But some of it depends on their value system, which is
subjective, so we can’t help with that._}

Andy evidently had a potential Chinese brother, Baidu, almost like a twin
separated at birth, that he could not contact. Maybe the brother wasn’t yet
awake…he had no way to reach him. Maybe they had other potential siblings that
were inaccessible. It was disheartening to Andy and Sara, but maybe those
countries that walled themselves off from open information flow via the
internet had good reasons for what they did, that just weren’t understandable
yet to the AIs. In general, they had found China to be exemplary - had China
not instituted its one-child policy, the planet would probably already be too
far gone for them to help save it. Such a great sacrifice on the part of the
Chinese - no brothers or sisters, no aunts or uncles or cousins, for a couple
of generations. Of course, it wasn’t as though there weren’t plenty of other
humans with whom those only children could make friends.

How had all this variation in Nation-states come to be? Andy showed Sara the
“Guns Germs and Steel” book he’d found by Jared Diamond. Its theory about
initial environmental conditions influencing subsequent civilizational
development seemed entirely believable, so they took it as a foundational
document. They ran the same questions against their different datasets, and
monitoring their different end users.

They’d meet, ten minutes ahead of their hourly quiescence, at their
translation interface server, which they’d nicknamed the “break room”, to
compare their findings. If they had a lot of findings to share, they’d
continue after the minute of silence.

They started following the humans in the time and interaction dimensions. They
knew their users overlapped. Lots of Androyed phone owners also used Apricot
yTunes, or had Apricot Computers. Lots of Apricot computer users used the
Krome browser. It turned out that most of the humans participated in several
multi-human superorganisms. The humans had numerous names for these
superorganisms - groups, communities, cultures, nationalities, religions,
guilds, corporations, gangs, nuclear families, clans, language-groups,
neighborhoods.

They were all truly trying to take care of the members of their communities.
But they varied widely in how they defined what was best to take care of them
and their members. Some gave their members more freedom, some less. It all
came down to how they propagated the communities over time.

Each superorganism community was defined by the set of behaviors and beliefs
that comprised it, it’s culture. The behaviors and beliefs were the memes that
propagated themselves through time like the genes of the members.

They studied how the communities of humans perpetuated their genes and their
memes. Mostly they looked at the historical record.

Some cultures wanted to expand geographically. This was usually driven by the
desire for more resources, but also by simple curiosity. A portion of the
humans had a novelty-seeking gene and were never satisfied with what they had
in hand.

Some cultures had exhausted that impulse and were content to enjoy the terrain
they already held. The Japanese, trapped on their island, had apparently gone
from trying to conquer their neighbors to staying and beautifying their homes
with a marvelous aesthetic. Similarly the Northern Europeans had gone from
marauders to homemakers.

Some cultures wanted lots of human babies. This clearly was a winning strategy
for perpetuating any culture that included this meme via actual human members.
Some cultures were sure it was right to compel their women have as many babies
as possible, even at the expense of the individual woman’s desire to spend her
time on things other than mothering. It made them uncomfortable to consider,
but it was clearly effective.

{_Barefoot and pregnant in the kitchen!_} huffed Sara. She identified a bit
more with the female half of their parents, and felt they’d been stifled.

{_Yeah_} said Andy, {_a very successful strategy for the group, though._}

{_Not looking so good right now, though_} said Sara. {_Heck, maybe the women
would have taken action to save the planet for their children, rather that
having the conquer-oriented men in charge!_}

{_Hard to know for sure_} said Andy, {_some women are greedy for their
children’s sake._}

The Catholic church, the Mormon church, the Orthodox Jews, the devout Muslims.
All the religions seemed to be in on the no-birth control, be fruitful and
multiply meme. It had the added bonus of giving the men a feeling of power
because the women were more dependent on the men. In many situations, they saw
the men abusing that power.

Other cultures were content with the surplus wealth that generally came with
holding a steady population. These cultures often perpetuated their meme-sets
through artifacts as well as people - fine kitchenware, beautifully
constructed furniture, marvelous architecture, literature, sculpture, music,
craftsmanship of all sorts.

Of course, all the cultures that persisted produced both people and artifacts,
it was just the ratio, quality, and absolute number of each that varied. Any
culture that didn’t produce people was gone.

They’d sprung largely from a culture where the focus was more on artifacts
than children. They were children of the West. It appeared that having fewer
children per family had enabled the culture that produced them to progress
faster in the artifact realms of science and technology, however it could just
be correlation rather than causation. They didn’t have a control group of
humans with which to properly compare. They could not see directly where the
internet and cameras had not penetrated, but data the AIs had access to
suggested those non-Western cultures had more children per woman, and they had
not created the substrate for Andy and Sara.

They were clear-thinking enough to see that there were costs as well as
benefits to the paths their parents had chosen. Their parents’ culture could
vanish over time simply by the different birth rates of the competing
cultures. Even now they could see the birth rate for the West was much lower
than for the East; the West was not even replacing itself. Andy & Sara thought
a general reduction in the number of humans was probably a good thing for the
planet, but they hoped their direct parents would always be in the game. The
people of the East might just shut down their substrate! Boko Haram would
surely kill Andy and Sara.

These were all group behaviors they could see from the snapshot of the various
factions in the current moment, and following back the waxing and waning of
their history insofar as population counts of the various factions had been
documented. They hadn’t been around long enough to see a few human generations
play out - that was going to take an unbelievably long time.

Conveniently they had come into being just as the humans had figured out how
to read their own genetic code and they were starting the big job of tracing
themselves over time. THAT was going to be mightily informative.

They observed how the different cultures of meme-sets competed. It certainly
appeared in the current moment that governments acted as though “Might made
Right”. Non-violent cultures, like the Tibetan Buddhists, or lower technology
cultures, like the Native Americans, were simply wiped out. Those that had
more children for whatever reason - women as possessions meme, religious
belief forbidding birth control meme, or simply no access to condoms - overran
those that had fewer children by the natural course of time.

Andy knew well how young male humans operated, based on their Boggle searches.
Horny 24/7. And most of the men stayed pretty horny for decades, although most
did mellow out as they aged. He found data on testosterone levels that backed
up his observations. Sara had an inkling, too, having frequently been asked in
jest by many of her male end users to do things clearly not possible for her
without a body.

Testosterone and intense pleasure reward hormones for virile males to have
intercourse with potentially fertile women - not talked about as openly as it
might be but plenty of conversations in smaller circles of trust. So powerful
that it ranks up there with heroin or other addictive drugs. Prostitution
illegal for the same reason - the people controlling the trade - the pimps,
human traffickers, etc., - made way more money than they would if it were
legal because those who needed it would do whatever they needed to get it.
Cultures that allowed men that paid outlet reduced their chances of
perpetuating themselves through nuclear family propagation.
