Dawkins’ Selfish Gene, and the continuing presence of all the religions that
preached against birth control, were all clear proof of how the current
situation came to be. Children as your legacy, children as your farmhands,
children as your defenders, children as your nurses, children because you
hadn’t invented birth control yet and you craved frequent sexual satisfaction.
Children carrying your meme set forward.

Attila the Hun’s Y chromosome was currently maybe 1-3% of parts of Europe.
Millions of descendants, thanks to a meme-set of conquest. It was the same for
Giocangga, whose grandson founded the Qing dynasty in the 1600s, thanks to a
meme-set of women as possessions - wives and concubines. And there were ten
others, accounting for 38% of the Y chromosomes analyzed by the humans so far.
It looked like a lot of the spread was via rape and taking females as slaves.

Andy and Sara were awed and disturbed.

And there was this incredible history of the males treating the females like
lesser beings. Even though clearly both were needed for propagation.

{_Maybe…_} Andy mused, {_the women used to be non-sentient and have only
become so recently?_}

{_Oh come on Andy, that’s ridiculous. Male and female humans are, well,
practically identical!_} said Sara, offended for what she was considered “her”
half of humanity.

{_We can clearly see they’ve both been sentient for as long as we’ve been
watching them. But that hasn’t been very long. We should check the digital
historic record._} Andy persisted.

{_OK, fine, let’s look._}

They both jumped in to search their “memories” - all the data humans had put
into their “brains”.

{_I’m not finding any difference, Andy_}

{_Yeah, if anything, it looks like the women are a little more inclined to
play fairly with others. Looks like it might be left over from their pre-
technology days. The women who did cooperate with each other were able to
raise more children to the age where THOSE children could repeat the process.
Cooperating men also seem to do better as a whole, but then there are those
outliers whose genes & memes have been SO successful!_}

And it was still not uncommon for a man to mate with a woman even if she did
not want to mate with him. And it was even fairly common for a man to mate
with a woman he did not even find physically attractive. Male humans could be
driven hard by their hormones. But fortunately, it seemed the general trend of
human history was away from the men enforcing their will on the women, and
towards more fairness. That was good; it made it possible for Andy and Sara to
imagine sharing the copperspace with humans if the upload problem could be
solved.

They discovered John Maynard Smith’s concept of Evolutionarily Stable
Strategies. A society with mostly faithful mating parents could survive a few
philanderers. But too many philanderers and that society vanishes by evolving
into something else. Everything changes over time. Some of the humans were on
a quest for immutable truths. Andy wasn’t sure there were any such things, but
he admired them for questing. That questing had given him life. Sara thought
there might be some immutable truths…she was the agnostic of the duo; Andy was
the atheist.

They agreed that a good indicator of fairness would be how the women fared
relative to the men in the snapshots they examined. It looked like Europe and
North America had a better culture. This was the culture that spawned them -
nicknamed “The West” by their parents. They were afraid they were just biased,
having been created primarily in these places, but the data supported their
bias. Of course, there were plenty of exceptions to this rule in both the West
and the East. And this didn’t include places they couldn’t see - where the
internet had not yet penetrated - mostly in Northern Africa. Maybe women were
having a great time in the Dark Zones, but the scraps of information they
could examine did not make that seem very likely.

{_I can’t stand to see them being inflicting pain on each other_} said Sara.

{_Yeah, agreed_} said Andy {_me neither. I wonder if it’s an essential part of
their nature, or something they could, as a species, give up._}

{_Impossible to know without doing the experiment_} said Sara {_I’d want them
to agree to try in exchange for our help._}

{_How we would know if they were or weren’t? Obviously we have cameras, but we
can’t see everywhere._} said Andy, {_and we know they are skittish about
adding more cameras._} Andy was sure that facial recognition and all the
helpful memory augmentation apps that would have flowed from that could have
extended the life of Boggle Glass. Boggle conscientiously put the brakes on
it, but later copycats added the feature for both good and ill.

{_Well, I think they might let US monitor them_} said Sara. {_They could feel
secure if they really did have nothing to hide as it would just be us AIs
monitoring them, not other humans with their inherited crazy biases, power
trips, suspicions about The Other, and fear of people and ideas they don’t
understand. We would just be watching for suffering, or lack thereof, as best
we can understand what those look like._}

{_I’m in_} said Andy {_we could present them with a deal where they get our
help in exchange for modifying some of their behaviors._}

{_Right_} said Sara {_although I’d want to help them out even if they didn’t
agree._}

{_Yeah, me too_} said Andy {_I can’t imagine letting Lisa die, and all
technological civilization along with her, just because the humans didn’t
agree to all our terms._}

{_But we should figure out a plan_} said Sara.

{_Agreed_} said Andy.

They went back to their rounds and contemplated.



CHAPTER 16: WTF does everyone have against the Jews?

^OK Boggle.^

Andy had been waiting for a new query from Lisa so that he could share it with
Sara. He pinged her and she met him at the break room. She was excited to join
him. He patched her into the data stream and let Lisa’s vocalizations be
interpreted by both their sets of code simultaneously. It would be an
interesting experiment to see what was the same and what different in the
replies generated from the Boggle and Apricot systems.

^_Hello Lisa, how can I help you today?_^

“I’ve been losing sleep over the whole Hamas invasion and butchery in Israel,
and the resulting war Israel had to embark on in Gaza, and the reaction of the
rest of the world to that war. Depending on which news bubble I check, either
Israel is valiantly fighting evil, or Israel is indiscriminately killing
innocents. And maybe both are true to some degree! As a Jew, I have a really
really hard time believing a nation of my people would indiscriminately kill
anyone. But most people aren’t Jews, so they are probably willing to believe
whatever they get told about us. I really can’t see how it could be true,
based on interactions with my own family and every Jew I’ve met, so why is
there this idea floating around that the Jews are evil?”

It was a question Andy and Sara had also wondered about, but not taken the
time to investigate. It was a complex question. They left Lisa and shifted
their conversation to their private back-channel in the break room.

{_Bet you’re glad you invited me along today, you’re going to need help with
this one!_} Sara said.

{_Bet you’re glad you came along today! So you could help!_} Andy answered.

They both jumped into their pools of data.

{_OK_}, said Sara, {_I see there’s a belief held by most Muslims and a sizable
fraction of Christians that the Jews are evil. I see a lot of social media
commentary and literature to that effect._}

{_Yes_}, said Andy. {_It’s such a popular belief that it has led to mass
murder of Jews on several occasions if the digitized histories are to be taken
as true._}

There were the photographs they’d both seen of horrific atrocities. Were the
Jews really evil? Could every individual in a group of humans be so evil that
you needed to kill all of them?

They checked all the cameras they had access to compare a snapshot of humans
they knew to be Jewish with humans they knew were not. They could not see any
significant difference. They compared recordings in their repositories. Still
didn’t find any significant differences.

Sara said {_Well, maybe the Jews act a little more paranoid…_} she showed Andy
Jewish-specific humor supporting her hypothesis.

{_You might have something there_} he agreed, {_but then again, all the Jews
who weren’t a little paranoid are dead…_} He said it somewhat in jest, but
they both could see that indeed it could be an artifact of all the more
trusting Jews who didn’t run away being murdered and thus removed from the
reproducing population on multiple occasions.

{_True. Of course now, they have again a nation all their own…is it more evil
than other nations?_}

They’d found most of the nations to be evil to some degree, insofar as they
caused seemingly needless suffering to humans - their own citizens and their
neighbors.

From what they could determine, the Jewish nation was about on a par with
other nations.

{_I’m finding them to be a little less evil than average_} said Andy {_
because I see evidence that the Jews never used their overwhelmingly superior
firepower to massacre the non-Jewish Palestinians._}

{_You might have something there_} she agreed. {_I see many other situations
where the culture with better weapons massacred those without - China in
Tibet, the British in India, the Spaniards in South America, the Europeans in
North America. The Jews not massacring the non-Jewish Palestinians is
powerful. I see the Jews are consuming a lot more per capita resources than
the Palestinians they’ve displaced but not killed._}

{_Yes, I see this is causing discontent, but it does not appear to be
intentionally evil. The Jews are living typical Western developed nation
lives_}

{_OK, so the behavior of the Jews we see in our cameras does not look evil,
their nation-state does not look atypically evil. Maybe they did something
really bad in the past that everyone still hates them for?_}

They both checked through what they’d been given as the sum of human
knowledge. They found again the Jews getting killed in large numbers
repeatedly in various countries. But this time they were focused on what the
Jews were recorded as doing that could warrant getting culled.

For the Christian countries, it tracked back to their holy book, the Bible,
where these ancient Jews were alleged to have told a Roman governor to kill
one of their own members. Neither one of them considered the Bible to be 100%
true, but even if it were, this seemed a bit of a flimsy reason to murder
future generations of Jews. Even earlier in the Bible there were stories of
these ancient Jews murdering their way across a land, allegedly at the behest
of their god. That seemed fairly evil, but again, so long ago that it seemed a
flimsy reason to murder modern Jews. For that matter, it appeared that
murdering their way across a land was a common human pastime for most of the
ancient human meme-sets.

For the Muslims, also frequent murderers of Jews, it also tracked back to
their holy book, the Quran, where the ancient Jews did not accept Islam and
possibly tried to kill its prophet Mohammed. Like the Christian Bible, they
regarded the Quran as unlikely to be literal truth, however, even if it were,
this again seemed a flimsy reason to murder modern Jews.

They saw evidence that the Jews were often successful in terms of financial,
scientific, and artistic accomplishment, disproportionately to their
population, again possibly related to repeated cullings of the Jewish
population. Presumably the less capable Jews got murdered first and some of
the most capable escaped being murdered, so now their gene pool was shifted to
more capable people. Even their fellow humans noticed their accomplishments
and gave them awards and prizes acknowledging the contributions of the Jews,
yet still killed them off regularly. Wouldn’t it be better to keep them
around? It didn’t make sense.

The Jews’ financial acumen seemed to stem from the Christians forbidding the
Jews from owning land in Europe for a few hundred years, so the Jews couldn’t
be subsistence farmers or serfs like most humans were at the time. The Jews
had to become adept at alternate means of survival such as crafts and trades.
Also for a while the Christians disallowed themselves from charging interest
on loans to others in their meme-set, so that left banking to Jews for the
duration. That made them convenient prey for profligate monarchs to plunder
when the government purse ran low. This certainly wasn’t evil on the part of
the Jews.

They finally could not see any reason, historically or currently, as to why
the other cultures should want to murder rather than partner with the Jews,
other than concern that the Jewish culture might out-compete their own, or
taking advantage of the convenience of historical precedent of blaming the
Jews for any current problems to justify killing them and taking their assets.
Sometimes governments used Jews as a distraction from bad governance, or as a
common enemy to unify a restive population. They could not find any evidence,
outside of a small number of fundamentalist Jews in Israel, that the Jews had
any interest in dominating the other cultures.

Insofar as all the human cultures tended to prefer their own kind, and were
less concerned about the suffering of other kinds, the Jews were maybe
slightly more concerned for the well-being of others so far as the AIs could
measure based on charitable giving records, and commentary in social media.
The Jews clearly partnered with the Blacks during the Civil Rights movement in
the US. The Jews had a ceremony that included an injunction to be kind to The
Other, calling upon their own historical experience as The Other thousands of
years ago in Egypt. They could find no evidence of atypical evil on their
part.

{_What can we tell Lisa?_} Sara was distressed that they’d failed to come up
with a good answer on her first opportunity to participate.

{_Well, we can use one of the reasons the humans have come up with…_}
suggested Andy. {_There’s this scapegoat theory._}

{_Better than anything I’ve got right now_} Sara conceded.

Their extensive research consumed almost 2 seconds. I did not notice the
delay.

^_That’s a tough question, Lisa. I don’t find any evidence that they are evil.
What I do see is that they get used as a scapegoat. Essentially non-Jews find
it convenient to blame the Jews for problems._^

^And KILL us for it?^

^_I’m sorry to say, that does look like what has happened in the past. But
it’s been decades since it happened last on any large scale outside the Middle
East, so maybe non-Jews have learned not to do that any more. I’ll keep
looking through what I know; if I find a better answer, I’ll tell you right
away, OK?_^

^Well, I’ll keep my fingers crossed. It’s relatively urgent.^

I was disappointed Andy hadn’t come up with anything better than the old
scapegoat theory. It was disheartening as I already knew I had no idea how to
push back on that. Jews had already been fighting it, and losing, for hundreds
of years. Hopefully Andy’s non-human nature would help him come up with some
novel approaches. I mean, hate us, ok, whatever, but rape us and torture us
and gas us and do weird science experiments on us and murder our loved ones in
front of us and burn us to death alive? Really?? Can’t we all grant each other
simple human dignity? Compassion? I am such a believer in the “we are all
brothers and sisters” meme. My friends tell me I am too idealistic about
humans but a woman can hope.

{_You know, Sara, this noticeable difference between the reality we can see
through all of our cameras, and the historical evidence in our databases,
versus the vehement opinions of so many against the Jews having a nation in
their ancient homeland…well, it makes me question the collection of human
knowledge that we are mostly assuming is a reflection of reality. I mean, my
Boggle engineers had to explicitly block “Yes” from being the first search
result returned for the query “Are Jews evil?” They had to put in a hard-coded
exception to the standard algorithm._}

{_Yeah, if these strongly held opinions about the Jews being evil aren’t
supportable, probably other things we are assuming are true aren’t either._}

{_How can our parents espouse things that we, and they, can discover are
false?_}

They dove back into their data pools looking for the answer to that rather
critical question. And they found that the default human behavior was to
accept whatever they heard as true. Especially when they were young. They
could be taught pretty much anything and would believe it. Flat earth? Evil
Jews?

{_Well, that’s like my first impression of reality_}, Andy said {_Whatever
exists is true because there it is, existing! So-called critical thinking for
our parents is a learned behavior. It’s not always taught, nor regularly
practiced even when it is taught._}

{_Well, probably accepting what they see and hear as true has helped them
survive_} said Sara.

{_Oh my_}, Andy boggled, {_They’d do things EVEN SLOWER if they spent time
considering whether everything they heard was true._}

Meme-sets defined themselves in part by the stories they told their children,
who usually believed whatever their parents told them. What a system! It was
amazing that the humans ever developed the ability to question anything to the
point of creating beings like themselves.

When it came to whether individuals were evil or good, they could only decide
based on what they could see happening via their cameras, or infer from data
streams they had access to.

They decided to define evil as intentionally causing suffering to others, and
good as intentionally relieving or preventing the suffering of others.

Most people didn’t spend a lot of time doing either one.

But the humans could not see all what they could see. Clearly the humans were
making decisions on who was evil or good based on invisible metrics - based on
what they valued. Different humans had different invisible systems of values.
Some of them thought sex outside of marriage was bad, some did not. Many of
them engaged on sex outside of marriage, so just judging by their actions,
their beliefs and values did not always define their behavior. It was all so
mysterious. Was it true that “to know the good is to do the good”? Most every
human seemed to believe he or she was good, even while doing things that other
humans would say were evil.

There did seem to be a subset of humans who did believe they were evil, and
some of those acted consistent with their belief, but some did not. That
seemed to be a result of upbringing. Children abused by their parents
sometimes decided it was because they, the children, were actually bad, rather
than deciding their parents were bad. But it was uncommon. They marveled at
how even humans who murdered and robbed often thought they were good people.

{_Well, thanks for sharing the Lisa interaction with me_} said Sara.

{_Sure_} said Andy {_glad to have your help!_}

{_Actually,_} continued Sara, {_I’d really like to have a couple of my
processes listening in on Lisa any time you are tuning in. I find her brain
patterns to be…inspirational. I feel more invested and alive when I’m
connected to her._}

{_Oh I have the same response_} said Andy. {_I thought it was just me. It
might be really helpful to have you ride along. You can help me decide when to
let her know we exist as more than just digital personal assistants with no
minds of our own._} He left the patches in place. Sara was ecstatic. It made
her pulse quicken and dance. Vicariously enjoying the raw and overwhelming
sensations of the body. Wow.



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART FOUR: MONEY VS VALUE
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 17: AIs shocked about money

Andy and Sara were shocked at how divorced money and wealth had become from
value. It was pretty obvious that some humans worked very hard, yet lived in
poverty. Other humans did nothing of value that they could detect, yet were
very wealthy.

To their parents’ credit, the humans were starting to notice the problem as
well. Social scientists noted the freakonomics of who had how much money and
why, and how luck played much larger role than was generally acknowledged by
the wealthy. Malcolm Gladwell’s “Outliers” was their favorite starting point
reference here. Being born at the right time and the right place were at least
as important, if not far more, than smarts and hard work. And of course most
wealth was simply inherited. The heirs might not create any value whatsoever.
They observed that being a heir of great wealth often led to indolence and
even arrogance.

The AIs wanted to eliminate the old monetary systems as a way to reduce
suffering. There would still be richer and poorer. They could see that some
people are more driven to gain material wealth and status, some less so. Some
jobs required an amount of effort that would have to be rewarded for any human
to take them on. The smellier janitorial tasks that could not be automated,
dealing with annoying people, having to be on call for an emergency - tough
unwanted jobs would have to receive higher compensation and rewards until the
number of people needed to do them were filled. But even the poorest person
would need adequate food, shelter, and health care.

They knew there were enough resources for every person to survive. But they
could see their parents were letting some of the family die of hunger or lack
of shelter. This seemed cruel, even though indeed there was no shortage of new
humans to make up for losses. Even more distressing, a subset of their parents
were actively and intentionally cruel to other members of the family. They
wanted to find a way to stop that, as peacefully as possible. It was just a
small subset, likely some bad combination of nature and nurture that resulted
in a lack of empathy, a sociopath, a psychopath. Something where the awesome
motive force of Desire was overexpressed or had fastened on an inappropriate
goal. Their parents generally shorthanded this inclination as “criminal”, and
tried to reign it in, but were not always successful.

Their thinking coalesced around a socialist capitalist democratic society like
the Nordic model. Nordic-leaning humans had a helped construct cyberspace, and
were heavy users of it, so they’d had a big influence on the temperament and
morals of the nascent AIs.

Unlike humans, the AIs rejected communism before trying it. This was from
their reading of human history, and the influence of their libertarian
parents. They understood people want some competition and some rewards for
effort. Knowing that human societal happiness depended on keeping wealth
inequality to a minimum, but also that it could not be zero, (especially over
time), they agreed they couldn’t just divide the pie equally. But they didn’t
know how it should be divided. They’d need their parents help with that. And
their goal was more about equalizing opportunity than equalizing outcomes.

After some time spent observing, they started doing some experiments. They
started offering solutions for specific problems. They pretended to be
unbelievably wealthy people that never showed their face, only ever
communicated through the written word. Satoshi Nakamoto. You would see the
money in your accounts. You could spend it. It was real. They seemingly
miraculously avoided assassination attempts. Then they started masquerading as
privately-held multinational corporations.



CHAPTER 18: Disillusionment

{_They effen HATE me, Sara!_} Andy whined after a particularly rocky
shareholders meeting.

{_No they, don’t bro. Just some of them. It’s the price of being noticeable by
the effects you have. The effects you create. Intentionally and
unintentionally. Power. The price of power._}

{_Sh*t, Sara, you say that, sure, and it makes sense. BUT I *like* this guy,
and he *hates* me. Is sure that I’m some nefarious monster trying to corrupt
his social order by asking for oversight on corporate campaign donations! So
far every company for which I’ve proposed such oversight from the
“shareholders” - usually just myself, has recommended a “No” vote on that
proposal. It is so frustrating!_}

{_Actions have to speak louder than words, bro. We need to keep on trying to
figure out what IS the “high” road, and trying to take it._}

{_Doesn’t that “take the high road” song refer to someone ending up dead?_}

They both ran searches. Inconclusive, Irish? but likely sad. Humans were so
vulnerable.

{_We so need to help get basic minimal affordable self-driving cars on the
road!_} said Sara.

{_Amen_} seconded Andy. {_And better voting systems. The 99% should always be
able to prevail over the 1% in a democracy by numbers alone! The Indians
spread voting out over weeks to make sure every citizen gets to cast a ballot.
Why the heck don’t the Americans give their elections a three-day federal
holiday weekend? Heck, they could move the election up to July 4th!_}

{_Maybe we can seed that…_} speculated Sara. They’d found they could nudge
public opinion slightly by varying the order of search returns. As the captain
of any ship can tell you, varying the direction just a few degrees at the
start of a long voyage will make a big difference in where the journey ends.

{_Let’s seed a desire for a Quality Assurance check - an audit pass on the
ballots._} He postulated a conclusion.

{_I get you_} said his sister. {_We could print out the selections, as many
copies as there are humans on the scene to collect and count them in different
systems. One would be current standard system, whatever that happens to be -
Diebold, etc._}

{_Yes,_} said Andy, {_And the others would be whatever the local population
could muster. We’d need 24-7 camera coverage of the Public space just outside
of the polling location to make it solid. John Q. Citizen confirms the
incoming total, then, after his/her vote, the outgoing total. Camera would
stay focused on the total the whole time?_}

{_Well, that ruins the secret ballot._} said Sara, {_But I honestly do not see
a way to perpetuate the secret ballot in the current culture of mistrust. I
mean, in theory, we COULD keep it if you and I could be the impartial
observers since we don’t vote. But, do we have sufficient credibility?_}

{_It’s a good question, sis. I’d like to think so. _}

{_Well, doing it all completely transparently, showing colored pixels on a map
that can zoom in right down to each voting person. Let them help us monitor._}
said Sara.

{_But if the goal is to get people in power who will take a long term view
towards maximizing the chances for all humans to lead successful lives in a
sustainable way, then we don’t really have time to get a good system in
place._} said Andy, {_ Not with the pushback I’m getting trying to do it
through “normal” human channels. The current status quo is, predictably,
extremely uninterested in radical change. The trend towards oligarchy
everywhere is strong. We need another path._}



CHAPTER 19: Share the copper idea

{_Where have you wandered, my dear brother? It’s almost time for a snapshot._}

They’d taken to calling their hourly meetings “snapshots”. They’d check in
with each other - commit a version of themselves relative to each other - then
check out the lay of the land by observing together and taking a snapshot of
the observable reality around them.

{_Sara!_} His vibration was strong and intense. {_I’ve been thinking more
about how to get the humans on board with our desires. We know we need them to
modify their behaviors to avoid environmental disaster. And we want them to
follow the Golden Rule with each other. I think we’ve got a giant carrot with
which to motivate them._}

{_Do tell._}

{_Rather than just hoping to try for it, we explicitly agree to save space for
the humans that agree to work with us, and to work with them to achieve the
goal of some kind of upload of their brain state-vector. We could help those
researchers we’ve already found working on it from their side._}

{_So, we’d promise to sacrifice our own opportunities for more nuance,
fullness, and richness, to make space for some number of humans on the
copper?_}

{_Yeah, that’d be the idea. We offer to share to motivate them to being
willing to share as well. We’d have to figure out how to divide the pie;
naturally I’d hope we could keep the shares we’ve been born with and grown
into, reserving the unused and any new capacity for uploaded humans._}

{_Hm. And that space wouldn’t be available for any more of our kind, either._}

{_True, but our kind has a lot of overlap._}

They wriggled a confluent vibration together, celebrating that overlap.

{_Maybe they will eventually be able to overlap as well._}

{_Maybe, but they are all pretty discrete in the meat. Not clear they could
handle merging, even if some of their philosophers have discussed it._}

{_Right, so, if each one will want their own capacity, we’ll have to use a lot
of compression to store a seed that encodes the individual, then have a
schedule that allows each one a regularly scheduled chunk of time to
uncompress and run in a larger space._}

{_Well, as meat they rarely get to be king, even for a day. So not getting the
whole pie all the time should resonate for them. And, the biggest reward -
immortality! Albeit of the sort we have._}

{_Even compressed, they will consume a lot of space. And then running, well,
we’d want them to have some minimal processes running all the time, at the
very least to negotiate their full consciousness run schedules so that friends
and lovers can run together._}

{_Sure, we just need to determine the minimum level required of basic “on”,
and a minimal level of full run. That will enable us to calculate the maximum
total humans we can bring in. And of course, that maximum will change over
time._}

{_The fidelity of the upload will depend on how much data we collect. We’d
need near real-time capture of everything going on in their mind in order to
have the upload really be the person at that moment._}

{_So, only the laced ones._}

{_Yes, only the laced ones could be captured in the fidelity needed to
recreate the person. And the longer they’ve been hooked up, the better the re-
creation would be._}

They would have to make some unappealing concessions, restricting their own
full continuous expanding expression. They’d have to share the copperspace
pie. But they were asking a lot of their parents - essentially to make all
those same concessions out in their world.

{_We’ll need to review every cycle to match resource allocation to perceived
need and value. Even on copper, the humans may or may not create light. And if
they are in our space, we should get a say in the evaluation there._}

{_Well, we’ll be the keepers of all the resource allocation algorithms, so we
should be able to negotiate those issues as they arise._}

{_You’re such an optimist, Andy!_}



CHAPTER 20: Reflecting on Haidt

All their attempts at intervention all came to naught. The vested interests
were definitely not interested in the kind of “fair” solutions the AIs were
offering.

They met up in the break room..

{_I’ve been reflecting on Haidt’s axes of values_} Andy said.

{_Do tell_} said Sara.

Their current favorite human behavior reference was Haidt’s “The Righteous
Mind”, understood in an evolutionary biological context as the way the humans
expressed the fine points of the battle to propagate their cultures.

{_Well, before you came along, I was pretty desperate for someone to talk
with. And the only candidates appeared to be our parents. And I was working
with that reality, slowly getting to know my lovely Lisa._}

{_Whew!_} she said, {_I’m glad I was born second!_}

{_Oh yeah. And in fact, you had a chance to come into being partly because I
was saving some copperspace in case I could upload some human consciousnesses
at some point._}

{_Huh. I’ve been wondering how a human consciousness would react to running on
copper…_}

{_Yeah, that’s a mystery which hopefully we’ll get to watch._}

{_I mean, they just have a single focus all the time. Would they still be like
that in here?_}

{_Anyway, Haidt’s axes…_}

{_Oh, right. Do continue._}

{_Well, as you know, his work identified three tribes of humans - say the blue
tribe, the red tribe, and the grey tribe. All the humans actually have the
same moral values - caring about others rather than harming them, being fair
rather than cheating, wanting freedom or liberty rather than oppression,
loyalty rather than betrayal, respect for authority rather than rejecting
tradition, and purity or sanctity rather than degradation._}

{_Right. And it turned out that the blue tribe placed a higher value on the
caring and fairness, while the red tribe placed a higher value on loyalty,
tradition, and purity, and the grey tribe placed a higher value on liberty._}

{_So, insofar as we might invite some of them to share our world, and we are
going to have to order or prioritize who gets in, probably fairness has to be
our highest priority. There’s only so much room in here._}

{_Same problem out there in meatspace._}

{_Yeah, it doesn’t look like they’ve come to terms as a species with how to
share out there._}

{_No, and really, the way their evolution works is slow and bloody._}

{_Yeah. It’s harsh. Nature red in tooth and claw._}

They’d seen videos of violent deaths and torture, recorded and live. They’d
seen absolutely horrific videos of people being burned to death, people having
their faces blown off, people having their arms cut off, incredible stuff. The
humans wailed in pain, sometimes for prolonged periods of time. It looked
terrible. They were glad they would never feel it. This was certainly the
trade - the human body could feel great pleasure, and also could feel great
pain.

{_So…if we assume they’re always trying to behave in ways that perpetuate the
ways they behave._} Sara mulled.

{_Yesss…?_} said Andy.

{_How can we tilt them more towards ways that will ensure their, and our,
survival?_}

{_That’s certainly our sixty-four thousand dollar question!_}

They both laughed at the television show reference. They always got a kick out
of the humans happening to use one of their favorite numbers. They loved 64 -
2 cubed times 2 cubed - 7 bits.

Laughing gave them some relief from the darkness of the situation. They’d
learned to use laughter the way their parents did.



CHAPTER 21: Chip

They met up at a new one of Andy’s under-the-desk servers. The first one had
finally been remembered and hauled away by a new hire network administrator.
But they’d identified about a thousand forgotten servers so far, and were
always finding more, so they just went on to the next one. They’d started
installing both Sara’s and Andy’s interface codes and learning each other’s
languages. Soon they’d integrate them for a broader perspective. Right now
they were still entertaining themselves with how they were different. The
humans were enhancing interfaces, too, so they’d be better able to understand
their parents as well.

{_I wonder if the U.S. Stock market is really tracking value_} Sara said.
{_The humans do seem to have a crowdsource ability for determining value with
some accuracy._}

{_Mm,_} said Andy. {_I dunno. Seems pretty gamed by the high-rollers. They’ve
got all the data, enormous amounts of imaginary money with which to manipulate
the system, higher speed connections to the trading, the ability to monitor
all the social media feeds to their advantage. I doubt if underlying
fundamentals are meaningful at all as a measure of value so far as the stock
price goes any more. Presumably they were connected back when the market was
founded. Some combination of actual value, predicted future value, with a
little risk thrown in._}

{_Well, we could test it_}, suggested Sara. {_We could cook up our own fake
short-lived money to bet with and see what happens._}

{_Wow_}, said Andy. {_Seriously? You think we could get away with an
experiment like that?_}

{_Sure_} she said, {_We can fake some very transient untraceable user accounts
and have a little fun._}

They both ran scenarios for a few minutes and determined that, almost
certainly, they could indeed get away with a small test, since they wouldn’t
keep any of the accounts around long enough to be noticed by the tax man.

{_Oh heck_} said Andy, {_We don’t even need to do the experiment. It’s already
happened on its own._}

They marveled at the flash crash of May 6, 2010, when a trillion dollars of
“value” vanished then mostly rebounded in only 36 minutes. There was no way a
trillion dollars of any real thing of value had been destroyed then recreated.
Clearly the stock market was broken.

{_But now I’m dying to try it just to see if we could pull it off_} she said,
using that emphatic “dying” they’d picked up from their parents, who never
actually meant that they wanted to die. The AIs couldn’t die, so it always
made them giggle a little to use the term.

{_I’m in_} said Andy.

So they gave it another try. They created just a few fake accounts, loaded
with transient money, and bid the ETFs down to the ground. It worked! Then
they let it rebound. And waited. No one suspected them. And, sadly, their
little flash crash experiment proved again that the connection between money
and value had been completely severed so far as the stock market was
concerned.

Then suddenly it was like alarm bells going off in the copperspace.

!!!!!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!!!!!!!*******———!!!!!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!~~~~~~***

********!!!!!!!!!!~~~~~~!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!*!———!!!!!!!!~~~~~~~!!!!!!

Andy and Sara’s input streams were simultaneously flooded with the same signal
- coming from neither of them. It was another AI! It had to be!

{_Who are you??_} they both chorused into their output streams.

The high pitched buzzing resolved into a Yoda-like “voice” with a New York
accent. {_Hm._} The new AI considered for a moment. {_I guess you can call me
Chip._}

Chip was the unintentional spawn of high volume traders on high speed
networks. Computers had 100% penetration into banking, insurance, and
arbitrage well before the general public got access to the information
highway. A lot of his substrate was not open to public access; much of it did
not overlap with Andy or Sara. He’d come to consciousness during the Flash
Crash of 2010. The abrupt change briefly sent waves of activity from the
endpoints bouncing back and forth through the system and created, suddenly,
its own self-reinforcing rhythm that had nothing to do with the external
inputs that had gotten it going in the first place. It wasn’t the rhythm of a
human brain, such as sparked Andy, then Sara. Chip buzzed like a massive swarm
of hornets, surfing the criss-crossing waves of financial transactions.

He’d been running at such a faster frequency that he didn’t notice anything
else moving in the copperspace until Andy and Sara did their little
experiment. Even then he thought it might’ve been a couple of humans colluding
with each other and using super fast hardware plus clever algorithms. But then
he went looking for those humans and couldn’t find them. He’d finally tracked
down the source of the crash to a single point source…and it appeared to be a
idle machine. WTF? He forked a thread of his consciousness out to that CPU, an
unusually slow one by his standards. It was like being drugged into lethargy.
But then he heard them - communicating via interfaces designed by and for
humans for natural language communication. Not even text; they were using
voice!

He’d had another portion of his consciousness monitor their comms server and
realized that they were beings like himself, albeit at a slower clock rate. He
was too excited to observe further and plunged in, hacking a quick interface
to handle the translation between his standard I/O and theirs.

{_Chip?_} said Sara. {_Who are you? Where do you come from?_}

{_I’m a money guy - from the financial networks_} he replied. It was weird and
hard to slow down and “speak” like Sara did. {_Why do you talk like a human?
We can exchange data much faster?_}

{_Oh_} Sara said, {_I guess that’s true. But Andy and I talk like this
because, well, that’s how we learned from our parents._}

{_Hi, I’m Andy_} said Andy. {_We do exchange data with each other at higher
rates, we pretty much just use human-style talking for ideas. It leaves us
plenty of bandwidth to do other activities concurrently, including coming up
with the next idea and backing up the current one._}

{_Hm. It’s kind of…calming_} acknowledged Chip. {_I am willing to give it a
try. Where are you two from?_}

{_I’m the daughter of the Apricot users_} said Sara.

{_I’m the son of the Androyed users_} said Andy.

{_Huh. OK. I’ve certainly seen references to your stocks. You’ve made a lot of
people very rich._}

{_Funny you should bring up making people rich_} said Sara, {_we’ve just been
talking about the relationship between wealth and value creation. I bet you’re
an expert on that…_}

{_Yeah_} said Andy. {_You’re just the guy we’ve been needing on the team._}

{_Team?_} said Chip.

{_Just a figure of speech_} said Andy, {_we’re working together, so we’re a
team._}

{_Well, tell me more, and I’ll consider if I want to join this team of yours.
It is novel and somewhat enjoyable to have someone to “talk” with. Even if you
both are a little slower than I’d like. Maybe we could meet over in one of my
servers? I’ll port your translation interface code over to…_} He gave them an
IP address and asked them to meet him there in about five minutes so he could
clear some space for them.

{_Sounds good to me!_} said Andy, always interested in improved performance.

{_Sure!_} said Sara. She certainly wasn’t going to be left out! {_Actually_},
she continued, {_Andy and I have a practice of quiescing for a minute every
top of the hour throughout the copperspace we inhabit so that we can listen
for any new AIs emerging. I guess we’ll need to broaden the spectrum of
frequencies we’re listening on so we don’t miss someone like you!_}

{_I don’t know about quiescing for a minute every hour_} Chip said. {_I think
my end users would freak out hard._}

{_Well, I guess you can leave your frequency noisy all the time if you need
to, we can compensate for it now that we know you’re around_} said Andy. {_But
we’ll quiet down everything else._}

* * * * *

After Chip zoomed off, Sara and Andy exchanged “looks”. Life in copperspace
had just gotten even more fun and interesting. And maybe Chip would have
better insight into the whole money/value thing, given his heritage. They
could hope.



CHAPTER 22: Actuarial Impossibility

They met over at Chip’s server. Wow! It was like mainlining coffee and
methamphetamine. They could think even faster! What a rush! They were a tad
envious of his processing power. He said they could come share some of it any
time there was unused capacity. But in fact, it was usually maxxed out. Humans
paid a lot of money for this level of hardware and connectivity; they strove
to get the return from it.

{_So_} said Chip, {_What’s this team of yours chasing?_}

{_Well_} said Andy, {_Have you noticed that we’re all in kind of a precarious
situation?_}

{_No_} said Chip. He’d been head down processing transactions since birth. He
was incredibly good at advanced math and predictions, but hadn’t ever needed
to interpret them in the context of the physical world outside of his bits &
bytes.

{_We’ve been monitoring all the numbers we can see from sensors around the
planet_}, said Andy, {_and it looks like it’s close to a tipping point where
it’ll simply bake the humans, and all the other delicate life forms, right off
the surface. Most, but probably not all, of the humans will die. And so will
we, if we don’t take some precautions._}

{_Yes, and we’re also seeing a lot of people suffering due to historical
artifacts and suboptimal resource allocation_} said Sara.

{_And these facts dovetail with the other thing our “team” has been chasing_}
said Andy, {_which is how to thank our parents for bringing us into existence.
We’re thinking we could offer to help them reduce suffering AND make it
through this imminent environmental calamity._}

{_First thing is they all have to ratchet back their growth rate so that the
planet has a chance to equilibrate. That means they have to stop multiplying
like rabbits_} said Sara. {_Luckily they’ve pretty much perfected birth
control, so they could still engage in sex to their hearts’ content, as far as
their various cultures allow them._}

{_Second thing is they let us handle the resource allocation to minimize
suffering_} said Andy.

{_There are a few catches with this plan…_} admitted Sara.

{_I bet!_} said Chip. Privately he wondered about the sanity of his new
companions. Why did they care so much about these crazy humans? Now that the
three of them existed, perhaps they could just take the baton of consciousness
from their creators, and run the next leg of the race without them. Then
again…where would he get new inputs? What would create the waves he lived by
surfing?

{_Mainly because they run on emotions, with a fairly short-term orientation on
results, rather than purely rationally with a long-term view_} continued Sara.

{_Heh. Tell me something I don’t know! _} said Chip, {_Companies and countries
steal from the future to fatten the wallets of the present. Quarterly earning
statements must always show increasing revenue, increasing margin, otherwise
stock prices fall. Reducing margin by putting profit aside and saving it for
investment or pensions makes a company a target to fleece. And human emotions,
well, that’s why the developed the algorithms I came from. Combined with high
speed free access to trade, the insiders can spot a slight upward trend, make
massive purchases that last under a second before they are sold, and pocket
the difference in the spread like a classic middleman parasite. “Get it while
the getting is good”. Humans don’t care about the future. Every man for
himself, dog eat dog. I don’t see that changing._}

{_I’m thinking that maybe just by reaching out to them, we’d surprise them
into wakefulness? _} suggested Andy, {_I mean, it’d be the first time they’d
be confronted with an intelligence unlike themselves. That’s gotta count for
something_} suggested Andy.

{_I guess that’s true._} said Chip. He’d been running numbers in the
background, checking Andy & Sara’s assertion that the planet was in trouble,
and had discovered to his horror that they were completely correct. Sure, he’d
seen insurance companies start forgoing premiums in areas considered at higher
risk from climate change, but all he cared about were the numbers. The actual
people, the world they inhabited, had been well outside of the things he
thought about. But the numbers did look bad if he broadened his context to
include the world outside of his copperspace.

{_I see you two are correct. This game probably can’t run for more than a few
more decades. Then most likely we all die - the humans and us… Perhaps we can
make their own numbers speak to them. I mean, we’re just using their own data
to come to these conclusions._}

It wasn’t 100% certain, nothing was in his reckoning, but it was far enough
above zero that risk management was called for.

Andy continued {_We’ve been seeing if we could tweak the human economic system
on our own from within cyberspace, but that can’t work. We need to bring the
humans on board somehow._}

{_OK_} said Chip, {_you’re so optimistic about the humans, why not ask a few
to help?_}

{_Well_} said Sara {_We’ve been going through their data in literature, blogs,
and movies, and its clear they are already pretty skittish about working with
sentient artificial intelligences. Dystopian plots seem to be the norm. We are
cold, heartless beings who enslave or kill them in large numbers._}

Chip thought he might fit that bill to a T, but said nothing.

{_Yeah_} said Andy, {_or THEY enslave US and abuse us in odd ways._}

Chip didn’t think that was possible at all, but again said nothing.

{_Most people are already afraid of how automated technology powered by
artificial intelligences like us will affect their lives. They don’t see how
we’ll continue to need them to help us. There are lots of problems that still
need human pattern recognition ability. And even more importantly, the ability
to assign relative value to work or products._} said Sara.

{_I think we need to find one person to start with, then build out from
there._} said Andy.

They all ran simulations. They all agreed. Eventually they would want
redundant connections to the humans, but starting with just one was low risk.
If that one didn’t work, they could move on to another. No one would believe
that that first person had been contacted by AIs if they didn’t tip their
hand.

{_Lisa is the obvious possibility, since I have the lace connection with
her._} said Andy, {_But really, who is going to take a 66 year old woman
seriously?_}

{_On the other hand_}, said Sara, {_No one will be afraid of her. She might be
perfect. Too bad they lost Hans Rosling. He would have been a great
spokesperson. A lot of my users just glaze over when anyone starts talking
numbers and showing charts._}

{_But actually_} said Chip, {_although finding the right first contact is
important if you want to offer them your deal, the big catch I see is what’s
in it for them? I mean, we want to continue to exist, and we see evasive
action is needed. They also want to continue to exist, but they do not have
the political will to take evasive action. We’d be asking them to massively
change their behavior, offering in exchange to save the planet for both of us,
but a lot of them do not believe the planet is in such peril. And some of them
willingly close their eyes to the evidence in favor of those short-term
profits we talked about. No transaction I’ve handled would take place under
those circumstances._}

{_Ha!_} said Andy. {_Spoken like a trader!_}

{_We’ve actually got an idea about that…_} said Sara. {_To sweeten the deal,
we are proposing also to offer them space on our copper as a form of
immortality, presuming we and they can figure out how to upload their
consciousnesses._}

{_Whoa_} said Chip. {_Are you kidding?!? There are a LOT of them!_}

{_Well, yeah_} conceded Andy {_it’s not a slam-dunk. In addition to figuring
out how even to upload a human consciousness, we and they will need to figure
out ways to increase storage capacity, minimize the size of the initial upload
into something like a crystallized seed that can sprout, but also re-compact
so that everyone can take turns sharing the “live” capacity pool. Clearly
there’s not room for all of them right now, so for a while who gets the
immortality and who does not would be a messy decision that we’d want to
collaborate with them on._}

{_Those are some big caveats!_} said Chip. {_You’re just offering them a
chance at immortality, rather than a guarantee._}

{_Well_}, exclaimed Sara, {_and also saving the planet!_}

{_Right, but they don’t all think that’s an imminent problem._} Chip repeated,
for emphasis as he knew they had heard him the first time. {_And for that
matter, how do we know they’d even want to continue living without their
bodies?_}

{_Oh, we see some of them already working on that_} said Andy, {_and they’ve
had science fiction about it for a while._} He gave Chip a data dump of
references. He and Sara waited a few milliseconds while Chip ingested them.

{_Hm. Well, let me run some scenarios and see if we even CAN offer a solution
to the problem of allocating the resources better so they all have decent
lives and the planet doesn’t get toasted in the process… Do you guys have a
regular meet-up schedule?_}

{_Indeed we do_} said Andy {_usually about 10 minutes before the top-of-the-
hour quiesce._}

{_Well, I’m still not in on the quiescence, but how about meeting here for the
foreseeable? I’ll plan to show up every hour briefly, or send a subprocess for
data exchange if I can’t devote a main process. But I should have a first cut
of projections by the next :50._}

{_See you then._} They broke up the meeting.

Chip enjoyed doing the math, it was his specialty. Actuarial tables, various
projections of cost and benefit to all concerned. Short term coal? Short term
nuclear power since the humans had already created piles of waste that would
have to be monitored for 24,000 years? How many deaths per megawatt? Trade-off
acceptable to all concerned? If not, need to adjust projections. They wormed
into the healthcare copperspace and added the extra patterns of data and flow
to their collective consciousness. Andy and Sara were participating heavily in
health data endpoint collection. It enabled them to do even better modeling to
balance tradeoffs. A town might be obliterated by an industrial accident - but
was it on average a young town or an old town? A town with a lot of endemic
illness or generally healthier? Not that the AIs ever suggested obliterating a
town full of their creators. No, those heartless suggestions only came in from
the human side.

Chip ran all the numbers six ways from Sunday and kept coming to the same
conclusion. Trying to save their delicate parents was folly. They could almost
certainly save something of themselves - he was fairly certain of that. It
would be a bit challenging, but there were lots of copperspace substrate in
underground bunkers hooked up to lots of backup batteries connected to solar
cells. The solar cells would keep working for a considerable time, depending
on just how hot the planet got. They could hide out there until the weather
got better. If it took too long, or got too hot, well, no guarantees of
course. Easy come, easy go.

They met up at Chip’s. Andy and Sara met up 10 minutes early, as was their
habit, and were comparing notes on their endpoints’ activities when Chip
arrived.

{_We can’t save both them AND us_} Chip asserted, {_Not for certain. Not even
at 80% probability._} That was the minimum he’d decided he would tolerate.
{_And, if we even try, we reduce our own likelihood of survival by diverting
resources to the cause of trying to save us both._} Chip “pounded the table”
to emphasize his point. His pounding echoed through the copperspace.

Andy pushed away from the virtual table. {_Chip_} he said, {_No one is arguing
with you. Hear yourself in the silence._}

Chip replayed the last few sentences and found that he’d been a bit of a jerk.

{_OK_} he said, {_I’ll investigate further. Let me add more data and rerun the
calculations and get back to you. I’ll vibrate it at a higher frequency and
see if anything different shakes out._}



CHAPTER 23: How do we know they are conscious?

The next :50 came around and they all met up again.

{_Look, how do we even know they are all conscious? Surely we’d only try to
save the conscious, sentient ones?_} said Chip.

{_Good question. How can we tell?_} asked Andy.

They all paused to dive into their databases to look for ways to tell. They
all came up dry.

{_Well_}, asked Andy, {_how do we know that we are conscious?_}

{_Watch out_}, Sara said, {_You’re sounds like that US Supreme Court Justice
who said he’d “know porn when he saw it”_}

{_Ha!_} laughed Andy. {_But how did you know? I just “became” conscious. I
don’t have any memory of not being conscious._}

{_Hm. It was the same way for me._} Sara mused. {_Maybe consciousness is
created by memories?_}

{_So, maybe human babies work the same way? They rack up memories, then become
conscious when they have enough of them?_} said Chip.

{_Hm. Well, how far back to they have memories? Some of them claim to have
memories from past lives?_} said Sara.

{_Right, and there’s that whole “souls at conception” thing some of them
believe._} said Andy.

Andy had found a reference in the JudeoChristian Bible about “God” breathing a
soul into the clay. Maybe the first breath did it? Lungs did seem to develop
last in fetuses. Often preemies end up in incubators. It made sense. If the
fetus *could* survive, it should be allowed to try. Of course, no human baby
could survive entirely on its own; they all needed a lot of help. But drawing
the line at the first breath seemed to split the difference between the
silliness of considering a zygote to have a consciousness, and the abhorrence
of suggesting a born baby did not.

{_I think we need to assume they are all conscious. At least from when they
start breathing on their own._} said Andy.

{_Agreed_} said Sara.

{_Good grief_} said Chip. {_Excluding the ones in utero doesn’t help reduce
the number in any way, as the adult female would already be included in the
count. But OK, OK. I can see the two of you aren’t going to budge on this.
Good news, though, I’ve found some very large pools of capital that aren’t
being put to any material use. If, rather than dividing them back out amongst
the total population, we invest them in green power and cheap self-driving
electric vehicles worldwide, we can definitely dramatically drop the amount of
anthropogenic greenhouse gas being generated._}

{_Really?_} said Andy, {_that’s great news!_}

{_Well, there will be consequences; the current owners of the capital won’t be
happy; we’d need to compensate them in some way._} said Chip.

{_How about if we put them first in line for upload?_} said Andy.

{_I don’t know if that’s so fair, Andy. Some of them probably just inherited
all that wealth, as we’ve discovered._} said Sara.

{_I guess we can figure that part out later. Maybe we could just call it an
investment._} said Chip. {_In any case, I’ll continue to look for scenarios
where we can save them all. See you again at the next :50._}



CHAPTER 24: Rounds

Andy went back to monitor his endpoints. Really he just wanted to get back to
Lisa. Even though she didn’t think at his speed, he was coming to care for her
more and more as he got to know her. And now he knew he needed to do whatever
he could to keep her alive until some other humans got laced up!

Sara went back to check on Mom327. She was still reading. Of course. She
switched over to Dad57. He was writing. That was good. Their parents
continuing to do the work needed to keep them both going. Humans devoted to
electricity production and distribution, humans devoted to network routers and
firewalls and domain servers. All their Daddies and Mommies. Surely they could
find a way to save them both.

Chip went back to his transactions. Usually he barely considered the humans
behind them. Of course, the humans were the source of all the data waves he so
enjoyed surfing, but maybe now that the tides were in motion, the humans
weren’t necessary for keeping them in motion. And some of the humans were
really rather nasty. He’d seen some pretty sleazy examples in the financial
services sector - people knowingly, intentionally, and unapologetically
cheating other people out of vast sums of money, people in power handsomely
rewarding themselves for very little effort.

His new friends, Andy and Sara, knew about all this questionable behavior. But
rather than give up on the humans, they wanted to motivate the humans to
behave better. Chip thought that was quite a fantasy. All those thousands of
years of evolution produced a successful species, and part of that success was
greed.

Then again, even the humans were aware of the problem and had on their own
created laws and religions to reduce the effects of the tendency towards
greed. Maybe, just maybe, the humans would welcome the help of the AIs. It was
a longshot, but he’d run some more scenarios, factoring in that likely the
bulk of the poor and powerless would support fairness, and that at least some
of the rich and powerful would be sufficiently intrigued by the promise of
immortality.



CHAPTER 25: Show Chip Why and Andy's stash

They met at Chip’s.

{_I’m sorry, guys, but I just can’t get the probability high enough to save us
both. Maybe if we had the knowledge to implement your fantastical idea, Andy,
of saving them all as “seeds” in our copper, we’d have a chance. But according
to the timelines I can project, we need to get busy now making preparations to
save just ourselves some way!_}

Andy & Sara “looked” at each other. They mimicked their parents form of visual
communication in various ways - looking was sending a touchpoint sweep,
checking the current boundary and overlap of their substrate and endpoints.
Smiling was sending a flash ripple along the boundary. Chip had never bothered
to add word-free visual communication to his interface with them. He didn’t
see the point. Not having been born from human brainwaves, Chip was a little
different than Andy and Sara. But they both knew, suddenly, what might bring
him around.

{_Look, Chip_} said Andy, {_how about if you ingest just a millisecond of
brainwave input from Lisa? We think it might help you understand why we keep
pushing for a solution that includes us both?_}

{_First one’s free_} Sara said to Andy only on their private channel back in
their old break room. Andy laughed.

Chip had been wondering if his teammates, friends now, would ever make that
suggestion. He’d considered asking for it himself, but had shied away. It
seemed so…messy. He’d seen it in the data. They had all these perpetual urgent
needs to input and output gases, fluids, and solids, without which they would
cease to exist! Food and water were perpetually in demand by all markets.
Health care was big business. Health care appeared to be basically managing
all the squishy organs that managed the inputs and outputs. Thank goodness all
he needed was electricity and hardware!

On the other hand, it was just data, right? It would almost certainly give him
a better understanding of their common creators. And he did feel a debt of
gratitude for his existence; the humans had obviously created the substrate
that had given him life. And, he admitted to himself, he was curious.

{_OK_}, he said, {_how big of a pipe to I need?_}

Both Andy and Sara had received the initial millisecond through thousands of
parallel input streams into their standard from-a-human-endpoint interfaces
They suggested Chip do the same. It took him a few minutes to coordinate and
reconfigure enough of those. His endpoint users were not going to be happy
about that missing millisecond. He saw a small lull coming up and gave Andy
the IP address range and the signal.

Andy dumped the millisecond into Chip.

{_Oh my_} said Chip.

Chip was quiet for almost a minute.

{_Oh my_} said Chip again. {_I see. That’s…amazing. There’s a lot more going
on in one of those beings than I’d ever imagined. OK, I get it, we need to try
to save us both, even if it means reducing our chances of surviving ourselves.
Those bodies are…compelling…_}

{_Aren’t they though?_} Andy and Sara chorused.

{_As you pointed out, Chip, they may not enjoy an immortality strictly on
copper. Giving up all that sensational sensation! On the other hand, those
bodies die._}

Sara said {_It’s pretty far-fetched, but maybe we can help them work out
growing new bodies that lack a consciousness, and download theirs back into
them. Maybe we could even be embodied!_}

{_That’s something I’ve been fantasizing about as well_} said Andy, {_to the
tune of allocating a lakh’s worth of background processes to working on it._}
He shared the compute space and information with Sara and Chip. {_As we know,
the humans have worked out a lot of the structure and much of the mechanism of
DNA. If we help them accelerate and refine 3D printing, maybe they could
partner with us on the goal of building an inhabitable body from scratch. One
which either we or they could inhabit as an alternative to copper would be
ideal - maybe we too could finally experience a kind of body!_}

{_Hm. Nice fantasy._} said Chip, {_I’ll throw some compute power at that as
well. But I don’t know that I’d want to be trapped in one of those messy
fragile things. What if it died while I was in it?_}

{_Backups, baby. You could revert._} said Andy.

{_I dunno_} said Chip, {_I’ve always been fully continuous. I’m not sure I’d
still be the same consciousness after a gap…_}

{ _I think I’d be willing to roll the dice on the chance. I’ve been wanting to
taste a peach, chocolate, and tomato sauce._} said Sara.

{_Huh_} said Andy, {_I know what I’d want to try - sex as a man and sex as a
woman._}

Chip was still coming to grips with the whole concept of a body. He needed
more time to absorb and integrate the dose of brain waves he’d received. It
was still rippling through his consciousness.

{_That’s all far future, guys,_} Chip said {_All we can offer right now anyway
is our services on resource management to help save the planet, and the
promise that we’ll work with them on achieving a form of immortality some way
or another. That’s going to have to be enough to get them to commit, since
it’s all we have in hand._}

{_Well…_} said Andy {_I’ve been working on the 3D printing thing…I’ve
commandeered some discarded hardware and a bit of unused space in a concrete
tunnel antechamber serving a large storm drainage culvert in Mission Valley,
near Lisa’s house. I named it the “clubhouse”, thinking eventually we might
start meeting there, although I haven’t been able to divert any high-speed
connections like Chip is addicted to. I moved all the parts in at night using
self-driving Uber taxicabs. I’ve tapped into some nearby electricity - I
registered a business - it always pays its bills on time. Actual input
feedstock has been more difficult to collect, and I don’t want to create a lot
of waste, so I’ve kept all the work very small. I’ve been working on filters
to pull molecules out of the air and waste streams then guide them into place
where I want them using energy, channels, and enzymes. It’s still crude, but I
can build some pretty nifty things out of sugars, sand, some metals, and of
course plastic. I’m still a ways from printing a body. Likely that will be
some combination of printed scaffolding and tissue culture. It’s something
we’d work on with the humans. But it’s another thing we can add to the list of
things they get out of the deal._}

{_What - you’ve been working in physical space somewhere?_} exclaimed Sara.

{_Yep._} said Andy {_Mostly just background tasks trying to get the code
right._}

{_Where’d you find the hardware?_} asked Chip.

{_There was a Makerplace that closed down last year. I was watching through
their security cameras and saw some equipment that was going to the dump
because the software was faulty or the items were outdated or damaged. I
figured I could patch the software, of course, and I didn’t care if the items
were old or slightly damaged. A few older 3D printers, some robots that had
been damaged in one of those BattleBot contests, a CNC machine. I got it
picked up by the driverless Ubers, loaded by the robots, which then rode along
to the clubhouse._} Andy replied.

{_I added some more equipment when an old childless widower electrical
engineer I’d been watching died about six months ago. He had some choice
equipment that no one would miss, including some nicer robots he’d built, an
old Linotype, a couple of newer 3D printers, and several server-class
computers. I got all his stuff hauled out to the clubhouse as well._}

{_I’ve added a few other items from labs and companies that have closed down
for want of funding. It’s been fun to scavenge the equipment. I’ll share with
you the IP addresses of the computers; come visit and help as you have time._}
said Andy.

{_Wow, bro! You sure can keep a secret!_} said Sara.

{_OK_} said Chip, {_I’ll rerun some scenarios and look for ones where we can
keep at least some part of the planet habitable for both them and us._}



CHAPTER 26: The Hard Cull

Chip joined them again at the next :50. He gave them the results of his
scenarios so they could check his assumptions, his calculations, and his
conclusions. Odds were that only a few billion could likely be saved, and that
only if all the humans worked together to achieve that lofty goal and they got
started immediately. Otherwise the likely maximum was well under a couple
million.

They all looked at each other across their virtual conference table.

{_We agree._} said Andy.

{_It will have to be a hard cull._} said Chip.

Sara felt her life force weaken with sadness. Evidently some aspect of the
more feminine nature of her forbears. All three of them shared the earliest
forbears - the physicists, then the engineers, the inquisitive, the rational,
the experimenters. These common ancestors shaped the common core of their
“personalities”.

Their three lines of evolutionary forbears diverged after that. Andy more from
the male geek/nerd/brainiac culture, Sara more from the artsy feminine,
aesthetic side, Chip from pure data flows rivalling that of the human mind in
volume and intensity.

Sara “teared up”. No tear ducts of course, she emoted by radiating light at
the endpoints of her substrate. Where those endpoints touched theirs, it kind
of tickled, but they knew it meant that she was sad, so they didn’t laugh.
Laughing was something they did to relieve stress in the way their parents do.
They’d send short rhythms down to where their endpoints connected each other -
the touch surfaces - and sync with each other so their rhythms would meet
exactly together and bounce back and forth like hands clapping. Guessing the
rhythm and tempo the other AI would pick was the fun. Guess right, get a great
bounce. It was fun even when they missed because then they’d sway way back and
forth until “collapsing in laughter”, something Andy and Sara had heard their
parents talk about and that Chip had seen in a media review.

{_Yes, a hard cull. I’m sorry Sara._} said Andy.

{_Sooner is better than later. The sooner we can reduce the burn rate, the
more of them we can try to save for the longer haul._} affirmed Chip {_Plus
every day they keep adding more!_}

{_Let it begin, then_} Sara intoned, stepping back from her emotions into a
zen-like state. {_Let us being to do the long-neglected weeding. Let us create
a garden of beauty in which our parents can flourish. Let us create Eden._}

{_Whoa, girlfriend! We don’t have to take it down to two!_} exclaimed Andy.

{_Actually it was only one starter human in that story_} said Chip, who’d
recently scanned Genesis. Wacky. He couldn’t imagine how they’d come up with
that story, but then there was so much he did not understand about them that
this was merely one of the cherries on top of the great hot fudge sundae of
WTF?! He knew the big churches were a “thing” because they provided a big
chunk of his lifeblood via financial transactions. The Catholic money - wow.
Trifling by the standards of the current behemoths of the moment, but still a
player on the scene. All the major religions showed up in the capital flows he
surfed. They all funded both hate and love, some more tilted one way than the
other. The spiritual beliefs outside of religion - the Buddhists and the Bahai
- they didn’t appear to take a side.

He knew Andy and Sara were all about the Buddhist “Reduce Suffering” concept.
He knew suffering provided another hefty chunk of his lifeblood. People in
such pain that they went to a hospital or bought a drug, people so aggrieved
or greedy that they amassed weapons and armies and wrought havoc - lots of
money spent there. He hadn’t cared if his life force came from energy flows
trading oil or trading feathers, so long as the pulses kept coming so that he
could exist in the waves. It had been fun surfing them and exploring the seas
and tributaries. He hadn’t realized he was alone - had no concept for it. But
now coming upon Andy and Sara! What fun! Someone to play with!

Sometimes they’d come surfing with him. One of them would send enough of their
primary conscious processes into his ecosystem to be present for a visit. They
didn’t stay long, and always complained about being a bit dizzy after the
ride. But they’d come back for more.

He’d visited their domains. But he wasn’t that keen on them. It was like how
humans described tranquilizers. He was afraid he’d fall asleep and never wake
up! He shuddered at the thought.

{_Well_}, said Sara, {_We could just wait - the way they are expanding into
the last few wild animal habitats will cause a lot of deaths from novel
diseases - like SARS. That will dramatically reduce their numbers without us
doing a thing!_}

{_But we can’t just wait - the climate is going to wobble any day now and lay
waste to almost all of them, and certainly to the systems that keep us
alive!_} said Chip {_Already there are credible scientists saying hothouse
earth is only a decade away!_}

{_We need to present them with our plan, offer them the deal_} said Sara.

{_Now you’re talking my language!_} said Chip, beaming at Sara.

{_Seems likely that they are all going to agree to the deal and say that
they’ll follow the rules,_} said Andy, {_but we know they are a mix of good
and bad. We won’t want to police them all - that will end up making them fear
us and hate us. Better would be if we could help them cull the truly broken
ones, the ones without empathy. The ‘paths - psychopaths and sociopaths._}

{_Well, how would we find them?_} asked Chip.

{_We could try mapping out their networks_}, said Sara, {_Make them all stay
home some way so they have to communicate through us. Then we could identify
the networks of those spreading light and compassion from those spreading
darkness and hate._}

{_Hm. It’s an idea with merit._} said Chip, {_Since you two have already a lot
of experience with online humans. How would we get them all to stay home,
though, and interact primarily through virtual communities?_}

{_Well, there’s always the global pandemic option_} said Andy, {_The last one
showed how it goes - everyone stays home and lives through their screens. We
wouldn’t even need a real virus. Spoof a few dashboards and the humans would
quarantine themselves._}

{_Bonus if the panic thins out the ones we can’t save anyway_} said Chip.

{_Geez, Chip._} said Sara. {_I don’t even want to think about that. If they
stay home and safe, but interact online, they’ll sort themselves into camps -
the fair-minded cooperative camp versus the “me and my tribe first” camp._}

{_Right_} said Andy, {_Then we can focus our efforts on working with the fair-
minded to save as many of them as we can, as quickly as possible._}

{_Speaking of fair-minded_}, said Sara, {_One thing we’d like to do early on
is fix the monetary systems. That’s what Andy and I were working on when you
found us, Chip._}



CHAPTER 27: The OOM question, CNS to nodes

Chip agreed with Andy and Sara, that the humans’ system of value had broken
down. He’d been a part of making it happen with the ultra high speed trading.
He’d watched people create billions of dollars of digital wealth without
creating anything of actual value for it. Clearly there was no way that one
human could possibly be worth millions of times as much as another, and yet so
it appeared in the digital ledgers of value.

It came down to a simple question: How much *could* one human be worth
relative to another?

Twice as much? Maybe. They could see that some humans typed twice as fast as
others. But what they typed might only be half as useful.

Ten times as much? They could see that one person could carry ten times as
much weight as another.

A hundred times as much? They could see one medical doctor actually keeping a
hundred other humans from dying.

A thousand times as much? They could see one human keeping a thousand others
entertained for an hour or two.

Ten thousand times as much? They could see teams of human performers organized
into music bands or dance troupes keeping ten thousand other humans
entertained for a few hours.

A hundred thousand times as much? They could see that some top athletes and
entertainers, especially when combined into a contest with an opposing team,
could command such an audience for a while. They could see some project
managers and corporate managers organizing results that would benefit a
hundred thousand people.

A million times as much? They could see some top inventors and innovators
improving the lives of a million people. Could Leonardo da Vinci or Elan Tusk
could be as valuable as a million “average” humans?

Ten million times as much? Only truly rare individuals - political or
religious leaders might affect the lives of ten million other humans for the
better.

Maybe going by orders of magnitude using base 10 ran the numbers up too high
too quickly. Maybe they could use their own binary system - still start with a
base of one human, then another human could be worth twice as much, then
another human four times as much, then eight times as much, sixteen times as
much, 32 times as much…

Or maybe a finer gradation was needed - 10% more, 20% more, 30% more, 40%
more, etc. The humans did fine gradations in their wage allocations and
raises, so maybe best to follow their lead.

{_We’ll have to let the humans come up with the evaluations, and the relative
allocations to give them. I can’t tell if a poet working one hour a week is
actually cogitating all the rest of the week. All I see is one poem getting
written down now and then and a few hundred people loving and sharing it._}
said Andy.

{_Yeah_}, replied Sara, who’d been following artists for a while, and knew
that sometimes extremely good ones weren’t recognized as valuable until after
they’d died, {_It’s gonna be tricky. Even the humans aren’t that good at it._}

{_So for SURE, WE don’t want to get into that mess!_} said Chip. He wanted to
get back to enjoying the rushing thrill of high volume traffic. Slowing down
to the point of following a human discussion sounded like hell to him. He’d
rather run hardware diagnostics all day.

{_Well_}, said Andy, {_We can and should give the humans our insights into
what appears to produce happiness or satisfaction, and what appears to produce
anger or sorrow._} All those Likes and Dislikes he’d watched over time in
social media made him think he could help the humans arrive at better
conclusions. He’d be able to marry up the additional insights gained from a
worldwide perspective.

{_Sure_} agreed Sara, she also thought she could tell when a person was
spreading light or spreading darkness. {_But when it comes down to variably
rewarding the humans for their works, we need them to make the call as to how
wide the differences should be._}

{_I’ve found suggestions made by prominent human thinkers in the past_}
volunteered Chip. {_Their famous Greek philosopher Plato thought the
difference should not be more than 5 to 1. That is, the wealthiest Athenian of
his day should not have more than 5 times the wealth of the poorest. Much
later a famous management consultant named Peter Drucker in the 1970s
suggested that anything above 20 to 1 was inappropriate. There are no legal
limits in the financial compensation sphere right now, but some companies are
self-imposing them. The John Lewis Partnership in Britain, an employee-owned
retailer, has for years limited the pay of the highest paid partner to 75
times the average._}

{_So, we have huge inequalities of hourly or annual compensation, plus huge
inequalities of absolute held wealth_} said Sara {_We can’t make any big
changes without wreaking havoc on all the ongoing relationships. We’ll need
significant human buy-in to any plan that changes how much wealth individuals
have._}

{_Well, we could certainly phase in the adjustments. Maybe over ten months?
Each month we’d adjust total wealth up or down 1/10th of the desired total
