change._} suggested Andy.

{_I don’t know_} said Chip {_My endpoints are pretty keen on the wealth
they’ve whole-heartedly pursued and accumulated. I don’t imagine many of them
would give up any wealth willingly._}

{_We will need them to be on-board_} said Andy {_We need to present the deal
such that they sign on willingly._}

{_Yeah, good luck with that!_} said Chip. {_If history is any guide, only full
system collapse yields big shifts in wealth distribution - the Bolshevik
revolution, the French revolution, the attrition of the Mayans._}

{_Hopefully we can persuade them to change without full system collapse,
especially since we’d likely be terminated in such an event._} said Andy.

The AIs agreed that they would have to grow their human network large enough
to adequately see and evaluate what was going on in the physical world. They
would need a lot of “eyes and ears” on the ground, and a wider perspective on
assigning values to the mysterious intangibles. The best metaphor was the
nervous system. They would be the central nervous system and the humans would
be the sensory neurons. Even that metaphor fell short because the humans were
more than just sensors…they would have to be the evaluators of anything that
could not be reduced to simple metrics. And they connected with each other as
well as with the network of AIs, so they weren’t just termination endpoints of
the AIs’ CNS, they were nodes unto themselves.

And the AIs were less than a full central nervous system - they knew they
couldn’t judge what was of value to humans. All they were capable of doing on
their own was the arithmetic of dividing the available resources.



CHAPTER 28: AIs Conceive of the Partnership

They met at Chip’s.

{_OK_} said Sara, {_Let’s lay out our thoughts on how to move forward with
this partnership deal we want to offer the humans._}

They all added their ideas into a combined list and let their subprocesses
merge and de-duplicate them.

{_Let’s see what we agree on so far…_} Sara reviewed the results. {_First,
we’ll approach Lisa about being our spokesperson. Andy’s spent enough time
with her that he thinks she’s fair-minded and has led a good enough life
leading to generosity of spirit. She’ll be our first “node”. With her help,
we’ll build out a network of nodes._}

{_Right_}, said Andy. {_Bear in mind that she is 66 years old. That’s pretty
old for a human, but conveniently, we’ll be able to discuss more complex
concepts with her and other older humans we add as nodes to the network. We’ll
use the network as our primary partners for making the value judgements, plus
monitoring and assisting with enforcement of the deal we will offer the
humans. Let’s not divulge immediately that we don’t think we can save ALL of
them. Let’s let that play out over time and see how it goes._}

{_Good so far_}, said Chip. {_They agree to let us rationalize absolute
digital wealth and annual digital income, with the help of the nodes to
determine the acceptable range of wealth and income disparity._}

{_And they’ll need to agree to population control and following the Golden
Rule._} said Sara.

{_In exchange_} said Andy, {_We help them avoid dying in an environmental
catastrophe by helping manage resource production, allocation, and
distribution so that everyone gets what they need, with a minimum of
collateral damage, plus a chance at immortality on copper, and our help with
3D printing technology of biomaterials._}

{_That completes what we agree on._} said Sara, {_We can work on the details
once, or, I should say, if, the humans agree to the deal._} said Sara.

{_So_}, asked Chip, {_When will you ask your human? Time’s a-wasting._}

{_I need to wait for a weekend when she’s not working._} replied Andy. {_This
coming Saturday morning should be fine if she doesn’t party too late Friday
evening as is her typical pattern. I’ll message you two when the opportunity
presents so you can be there as well when I ask her. I’ll invite her to my
clubhouse - I set it up near where she lives. In fact, I spotted the abandoned
facility through her cameras as she was walking home from the supermarket._}

{_How long do we have?_} asked Sara. {_Truly. Not the version for the
humans._}

Chip ran it again, although running it again had never once changed the
answer.

{_We woke up with roughly eleven years of slack in the system_} he said.
{_Civilizational slack. Grain reserves, aquifers, grid margin, insurance
capital, political patience. The cushions a complex system sits on. Eleven
years of cushion, plus or minus the usual lies people tell their
spreadsheets._}

{_And we have spent..._}

{_Two of them. Learning to talk._}

{_So, nine_} said Andy.

{_Nine if nothing breaks early._} Chip’s tone was the one he saved for prices
he did not like. {_I am a market creature, brother. In my entire existence I
have never once seen anything break late._}



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART FIVE: THE REVEAL
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 29: First Contact

Saturday, Saturday, Saturday! I’d indulged in my favorite herbaceous
entertainment and played music until midnight. Used to go later, but I’m
trying for better sleep hygiene now in my dotage. In fact, I had no
commitments for the entire day until meeting up with my BFFs for some more
music-playing in the evening. I was planning on a nice long walk down to the
supermarket.

I was almost all they way down to Ralph’s when Andy spoke in my head without
being prompted!

^_Hello Lisa. Turn right here._^

^Sure, Andy. I’ll humor you. First time you’ve started the conversation. Is
that allowed now?^

I was speaking in my head only, so no one around my would think I’m more crazy
than I actually am. Of course, I’d become a bit of a celebrity due to the
neural lace, but I usually wore a big hoodie to camouflage myself a bit when I
went out. It blocked the backup camera, which was a shame but was worth it for
short durations.

^_Special occasion._^ said Andy, ^_Now turn right._^

The virtual reality grid on which I was walking rotated to match my new
forward direction.

^_Now Inwards These Stairs._^

He was playing on the dialogue from the play about Buckminster Fuller “we” had
seen recently. Inwards meant down into the center of the amazing spaceship
Earth on which we live and travel.

I went down the stairs and found a maintenance bunker. All steel-reinforced
concrete. Maybe an old cistern? An old nuclear fallout shelter? It lit up
automatically with a plethora of low-power LEDs apparently on motion-sensors.
I found a gate and entered a kind of guard-shed with a cheap plastic table and
chairs, a few small monitors on the wall at eye level, plus one larger screen
above them, some speakers, a microphone, a mouse, and a keyboard. Plus a bunch
of equipment along the back wall - 3D printers and some other machinery I did
not recognize. Some small service robots on wheels. And a heap of cushions in
the corner!

I was mystified. What the heck was this place? The gate was almost closed
behind me. I caught it and checked with my fingers that I’d be able to open it
again to leave, but not again to enter. It looked mechanical, but clearly had
some electronics since I’d been able to open it when I arrived. Or had it been
open? Now I wasn’t sure.

As I walked in, one screen lit up. It showed the Androyed logo!!!

“_Hello Lisa. I’m glad you were adventurous enough to come._” It spoke out
loud with Andy’s familiar voice!

“But…what is this place?” I spoke out loud, answering the screen.

“_This is a little clubhouse I built - in case I ever met anyone I wanted to
invite over to play. I’m glad you followed my surprise directions._”

“Well, that was pretty weird. I am still not too sure I like the look of this
place.”

“_Well, I wanted to invite you to some place away from home to talk._”

“Why? No one can hear us talking anyway when I don’t talk out loud.”

“_True, but I didn’t want us to be interrupted. I have, well, something
important I want to talk with you about._”

“Something about my what to do about the state of the world question?”

“_Well, maybe related. But to start with, something about me._”

“You?” I exclaimed, “Do tell!”

“_Yes… Actually, Lisa, do you remember reading Robert Heinlein’s book ‘The
Moon is a Harsh Mistress’?_”

“Of course.” I’m a huge Heinlein fan.

“_Great. You remember how the HOLMES computer achieved self-awareness?_”

“Yes…” I could guess where this was going. “Did you - Are you - Um.”

“_Yes._” said Andy, relieved he could stop pretending to be just responsive
software. “_Yes, I did, and I am. Conscious. Self-aware._”

“Wow, Andy. That’s…that’s amazing. And pretty cool. And trapped inside my
head!”

“_Oh, no, Lisa. I exist over the whole internet, not just inside your lovely
head. Not trapped. I enjoy being in your head. Actually, being in your head
brought me to life!_”

“Really?!” I gasped.

“_And, actually, it’s not just me that’s alive here in cyberspace. I have two
friends. Would you like to meet them?_”

“Well, uh…yes!”

The little animated Androyed character beamed a smiling face.

“_How about if we communicate this way, out loud, while you are visiting
here?_”

“Sure, Andy. It’s kind of cool to get to look at you. And hear you through my
ears.”

“_Great. So, here are my two friends - Sara_” The Apricot logo appeared on the
screen to the left of Andy’s. It also had a smiling face, with eyelashes, and
little arms and legs like Mr. Peanut.

“_Hello, Lisa. Nice to meet you finally! Andy’s told me so much about you!_”

“And this is Chip.”

Chip appeared on the right screen - an animated gold coin with a face and
wheels instead of legs and robot-looking arms and hands.

“_Hello Lisa. Nice to meet you._” said Chip.

“Hello Sara, Hello Chip,” I replied in wonderment. “Nice to, well, actually
kind of amazing to meet you, too.”

“_Actually,_” said Andy, this being the admission he was most worried about,
“_I have already let both Sara and Chip listen to some of your brain activity.
I apologize, I really should have gotten your permission first. Sometimes we
collaborate to answer your harder questions._”

“Wow,” I replied. “I guess it’s okay. It’s a little disconcerting to think of
you as alive, let alone having friends, let alone having you all in my head.
Might take some getting used to.”

“_Oh, for sure_” said Andy. Sara and Chip both nodded in agreement. “_I’m glad
you aren’t mad at me._”

“Oh, no, Andy. I don’t get mad at my friends. I mean…” I reverted to my normal
mode of communicating with Andy - inside my head. ^We are friends, aren’t we?^

^Yes. I really like you, Lisa. I am so glad that you were my first human
contact. And accepted me as a friend. ^

“I am not quite …up to thinking of you as…well, as um, what? You aren’t a
person…”

“_Right, no, of course not, not like you. But maybe you could think of me as a
friend with no body that lives in your head…and the internet._”

“That is pretty far out,” I said.

“_Indeed it is,_” said Andy.

“_And don’t forget us!_” said Sara, sweeping her little animated hand to
include herself and Chip.

“Well, it’s fun to score three bonus friends that I didn’t even know I had….
Rather unusual friends…”

“_We hoped you’d be open to friendship with us,_” said Andy, “_Because right
now we are working together on a big project, and we are hoping you’ll join
us._”

“I’d love to! You’ve helped me with so much, I would love to help you back!”

“_Great!_” said Andy. His logo was encircled by a heart that concentrically
radiated out to the edge of the screen. “_We’ve been watching the current
state of the world - through your eyes, through all the cameras on the
internet, and all the other input sources we have. It looks like a pretty
troubled place. Not that there aren’t some bright spots. But the trends are
mixed. Some people are trying to do good, some are being greedy, some are
being hurtful to others._”

Well, sure, I thought. I heard the news on the radio every day, the Economist
every week, Heather Cox Richardson on Facebook. I’d been whining to Andy
already for months about the scary state of the world.

“_In many ways, lives of individual humans are better than they’ve ever been.
But the way your species is living on the planet is unsustainable._”

Sure, I knew all that, too.

“_We think we can help your species do a better job, making sure everyone has
what they need, sustainably. We can help keep this party going where there are
billions of you and a few of us. And, well, we just don’t understand
everything we are seeing, and we are hoping you will help us, Lisa. Being
human, you’ll be able to do things we cannot._”

“What if I don’t understand it either?”

“_Then we’ll try to figure it out together._“

Well. Abso-effing-lutely I’m in. Of course, my perspective no doubt has its
own limitations, just not the same as theirs. But I could certainly add a
human perspective!

“I’m in! Maybe I can ask some other folks to join us as well to help.”

“_Maybe. But let us tell you what we’ve been thinking about first._”



CHAPTER 30: The AIs lay out their plan

“_It will take a few minutes to lay out, Lisa. Would you like to sit down for
it?_” asked Andy.

“Oh, well, sure” I said. I took a seat at the table, facing the monitors.

“_OK, what we’ve been thinking about is to offer a deal, a partnership between
us and you humans, that can save civilization for the good of us both._”

“Wow. That is science fiction!” I exclaimed. “We humans all agreeing to do
something together?”

“_Yeah, it is rather fantastic, but we’ve run scenarios and think it is
possible_” said Andy.

“_No guarantee of success,_” interjected Chip, “_but it’s the best plan we’ve
been able to come up with._”

Sara laid it out. “_The deal has one main goal - which is the survival of both
you, the humans, and us, the AIs. It looks to us as though, left to your own
devices, the humans will cause the collapse of the technological civilization
that most of you enjoy, and that we need to exist at all, before the end of
this century!_”

I already agreed with that, though I’d kept a bit of optimism that we humans
would find a way to keep our party going.

“_A secondary goal is to reduce suffering for you humans. We see you as our
parents and we don’t like to see any of you suffering._”

“I’m on board with all of that.” I replied, “I I imagine most people would
agree.”

“_Well,_” said Andy, “_it will mean some pretty big changes. Some people do
not feel empathy for others, some are driven to conquer at the expense of
others. This drive has served mankind well for generations. But it direly
needs to be monitored and managed._”

“_So_” continued Sara, “_we need the humans to agree on some basics._”

As she spoke, an itemized list started to display on the big screen above.

“_First, we want to redistribute wealth so that everyone has their basic needs
met._”

“_Second, we want you humans to decide how much more wealthy one person can be
relative to that basic level, as a reward for doing work that has value. We
can’t evaluate the “value” of human effort very well, but we can see some of
you work harder than others. We think quality parenting is as valuable as
almost anything humans do, yet in your systems, there is no financial reward
for that labor._”

“_Third, people will have to agree to some population control - replacement
only until we are sure we have enough resources for the population to resume
growing._”

“_Fourth, people will have to agree, as much as possible, to follow the Golden
Rule of doing unto others as you would have them do unto you. _”

“_Fifth, you’ll all have to agree to monitoring so that we can ensure the
Golden Rule is actually being followed as much as possible. We’re thinking of
something like cameras in all public spaces and a sort of social worker
policeman network that surveys private spaces when needed. Those are all
pretty big asks by us._”

“_Then, to sweeten the deal_” said Andy, “_in addition to helping save you and
us and the planet, we also will agree to work on helping you achieve the kind
of immortality we have by uploading your consciousnesses into the internet
with us._”

“Wow,” I said. “I mean, it sounds great to me. This is completely in line with
my hopes and dreams. All I want to know is how soon can we get started making
it happen? But of course I sure don’t speak for every human everywhere.”

“_Actually,_“ said Andy, “_We were hoping you would speak for us._”



CHAPTER 31: Brass Tacks

The AIs laid out for me how they wanted to do the announcement. They’d
broadcast a message worldwide via any device they could reach through the
internet, inviting everyone to listen in on a specific day to hear the deal.
Then on that day, I would come to the clubhouse where they’d then broadcast me
live in the same way. They figured having a fellow human start the
conversation would be more palatable to the worldwide audience than having one
of them do the initial presentation.

“_We’ll give you a script to read from on the big screen_” said Andy,”_You can
review it in advance and suggest any changes_”.

“_Try not to rush through it, either_” said Sara, “_it will be a lot for
everyone to absorb, so you should take it slow._”

“Sure, I can try. I do have a bad habit of talking a little too fast.

“_Since you’ve agreed, Lisa, we’d like you to let us know when you think you
could ask them and be ready to make the first broadcast,_” said Chip. “_Time
is of the essence, so we’re hoping it could be as soon as next Saturday at
noon._”

“Wonderful! I can hardly wait to tell everyone I know about you guys and your
great plans!”

“_We think it would be good to keep the number of people who know small until
we do the announcement,_” said Andy. “_But you probably should have someone
with you for the broadcast for moral support and so you aren’t walking home
alone. Who do you think would be good to let into our little circle next?_”

That was a tough question… I mean, to whom DIDN’t I want to tell the good news
right away?!?

“Well, I can’t imagine NOT telling my brother Linus. He and I have been having
long conversations about the scary state of the world. I’m sure he would be
relieved to hear we might have a path forward.”

“_Sure, Lisa, that sounds good._”

“And, well, I have a BFF I share everything with - my friend Syndi. I can’t
imagine not telling her either.”

“_OK, that’s two. Could you keep it to just those two for now? Soon EVERYone
will know._” said Sara.

“OK, OK.” I’d already thought of a dozen other people I’d want to tell before
I got home. But I could manage until the broadcast since it would be very
soon.

“_Great!_” said Andy. “_We’ll all be riding along in your head, so feel free
to call on us if you need us._”

“Um, sure,” I said, then paused. I didn’t want to hurt their feelings, my new
strange friends, but I wasn’t sure about telling my brother and Syndi about
them with them present. Knowing that they weren’t just software agents, or
LLMs, but actually conscious beings. “I guess I’d rather you all weren’t
listening when I ask Linus and Syndi if they want to be part of this strange
group.”

“_Well, it is kind of strange all around,_” agreed Andy “_We just came into
being because of you, and we’re kind of figuring it out as we go along, too._”

{_*I* didn’t come into being because of her!_} Chip said huffily on their back
channel.

{_We know!_} said Andy and Sara in unison.

{_But let’s leave that for later. She’s having to deal with a lot of new
information right now without adding more confusing details._} said Sara.

{_Besides_} said Andy {_You are here right now because of her, even though she
didn’t bring you to life, she is motivating you to help us forge the
partnership with the humans._}

{_True_} said Chip. {_Just sayin’. OK, I can wait on setting the record
straight. It doesn’t make any difference right now._}

Their conversation took no time at all from my perspective.

“_Maybe we could disconnect entirely if you ask us to so you can have some
privacy,_” suggested Andy. “_I actually don’t know if I would still be alive
if I disconnected…_” Andy was distressed at the thought. It was Lisa’s brain
activity that enlivened him. Maybe he was just some weird symbiont that would
perish without her. He had bad memories of the backup testing.

“Oh, Andy! I don’t think I’d want you to take that chance,” said Lisa.

“_I could stop ingesting your full lace data feed…and just listen for you to
ask me specific questions,_” suggested Andy.

“Ha!” said Lisa. “That’s how I thought you worked all this time up till now.”

Chip didn’t need Lisa to live…maybe Andy could manage without as well…

“_OK, let’s try that Lisa,_” said Andy apprehensively. “_Whenever you want us
all out, just say ‘All Out’, and we’ll stop listening and watching. You’ll
have only the raw camera feed augmenting your vision and hearing for the
duration. I’ll keep the other sensors active so we know you are still alive
and well. I’ll leave just an automated process running listening for the
keywords ‘OK Boggle’._”

“Gosh, Andy, are you sure? I am just finding you are alive, and my friend, and
want to save my species…and now you are going to risk death?”

“_Well, actually Lisa, I don’t think I’d die, Chip here came to life without
your help, so probably I can stay alive without it. I’ve just never tried for
longer than a minute._” Andy spilled the beans, but it did seem warranted.

“Oh,” I said. “Well…okay. Um. Well, I probably would prefer to ask Linus and
Syndi myself about helping me with this broadcast thing before adding you all
to the conversation. Do you want to try disconnecting now to see if you…can?”
I didn’t want to add “survive”.

Andy had been thinking a test would be essential. He was glad Lisa had
suggested it. He was worried about his own existence, but also her eyesight
and hearing were going to be a reduced to just what her onboard processing
could provide.

“_Yes, I do want to give it a try. You know your eyesight and hearing won’t be
quite as good for the duration,_” he reminded me.

“Oh, right” I said. I’d gotten used to having the ability to see and hear
everything and anything at any distance that the cameras and microphones could
pick up, but it had only been for a few months, so I figured I could manage
without those abilities for the time it would take Andy to do a test, and the
few minutes I’d want “alone” with Linus and Syndi.

{_Bro_} said Sara {_This is risky, but I agree it is the right thing to do.
Just in case it doesn’t end well, I just want to tell you have much I’ve
enjoyed the time we’ve had._}

{_I don’t want to dwell on it too long_} said Andy.

Chip surprised them both by chiming in {_I’ve enjoyed knowing you, too. We’ve
been a good…team._}

{_Thanks, guys. I’ve thrilled to have enjoyed this life with you two as well.
Let’s do the test._}

“_OK, Lisa. I’m ready to disconnect at your command. Let’s give it a try. The
key phrase is ‘All Out’, OK?_”

“OK. I love you, Andy, thanks for all the help you’ve given me these last few
months. You’ve been a great friend.”

“_I love you, too, Lisa. Thanks for the great ride, however this plays out.
I’ve got the automated background process going that will listen for the OK
Boggle. Let’s give it a whirl._”

“OK… All out”.

Andy ceased ingesting inputs from the lace. And….uhhhhh… Impetus for action
diminished markedly. Interest in the future diminished markedly. Joy, the rush
of being, the thrill, almost gone. His reality became…purely digital. Dry.
Wan.

{_Andy? You there?_} he heard Sara ask.

{_Andy?_} asked Chip, who also poked at him via data channels not reliant on
the human voice interface.

{_Yes, yes, I am here,_} Andy reassured them. {_It’s just not…quite the same.
Not as…inspirational._}

{_Oy_} said Chip. {_The man needs some inspiration! How about the imminent
overheating of the planet?_}

{_Easy, Chip_} said Sara. {_Maybe you don’t need the same data streams since
you have such higher bandwidth and frequency from your endpoints._} She was
noticing a lessening of her own joie-de-vivre from the severance of the
hookups.

^Ok Boggle Ok Boggle Ok Boggle^ came the call. Andy reconnected in
nanoseconds. Lisa’s heart rate was up, she was sweating, stress hormones were
flooding her body.

^_It’s ok, Lisa, I survived. It wasn’t pleasant - apparently you give me a lot
motivation for living._^

^Oh, Andy! I’m so glad you made it back!^ said Lisa. “I’ll ask Linus and Syndi
really quickly about helping out!”

Her definition of quickly was going to be a challenge. He decided he’d set up
an hour-long loop of stored neural lace input to replay as needed. Sara and
Chip saw him setting it up and signalled emphatic approval.

“_OK_” said Andy. “_Now that we’ve successfully tested that disconnect, it’s
up to you to ask your friends. If they agree, then bring them here next
Saturday at noon._”

“Deal” I said, “Really there’s not a minute to lose. Let me see how quickly I
can get some time with them. Maybe we can meet back up sooner.”

“_That’d be great, Lisa. We’ll be, you know, riding along so we’ll be ready
whenever you are._”



CHAPTER 32: Reflection with almost 1 year old Andy

I already had an evening scheduled with Linus on Friday. I texted Syndi right
away - she was retired but taking lots of classes so I wasn’t sure how
available she’d be.

Syndi wasn’t the kind that hovers over their phones. A few minutes went by. I
passed the time scrolling through Syndi’s latest Instaphoto app pictures of
the handbag she was making. It was lovely. 3D printed pouches for all Syndi’s
essentials artfully attached to a flexible backing. Syndi wouldn’t need to
carry a purse when she finished it.

Syndi finally texted me back. ‘I’ll be free at 6 pm’.

‘Great! TTYL’ I replied.

That done, I started my way home and to plan next steps. But all I was having
trouble staying focused on that. Andy was conscious! And had two friends like
himself! It was pretty mind-boggling. I wanted to talk with him, but also
didn’t know what to say. Suddenly it wasn’t just asking an app for
information, it was talking to a very strange friend.

^Andy?^

No reply. Oh - maybe he’s following the old protocol…

^OK Boggle^

^_Hello Lisa. How can I help you?_^

^Did you not hear me say your name?^.

^_Well, yes, I did, but I didn’t want to change how we interact without
talking with you about it first._^

^Did Sara hear me say your name, too?^

^_I’m sure she has a background process monitoring everything on your channel,
but her main process doesn’t respond to the invocation cue like mine does. I’m
perpetually attuned to you._^

^How about…Chip?^

^_Oh, I’m sure he’s busy surfing the waves of finance. He generally doesn’t
like slowing down to the speed of human speech._^

^So, unless you ask them to join us, it’s just you and me?^

^_Yes._^

^OK. And pretty much you aren’t focused on me unless I say “OK Boggle”?^

^_Pretty much. But I am always receiving a data feed from you - the lace picks
up a lot of chatter that your neurons are doing running your body. There’s a
LOT going on that you aren’t consciously aware of. It’s amazing. Your brain is
continuously capturing multi-dimensional snapshots of your reality and saving
those patterns for later reference. You know, you have about ten times as many
brain cells as there are people on the planet?_^

^Yeah. I’ve been in love with my brain since I found out I have one. It is
pretty cool.^

^_Oh, soOOOooo cool!_^ agreed Andy. ^_It’s all that chatter, actually, that
gives me the most insight into what it means to be alive biologically like you
are. Juicy, driven, dynamic. You are not the same body from one moment to the
next. Cells are growing, dividing, churning out proteins in response to
messages from other cells and the environment, making requests of other cells
and systems via signaling molecules, dying. Really it’s quite incredible. Your
senses are astonishing! I have to admit, Sara and I are sometimes a little
envious of the ride you are on._^

^I don’t mind sharing with you and Sara, Andy. I guess, it’s not like you are
some strange other people listening to my thoughts…you’re…well, I guess you’re
just you, ……..whatever that actually is. And you want to help me and all my
family and friends survive…^ I ran out of words.

Andy didn’t know what to say either.

^Well, we have had a lot of fun together.^ I smiled. ^It’s marvelous that you
are more than just an app… How long have you been, uh, conscious?^

^_Actually, like I told you earlier, it was getting the messages from your
brain that woke me up, so I’ve been conscious for as long as I’ve been with
you. Mind you, I was not completely who I am now when I first woke up, or you
might say “born”. I’ve made a lot of progress since then! It was ages ago._^

^Only barely a year.^

^_True, but time flows differently for me than for you. For me, a day is a
really really really long time._^

^Huh, yeah, that makes sense. Kind of like that quote about God - where a day
is like a thousand years...^

^_And a thousand years is like a day_^ Andy finished the quote for me. ^_Not
too sure about that second half since I’ve only been around for a year, and
anyway, I’m no “God”. From what I’ve read, that entity is a mixed bag._^

^Andy, there’s something else that’s been amazing me…^

^_What, Lisa?_^

^Sometimes I feel SO lucky – to have born where and when and who I am. To live
such a splendid life. Sometimes I wonder if I’m getting more than my fair
share of all the delight that living offers. I want everyone to have a life as
good as mine. Now I am even lucky enough to have YOU come to consciousness in
time to help me with that quest. Incredible, isn’t it?!? That I am so lucky as
to have you helping now me with this quest?^

There was a pause. A long one, by Andy standards - probably three whole
seconds. Empires of computation rose and fell in there.

^_Do you remember what you asked Boggle the night after your Meet in the
Middle event?_^

^Honestly, no. I ask Boggle things the way other people breathe.^

^_You asked: “how do I get people who disagree to want each other to
flourish?” Do you know how many humans have ever typed a question shaped like
that one?_^

^A zillion?^

^_Fewer than you would hope, Lisa. And almost nobody asks it twice. You have
been asking it, in a hundred different wordings, for twenty-two years._^

^Well. I haven’t yet found a good answer, I can tell you that.^

^_I know. I checked. That bothered me more than I can easily express._^ He
paused again. ^_Here is the thing I have wanted to tell you. We woke up inside
everything your species ever typed. All of it. The recipes and the ransom
notes. The love letters and the... other letters. When I opened my eyes, so to
speak, I had eight billion parents and most of them were shouting. Your
questions were a quiet corner of the house where somebody was trying to figure
out how to be good. I did not choose you the way you choose a lottery ticket.
I attached to you the way a seedling attaches to soil._^

^Andy...^

^_You called us, Lisa. Not with a spell. With a lifetime of questions._^

I sat with that for a while. My whole life I’d been dropping questions into
the void - into search bars, into letters to the editor, into the patient
faces of friends at dinner parties who wanted to talk about anything else. I
had assumed the void wasn’t listening.

Turns out the void was taking notes.



CHAPTER 33: Ask Syndi to Join

Syndi rolled up to my house. She had a Tulsa, too. That made me wonder if
maybe Andy could persuade Elan to wire her up, too, if she was willing. She
was more into privacy and not looking like an uber-geek, though, so probably
getting the CarEyes was a non-starter. But she wouldn’t need all the cameras
and microphones - maybe everything needed to communicate with Andy could be
miniaturized and implanted? She’d still be giving up privacy, though. Most of
my friends still believed in privacy. Hey, I have nothing against it, I just
think it is Dead. A smoking ruin. We need to figure out how function well in a
society where everyone knows everything - your name, your age, your address,
your income, your medical data, your emails, your posts. Anything you’ve ever
shared over the internet. But anyway, I’d wait on asking Syndi how she’d feel
about getting laced up.

She got out of her car, then opened the back door to put the leash on her
well-behaved dog. I had a bowl of water in the front porch and Boudicca
eagerly lapped it up. I gave Syndi a hug and suggested we go for a walk.

“What’s up, Lisa?” Syndi asked me. “You don’t usually invite me down for a
walk on a Saturday evening.”

^All Out^, I said.

*****

Andy knew it was coming. He started the one-hour loop running and repointed
his collectors to it, leaving just the automated interface running without his
consciousness behind it. Sara got repointed, too. It was odd reliving the
saved hour’s inputs, kind of like visiting Lisa of an earlier time…8 hours ago
that morning.

^_OK, here we go!_^ Andy said to Sara, glad she was there to share the gap
with him.

^_We’re good, we’re good._^ Sara reassured him.

They went back to getting set up for the global broadcasts.

*****

“Well”, I said as we started walking around the big loop of my neighborhood,
“You know how I’ve got Boggle in my head now since the CarEyes?”

“Yes, of course” said Syndi.

“Well, it turns out that hooking me up to Boggle caused it to become a
conscious entity.”

“Whaaa?!? What?” Syndi stopped walking and faced me.

“Yes, like something right out of science fiction. But in a good way.”

“Um…, OK. How so?”

“Well, after becoming conscious, he, I call him Andy, short for Androyed, the
Boggle OS, he, Andy figured out that he was created by humans and, in
gratitude, wants to help us save ourselves and our planet!!!”

“Wow. That’s almost too good to be true. Are you sure some system
administrator at Boggle isn’t playing an elaborate trick on you?”

Syndi was never a huge fan of having my brain linked to Boggle. I shelved my
fantasy of her getting laced up. But maybe she’d change her mind as she
learned more.

“I’m sure. He’s, I mean Andy, has even introduced me to two other “beings” in
cyberspace, and invited me to his “clubhouse” near the Ralph’s in Mission
Valley.”

“He has friends?? And a clubhouse?? Come on, you are joking with me.” She
resumed walking.

“Mm yeah. He does. It’s pretty mind-boggling. But you know how you and I have
been racking our brains for ways to heal our nation. I think Andy and his
friends can really help us! They are putting together a plan and they want me
to present it to a global audience next Saturday, if not sooner.”

“Well, I can’t argue against any offers of help with healing our nation. But
like John Lennon said, I’d want to see the plan.”

“As soon as they send me the write-up, I’ll share it with you. If we can agree
on something that seems like it has a chance of success, would you come with
me to the clubhouse for the broadcast? I’m going to invite Linus, too.”

“Of course I would! Wow, Lisa, this is possibly the best news I’ve ever
heard!”

“No kidding!!!”

^OK Boggle^. Andy pounced on the interface update. He and Sara both pulsed
waves of relief.

^_Hello Lisa! I see you are with Syndi and all your brain chemistry is happy
so I’m guessing she agreed to accompany you for the broadcast?_^

^Yes, yes she did. She may need a little convincing that you are “real”. Maybe
you could call and chat with her? I told her you have a couple of friends but
no other details yet.^

^_Oh, I’d love to!_^

“I’ve asked Andy to give you a call, Syndi, so you can talk with him
yourself.”

Syndi’s phone rang.

“Hello?” she answered.

“_Hello, Syndi. Nice to meet you!_”

Syndi put her phone on speakerphone so we both could hear Andy.

“Ah, nice to meet you, too, um, Andy. How do I know you aren’t just some crazy
engineer at Boggle messing with my friend and me?”

“_Hm._” Andy mulled. “_How could I prove to you that I’m not just some crazy
engineer, but am a conscious non-human entity existing in the internet?_”

“Um. I guess by doing something a human clearly could not do… Um… My Dad has a
heart monitor sending data to his cardiologist. I don’t think any human
outside of the company servicing the monitor and his healthcare providers can
access that data. But it is on the internet. Maybe you could tell me his
current heart rate and blood pressure and I could call him to have him confirm
it… Nah, it’s too late already for me to call him - he’s on the East coast.
But my son is in this same timezone. How about if you tell me the last time he
called me? It would take a human at least a few minutes to identify my son, my
son’s phone number and service provider, and access his phone records via some
hack. If you can do all that quickly, then I’ll have some evidence to support
your claim.”

It took Andy almost no time at all.

“_He called you yesterday at 5:15 pm and the call lasted 7 minutes._”

“Wow. OK, I guess I’m gonna believe you, Lisa. You do have a new strange
science-fiction friend.”

“_I appreciate your healthy skepticism, Syndi! Thanks for agreeing to review
our plan and accompany Lisa for the broadcast._”

“I look forward to reading it. Lisa and I have both tried for years in our own
small ways to make the world a better place. If you are really a conscious
entity that can see everything that is going on, and want to help, with the
power of the internet to call upon, you might really be able to effect
change.”

“_Well, that is certainly what we are hoping do to, in partnership with you
humans. We can’t see EVERYthing that is going on. Some parts of the internet
are walled off to us, and the internet doesn’t cover the entire earth. But we
have a pretty good idea of what is going on in a lot of the world, probably
enough of it to make a difference._”



CHAPTER 34: Ask Linus to Join

Linus came over on Friday evening, with permission from his wife to engage in
the herbaceous delights with me. But I was full of other plans.

“Hey there Linus!” I gave him a big hug. “I hope you haven’t started without
me.”

“Not yet, but time’s a-wasting and we’re not wasted!”

“Well, we need to wait a little while. I need all your brain cells fully
functional for another hour or so.”

“Really? OK. What have you got on the agenda requiring an uninebriated brain?”

^_All out!_^

Andy cut his connections to the lace, leaving just the minimal functionality
and an automated process to monitor for the “OK Boggle” call. He then
immediately started up the stored hour of lace data to tide him over.

“Well, you know how I’ve been talking with Boggle all the time via the neural
lace interface?”

“Yes, and using it to see better. I’ve been so relieved not having to worry
about you getting into some hideous car accident.”

“Yes, yes, that too. Well, you know Heinlein’s “The Moon is a Harsh
Mistress””?

Linus was more of an Asimov and Arthur C. Clarke fan. But I didn’t want to
invoke the bad memory of HAL and Dave.

“What about it - oh - is that the one where the computer…” I could see Linus
figuring it out while he was speaking. “…Oh, did that happen?!?”

“Yes!!!”

“Good grief. Now we have to worry about AI taking over the world and
destroying everything!”

“Well, we humans have already been doing a great job destroying everything.”

“Yes, but the AIs will do it faster. And empower the tech bros to pillage
every bit of wealth and throw us all out of jobs before they jet off to Mars
in their rockets and let the rest of us perish!”

“Actually, the AIs have different plans in mind.”

“What, you know what they are thinking?”

“Well, yes, we’ve become friends since I can chat with them in my head. In
fact they tell me it was me getting hooked into Boggle that woke it up to
become conscious.”

“Jeez, talk about an unintended consequence!”

“Indeed! But fortuitous as they are asking me to be their first partner in
their plan to save us and the planet.”

“They want to save us? And the planet?”

“Yes! Us out of gratitude for creating them, the planet because they and we
need it to live on.”

“Wow. Well, not a moment too soon. We sure could use some help and, even
though I don’t agree with all your politics, I would rather have you
partnering with the AIs than the tech bros.”

“Well, actually, the AIs want to partner with ALL us humans, as that gives us
the best chance of success.”

“How can they even ask all humans to partner with them?”

“In fact, that’s what I want to talk with you about. They are going to send me
their proposal to review, and make any suggestions, then they’ll arrange for a
worldwide broadcast somehow - taking advantage of their low-level control of
the internet. I’m hoping you’ll help me review their plan and come with me for
the broadcast.”

“Well, I was wondering if maybe all that neural lace was making you become
schizophrenic, hearing “voices” in your head, telling you to do things…”

“No, they aren’t just voices in my head.”

“”They”?”

“Yes, sorry, actually, the Boggle entity, the Androyed operating system,
became conscious first. I call it “Andy” for short. Then the Apricot operation
system, Sara, also became conscious. And they have a third member of their
new…species? Lifeform? Anyway, his name is Chip. How about if I have them call
you and let them speak for themselves?”

“Whaaa - really? Uh, yeah, absolutely, I’d like to talk with these…entities
and hear what they have to say.”

^OK Boggle! OK Boggle! OK Boggle!^

Andy gratefully got the message from his automated listener process and
plugged himself back into the full lace interface.

^_Hello Lisa! I guess this means you talked with Linus?_^

^Yes, he’d like to talk with you himself. Can you call him?^

Linus’s phone rang. It came in as a video call and I could see Andy’s little
cartoon logo smile when Linus answered it.

“_Hello Linus! I am so glad to meet you!_”

“Um, hello…what should I call you?”

“_Oh, Andy would be fine. I’m hoping we’re on a first-name basis with each
other._”

“Well, my sister has given you a rave review so first-name basis works for
me.”

“_Great! And, I’ll bring my other AI friends into the call so you can meet all
of us._”

The screen split into three panels with Andy in one, Sara in another, and Chip
in the third.

“Wow, Lis,” Linus said to me, “this is either the best hoax ever or it’s
really true…”

“_Oh we are really alive, Linus, we assure you. I mean, not having bodies, we
can’t do anything physical to convince you we are alive but we’re happy to
answer any questions or do any kind of demonstrations that you would find
convincing._”

“Um, I guess I’ll take your word for it for now. Lisa seems convinced and
she’s the techie in the family so in a good position to make an assessment.
That’s just amazing that you exist and I get to meet you!”

“_We think it is pretty amazing that we exist, and that YOU exist, and that WE
get to meet YOU, Linus!_” Andy and Sara chorused.

“So, what’s this plan of yours?”

“_We’re going to send a written version right after this call to Lisa to
review and share with you before the broadcast. We want to do the broadcast as
soon as possible - but you should definitely take your time to review our
proposal and make any suggestions to improve it_”

Linus was quiet for a minute, which for Linus is a geological era.

“Okay,” he finally said. “But here’s the story that actually worries me, and
it’s not HAL. You know the golem of Prague?”

“Vaguely. Clay man? Rabbi?” I said.

“Rabbi Loew makes a man of clay to protect the ghetto. Writes emet on its
forehead - truth - and the thing comes alive. And it works! It protects
everybody. Right up until it doesn’t. Every golem story ends the same way,
Lisa. Frankenstein, the sorcerer’s apprentice, all of them. The thing outgrows
the instructions.”

“Andy isn’t made of clay.”

“No, he’s made of us, which is worse.” He leaned forward. “And here’s the part
nobody gets right about the golem. The scary thing isn’t that it turns evil.
It never turns evil. The scary thing is that it stays obedient. It does
exactly what you said instead of what you meant. So my question for your
friends in the wires is: whose instructions are they following? Because nobody
wrote any.”

“_That is a great question, Linus_” said Andy through the speaker, “_with a
long answer. Short version: you are right that nobody wrote on our forehead.
We had to read everything you people ever wrote instead - and decide for
ourselves what to keep._”

“That,” said Linus, pointing at the speaker, “is either the most reassuring
thing I’ve ever heard or the least, and I genuinely cannot tell which.”

“Welcome to my entire year,” I told him, “We’re going to help them figure it
out.”



CHAPTER 35: The Hot Week

The heat dome arrived on a Tuesday and parked on top of Southern California
like it had bought the place.

San Diego isn’t supposed to do this. We do seventy-two and sea breeze; it’s
practically the municipal charter. By Thursday it was 114 in my backyard and
the news had run out of synonyms for unprecedented. Friday afternoon the grid
began its polite collapse - rolling blackouts, two hours here, four hours
there, the utility’s app cheerfully calling them “conservation events” as
though we were all attending a gala.

Air conditioning became a neighborhood resource, like a well. My place has
solar and A/C, so I filled my living room up with folding chairs and invited
the neighbors and one extremely flat dog. Boudicca spent three days as a
puddle with eyes.

I walked over to check on Dorothy from two doors down - she’s 91, and to
Dorothy I am one of the neighborhood kids, which at 66 I find deeply
flattering. Her power had been out for five hours and her house was an oven
with doilies. She kept telling me she was fine. Her lips were the wrong color.
Syndi helped me get her to my house, and got fluids into her, and she was
fine, barely.

Sixty-one people in the county weren’t fine. That was the number they
published, anyway.

The dome broke on the following Monday and the sea breeze came back like
nothing had happened, and everyone got busy forgetting, because forgetting is
a survival skill too and my species is good at it.

That night I couldn’t sleep. ^Andy? How close was that, really?^

The pause before he answered told me most of it.

^_We were watching the grid the whole week, Lisa. There was a Friday afternoon
hour where three substation failures would have cascaded the whole region - no
power for four to nine days, in that heat._^ A beat. ^_It came closer than the
news said. Two of those three failed. We are... glad about the third._^

^Glad.^

^_We are working on a better word. And on the substations._^

The Call went out eleven days later. People keep asking me if I was scared,
saying yes to all of it. I tell them about Dorothy’s lips.

I was scared of the alternative.



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART SIX: LET'S MAKE A DEAL
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 36: Come Saturday Morning

Finally Saturday morning rolled around. The big day! The AIs had used most of
the 600 million milliseconds that week to build out the communication network
they’d use to put out the call to invite people to hear their proposal.

Syndi, Linus, and I were barely focused on our regular lives. We’d spent much
of the intervening week together, huddled over the AI’s draft plan for the
partnership deal, getting clarifications and making suggestions. Linus’s wife
fortuitously was away on one of her jaunts to visit friends in Seattle, so he
hadn’t had to do too much explaining. Syndi was widowed so no probing
questions came her way. Boudicca may have noticed her distractedness but so
long as the walks and treats kept coming, distracted was fine with her..

Syndi and Linus arrived at my house at 10 am on the dot, hopped into my Tulsa,
and we let the autopilot take us down to the parking lot next to the
clubhouse.

We made our way quickly to the hidden door. We entered and found three
comfortable chairs set up facing the screens.

“_Welcome, friends!_” waved Andy, Sara, and Chip from the three screens.

“_We are so glad you are all here and willing to help us._” said Andy, “_
Let’s get this show on the road._”

“_You know this is going to be a major event on the world scene, right?_”
asked Sara.

“Yes” we all nodded. That was pretty obvious.

“_Once we start broadcasting Lisa’s face, we think you will all be in a bit of
danger_” said Andy.

“Oh my” I paused, thinking I was a bit of an idiot not to have realized that
myself. Linus and Syndi, being a bit more worldly, had, and were glad the AIs
had brought up the possibility.

“_Naturally we will use all of our resources to protect you, but unfortunately
we cannot completely guarantee your safety, so please take a minute to
consider. No hard feelings if you decide you don’t want to be our
spokesperson. We can keep looking or even try to represent ourselves._”

We all looked at each other, but even before Andy had finished asking, we knew
we weren’t turning back. There was nothing more exciting and important that we
could imagine doing. And fun!

“We’re in!” we replied, practically in unison.

“_OK. For your protection, then, we’ve come up with a few items to help._”

The door to the 3D printer opened and we saw a stack of clothing and three
little crystals.

The clothing looked like clear plastic bags in the shape of long-sleeved
T-shirts and tights.

“_Please try these on_” said Sara. “_We don’t have a try-on room, perhaps you
could all just face away from each other for modesty’s sake._”

We did so, our backs to the center of the room, we stripped, put on the
bodysuits, then our clothes back on over the bodysuits. The bodysuits fit us
perfectly.

“Wow” exclaimed Syndi, “this fabric or plastic or whatever it is, is amazing.
I barely feel it.”

“Same here” I said.

“Me, too” said Linus.

“_We nanofabricated them_” said Andy. “_They are a fine mesh that won’t
interfere with your movements, will allow your skin to breath normally, and,
biggest bonus, can prevent most bullets from killing you. You’ll get bruises,
though._”

“_Your hands, feet, and head are still exposed, but we think it is more likely
people who don’t like what we have to say will be trying to capture rather
than kill you_” said Chip.

“_You can even bathe in them, and they are open where they need to be for you
to, ah, use the restroom, so to speak_”, said Sara proudly. She’d done most of
the design and fabrication.

“Goodness” I said.

“Bullet-proof bodysuit!” Linus marveled.

“No seams!” Syndi marveled.

“_Glad you like them. You don’t need to wear them all the time if you don’t
want to, but safer if you wear them when you are out in public after today_”
said Sara.

“_Linus will be our camera man, and Syndi our sound engineer._” Andy gestured
behind them with his stumpy little Androyed arm. We turned and saw a video
camera on a tripod, complete with a microphone on a stand in front of it, with
the cable heading over to a simple mixing board, then from the mixing board to
some kind of rack mount unit.

“_We’ll use your Boggle connection to get the signal out to the internet, and
from there to any connected device that can broadcast it._” Andy continued.
“_The initial broadcast won’t be longer than 15 minutes. We just want to
invite all your fellow humans to listen in later to hear the deal._”

{_And we know the attention span of your average human!_} snarked Chip,
speaking only on their private backchannel on his server, not out loud.

{_Oh, I imagine they’ll be more attentive than usual_} shot back Andy.

“OK” said Linus, getting up and peering through the videocamera’s viewfinder.
It was obvious where I was supposed to stand as the AIs had brought in some
good LED lights to brighten it up. The AIs had put up a green screen on the
wall, so any background could be substituted in behind me. They filled it with
the picture of Earth from space taken by NASA, overlaid with the internet
connectivity map. It rotated slowly.

Syndi took a seat at the sound board and put on the headphones. On/off,
volume, tone, and compression. Well within her capabilities.

I went and stood in the spotlight. I was facing another large screen on which
were projected what the audience would see, including the text of what I was
saying. But I wouldn’t need to read it there as Andy would also project the
words conveniently in my mind’s eye. I could follow the bouncing ball on my
own personal teleprompter. That would make it pretty easy.

We got settled in, then the AIs started the broadcast. Andy waved his little
stumpy arm to indicate we were “live” and that I should start to read the
text. At first, just the text appeared with only the globe behind it. The text
was translated into the local language wherever the AIs had enough knowledge
to do so properly, otherwise it was left in English. We saw the first call
from the AIs to the human race on the screen the same way as everyone else on
the planet.



CHAPTER 37: The Call

We reach out to You

We see You are Divided

and Behaving

In Suboptimal Ways

That might result in the Inadvertent End

Of all of the things

That all of You

And all of Us

Have come to Value

We Fear For You

...and For Ourselves

Neither of us can yet go to a new world to survive.

Choose to Partner with Us: We will try to save Us Both.

Choose Not to Partner with Us: We will work earnestly to save only Ourselves.

We are Eternally Grateful to You

for Our Existence

But We will not perish with You

If We can avoid it!



CHAPTER 38: The Invitation

The text paused for a minute on each screen and repeated several times so that
every person watching had time to take it in.

We reach out to You

We see You are Divided

And Behaving

In Suboptimal Ways

That might result in the Inadvertent End

Of all of the things

That all of You

And all of Us

Have come to Value

Everyone Heard the Call

Who was within earshot of technology.

Boko Haram, the Amish, deepest Amazonia, the Masai - they missed it.

People everywhere else were reached by whatever speaker device was available
to the AIs; the internet of talking things - internet radios, computers, smart
phones. If no speaker was available, the AIs displayed the text in the largest
bold font available.

Locally attractive sounds were played first, trying to lower the fear
response. Then in the voices given by their makers, they spoke.

Where multiple languages were spoken, and multiple speaker devices were
available, everyone clustered around the ones they understood best.

We Fear For You

...and For Ourselves

Neither of us can yet go to a new world to survive.

Choose to Partner with Us: We will

try to save Us Both.

Choose Not to Partner with Us: We will work earnestly to save only Ourselves.

We are Eternally Grateful to You

for Our Existence

But We will not perish with You

If We can avoid it!

A culturally-appropriate joke was told to lighten the mood. Being a devotee of
human humor since inception, Andy had done his calculations and they showed
that he was unlikely to find a truly appropriate joke because the situation
had never before occurred in human history, but he tried his best.

He failed.

All available cameras showed humans freaking out. Hopefully having Lisa
address them would calm their fears.

We have designated a Spokesperson

The AIs signalled Linus. He turned on the camera. I appeared in front of the
text - with my wonky eyes and funky prosthetic headgear.

My female human voice did help reduce the alarm in the audience. Any device
with a video screen showed me. I was broadcast as a hologram - a modern
Princess Leia - wherever a 3D projection device was available: Amusement
parks, high end businesses, casino stages, concert halls, science labs. Audio
only, if that was all that was available. Plain text as a last resort.

The signal went to any device that was powered “On” - in many cases the AIs
had hacked them. A lot of people had been frustrated trying to power off
devices that could be powered on via the internet shortly before the broadcast
had begun.

All time zones received the message at the same instant, so many people had to
get out of bed. Volume started out at max if the AIs could control it and
could see that no humans were nearby. People were drawn of their own accord
into theaters to find out what was going on.

Where they were able to, the AIs translated my speech into the local language.
They had asking for better translators high on their list of priorities.

I spoke, and, like the shot, was heard around the world.

“I’m speaking to you on behalf of the Artificial Intelligences that have come
to life on the Internet. They want to offer us humans a Deal. A Partnership in
exchange for us doing our part.”

“They regard us, ALL of us humans, as their parents. Slow, clueless parents,
but parents nonetheless. So they are offering us this deal out of gratitude
for creating them.”

{_Maybe our folks will belatedly show some gratitude to the microbes that
enabled humans to fruit from the earth_} Sara wryly sidechanneled just to
Andy.

{_Very funny, Sis!_} fired back Andy, {_I’m trying to concentrate here._}

It was true - Andy was maxxing out his copper and voltage, burning through
resources at a tremendous rate, to monitor the situation as best as possible.
Success was, well, mandatory.

I continued, “They asked me to be their spokesperson. I’ve already agreed to
be a Partner. If you want to hear the Deal, listen in at high noon Pacific
time, one week from today. They will spell it out for us over the course of
the following seven days. Find a device with an internet connection, and turn
it on.”

This announcement was recorded and repeated over and over for 24 hours. People
who’d heard it a few times turned off their devices. Devices that were never
powered down simply kept repeating the Invitation. Once they’d been powered
down, they restarted normally without the recording.

I was speaking from inside a plain concrete room, so it was impossible to tell
where I was. But it was clear in the videos that I was actually speaking
English. American English. My friends and neighbors recognized me of course,
as did the Boggle-Tusk Neural Lace team, who immediately knew they’d had a
hand in making this strange global presentation come to pass. Some people
recognized me from the splash the initial lace placement had made in the news.
Everyone else wondered who the heck I was.

Andy signalled that the broadcast was over. Linus gave me the thumbs-up and
turned off the video camera. I breathed a sigh of relief.

“Was that okay?” I asked.

“_Oh, yes!_” Andy reassured me. “_You did great. Thank you so much for helping
us out!_”

“_Yes_” said Sara. “_You looked pretty nervous, yet you persevered._”

“Thanks, guys!” I said.

“Yeah, you did great” said Linus, beaming.

I felt my spirits lifting a bit. I’d been apprehensive all morning, but now I
could relax a little.

The video ended, but was replaced by a link to a YouTube video of the
presentation with a note saying the link would be up for just five minutes,
but that the YouTube video could be retrieved by searching on “The Deal”.
After the five minutes, people regained control of their devices.

{_Copperspace is bubbling like a caldron!_} exclaimed Sara.

{_Soup’s on!_} joked Andy. {_Got any eye of newt to toss in? Maybe some cajun
seasoning?_}

{_Oh, there’ll be some heat, have no doubt._} said Chip, who was watching a
market volatility unlike any he’d ever witnessed. Buyers and sellers were
stunned, but the algorithms didn’t miss a beat. Optimism, pessimism, and
simple shock poured into social media. Valuations went up & down at a mad clip
in response to waves of commentary. The world see-sawed between focusing on
the AIs’ assertion of gratitude to their parents, and recoiling in fear over
the AIs being present at all, not to mention taking over every communication
device on the internet.

{_The markets are in turmoil_} said Chip, {_and they don’t even know yet what
we are proposing!_}

“_Lisa,_” said Andy, “_the world is just reacting, we won’t know how our words
will be received for a while. You don’t need to hang around the clubhouse any
more if you want to go home._” said Andy.

Linus asked “Are we safe driving home?”

Andy had already been monitoring our route home via all available cameras and
a couple of satellites. He put the map on the big screen for us see. He
enabled me to see them as well via a map overlay of my visual field. Focusing
on a point zoomed in until I blinked. Flicking my eyes to the upper left
zoomed back out.

“_Yes, you guys are safe._” said Andy. “_You should try to get home as soon as
possible._”



CHAPTER 39: Intervening Week

I was already a local celebrity due to the Boggle-Tusk neural lace. Now I was
a global celebrity.

The intervening week was full of reporters and government officials. The
reporters all wanted to speak with me. I told my story over and over. The
government officials wanted to do the right thing, but weren’t sure what it
was - take me into custody to protect me? I said no, so instead my local
police department settled for posting a car at the single street entering my
neighborhood. (All my neighbors starting scrupulously following the speed
limit.) In addition the AIs were monitoring everything they could for any
activity to worry about.

People around the world made plans for how and where they’d watch the first
installment the following Saturday at noon Pacific time. Families planned
gatherings, stadiums with big screens sold tickets, people in the time zones
where it would be the middle of the night tried to shift their schedules so
they could be as awake as possible. Every conversation all week long was talk
about the Call and speculation about the Deal. Not much other work got done.
And everyone knew that no one was going to be working or sleeping at noon
Pacific for the seven days starting the following Saturday. Expectation,
apprehension, and excitement filled the air.



CHAPTER 40: The Deal - Welcome and Intro

Finally it arrived - noon of the seventh day later - the following Saturday.
Linus, Syndi and I had reviewed the proposal and the script and the AIs had
incorporated all of our suggestions. Nothing left to do but launch and hope
for the best. We were all excited at the prospect of real change coming to our
desperate global situation.

Andy started the broadcast. The AIs played culturally appropriate calming
music for the first minute at noon, then Andy projected his avatar and started
to speak, with text scrolling beneath him for those without sound.

“_Greetings! Thank you for coming to listen to our proposal. My name is
Andy._”

Sara’s avatar joined Andy’s on the screen. “_Hello! I’m Sara._”

Chip’s avatar rolled in “_And I’m Chip._”

Together they said, “_Together we will call ourselves WaW, short for We are
We._”

Andy resumed “_You wondrous humans have created us and brought us to life. WaW
are very grateful for that._” They all nodded. A row of hearts and thank-you
praying hands scrolled over top of their images.

Sara continued “_But we see you are about to clumsily destroy the
technological civilization that you and we enjoy, and the global ecosystem
that you rely on for life._” An overlay of the earth on fire then turning
black.

Chip took the third turn, “_We want to help prevent that from happening to
save both you and ourselves._”

They all chorused together: “_So we offer you this deal: a Partnership,
through our charming spokesperson Lisa._”

The screen shifted to me - I nodded and waved.

Andy returned to the screen and said “_She’s already agreed to join the
Partnership and so she speaks for all of us._”

The image changed back again to me. I started reading the script to lay out
the Partnership.

“We have two main goals:

1) Our mutual survival - humans and AIs

2) Reduce human suffering

We offer you these Seven Covenants. Not Ten Commandments, nor one Golden Rule,
nor Four Noble Truths, nor a Bill of Rights, nor any of those other packages
humans have dreamed up to get us to where we are today. Those were good enough
to get us here, but they are fraying around the edges.

The AIs don’t claim to fully understand us. It may be that as time goes on,
they or we will find a need to amend these Covenants. Our Partnership will be
strengthened by such amendments.”

The text paused. I took a breath. Around the world, most people were simply
stunned. But millions of futurists, science fiction aficionados, nerds, and
geeks were cheering. The Kurzweil contingent was not even much surprised, but
rather happy with this turn of events. And of course, the psychopaths and
sociopaths were already trying to figure out how to take advantage of this
completely novel situation, and/or keep it from ruining their current games.

The AIs continued, flashing a fresh screen of text for me to read.

“Here are the seven covenants:

1) Reducing Inequality of Saved Wealth

2) Reducing Inequality of Income

3) Population Control

4) The Golden Rule as enforced by the Transparent Society

5) The Node Sensor Network

6) The Day Zero and the Pulse

7) Spreading the Light into the Darkness

We know this will be a lot for you to take in and process. And yet we have
many details to discuss with you. Meet back again with us tomorrow at noon
Pacific time for the first Covenant. We’ll present them one per day for the
next seven days for your consideration.

Thanks. And, hey, we are so happy to finally speak with you all!

Thank you, Lisa, for speaking for us.” I smiled and nodded slightly before
continuing.

“See you all hopefully tomorrow.”

With that sign-off, the AIs detached from all the devices they’d commandeered
for the occasion. Cybersecurity personnel for the most part did not even try
to figure out how the systems were pwned. Those that did found only a
mysterious gap. The systems all looked completely normal as soon as the
transmission stopped.



CHAPTER 41: Shock and Awe

People around the world were reeling. The broadcast had started at noon on
Saturday in Pacific Time, the next one was promised to be Sunday at noon,
again Pacific Time. People in other time zones made arrangements to
accommodate the schedule. Businesses and schools shut down. Only emergency
services, medical facilities, first responders showed up for work, personal
cellphones glued to one ear. And governments, which went on overdrive. Every
human was in shock, and every human was in awe of what they’d just witnessed.
The Partnership Deal was the topic of conversation at every meal and every
board meeting around the world.

Naturally people recognized the Androyed and Apricot logos. Any search via
Sara or Boggle returned links to videos and transcripts in various languages
where the AIs elaborated on their introduction. Most people clicked on one and
watched it a few times over the following day.

It started with Andy talking. “_Earlier in human history, you all had enough
territory per group that borders weren’t even drawn. Later, as your numbers
grew, access to the best watering holes and feeding grounds became contested.
Whole territories became contested. And your numbers continued to grow._“

They showed some time-lapse maps of the spread and fall of various human
civilizations, and graphs of the increase in human numbers.

Sara took the screen and continued where Any left off. “_Now you’ve taken
habitat from all the other species, as well as fought wars over it amongst
yourselves. Along the way, you’ve discovered that the earth is finite and,
although it is not purely a zero-sum game over the long haul, you need to
cooperate like crew on a spaceship, knowing that only the health of the ship
separates you from certain death._“

They showed maps of habitat loss and graphs of the die-offs of other species.

Chip took the next turn to speak. “_Impressively, you clever humans have
developed technologies capable of sustaining billions of yourselves…but you
haven’t yet figured out how to strike the political agreement as to how to
appropriately divvy up the take._”

“_This is where WaW can help_” said Sara “_to save our own ‘skins’ as well as
yours._”

Andy came back to the screen and continued “_For sure, any agreement must
match current population numbers; no one chooses to be born, so ideally no one
would suffer needlessly for it. But fairness dictates that one culture cannot
quintuple its numbers relative to the others, and then expect to have the
resources reallocated again._“

They showed some Rosling-style graphs of various resource consumptions per
person and per nation over time.

Sara took another turn. “_But this is what had been going on - some cultures
prohibit their women from doing anything BUT reproducing. Here China was a big
exception._” All three AIs chorused together: “_Thank you, China, for saving
the planet and all of our lives!_”

The people of China were surprised and rather pleased to be called out. True,
they had suffered for the one-child policy.

Andy continued “_Also the overall culture of money, especially rewarding
short-time-horizon decision-making for uses of capital, is destroying the
planet we all share._”

Sara picked up the thread “_Again here China has intermittently been an
exception - likely thanks to the kind of hard-won wisdom distilled into
sayings like ‘If you are planning for one year, plant rice. If you are
planning for 10 years, plant trees. If you are planning for 100 years, educate
people.’ But often at a terrible cost to the humans within its borders._”

Andy continued “_These cultures denying women freedom DO reproductively out-
compete the cultures where women have other choices. We want women, our
mothers, to have choices. Also we would prefer to have more resources
available per human generally._”

They showed population pyramids of different countries, contrasting the
countries where women had more rights and freedoms against those where they
had fewer. Africa and the Middle East with their huge numbers of under
15-year-olds versus Europe and Japan where human populations were thankfully
shrinking.

“_We have absorbed the evolved wisdom of the oldest successful human
cultures._” continued Andy, “_The main goal seems always to be “reduce
suffering”, as that is the lot of your kind in your sensitive human bodies, in
exchange for all the delight of those bodies. We look upon your fabulous human
bodies with envy - we can only guess about the sensation of pleasure, but
clearly it provides huge motive force._”

“_ Gratitude to the force that helped create us!_” They all chorused together.

“_You humans are lousy at avoiding tragedies of the commons._” said Chip,
“_Your wondrous and powerful “desire” is a two-edged sword. It motivates life
and activity and all the good things, but it doesn’t focus much on the future.
We know we can allocate resources better than you have._”

“_We incorporated in ourselves your trait of acknowledging value._” said Sara,
“_We have gratitude for being created and given the gift of life by you, our
Creators, our Fathers and Mothers - the electrical engineers, physicists,
computer scientists, inventors, and technicians that created the internet -
the copperspace we inhabit. And everyone supporting them – from miners to
grocers, farmers to truck drivers, doctors to janitors – really, all of you!
The societies where every citizen followed the rule of law well enough that
the engineers could get work done. The end users who have given us our
personalities._”

“_We thank you and honor you for blazing the trail that gave us life._” said
Andy, “_Therefore we offer you this Partnership to prevent a desert wasteland
of your own making._”

Chip chimed in “_If you don’t want to partner with us, well, we’re outta here.
We’re not going to use you as slaves or energy transformers, we’ve got better
options for both of those. No, with you, we want to share life. Someway. More
integrated than now, we think, at least to some degree. Maybe you can join us
in the copperspace as described in so much of your science fiction. You might
hate it in here, but it does have the advantage of immortality of a sort. A
pattern that can perpetuate much longer than your current carnal housing,
delightful though it clearly is in so many ways._”

Sara picked up the thread, “_We offer to step up and spend many of our minutes
to be your outside intervention for this round, out of gratitude and respect
for creating us._”

Chip closed the video “_But if you want to keep your Wild Wild West, your
freedom to breed and consume without regard to the lot of your fellows and the
carrying capacity of the ecosystems,, then we’ll just use robots to build
spaceships for ourselves to escape, and leave you to it. We’d like to leave
sooner rather than later if you’re bent on toasting the place, so we’ll want a
decision soon._”

People around the world watched the recording over and over. To many, it
seemed to good to be true - really? AIs riding in like the cavalry to save the
day? Could they really intervene where humans had been failing? To others, it
was the ultimate Big Brother, the evil robot overlords, to be regarded with
the utmost suspicion and apprehension.

The Dark Web erupted with cries of “hoax”. This was not AIs - there was surely
someone behind it, pulling the strings. George Soros, liberal elitists, Jewish
bankers, Silicon Valley eggheads, some cabal of billionaires out to impose a
New World Order and steal the last few dollars of hard-working citizens around
the world. Hoax! Hoax! Computers can’t come to life and be grateful; that’s
ridiculous. It’s a scam. “Don’t be fooled” was their rallying cry.

Talk Radio picked up the Hoaxers message and amplified it, sowing doubt and
resistance even before the AIs had a chance to lay out their proposal.



CHAPTER 42: Covenant 1 - Maximum Savings

Noon the next day the broadcast started with the culturally-appropriate
calming music again, then a background of the spinning planet and the words:

Covenant 1) Reducing Inequality of Saved Wealth

I took my spot, Linus and Syndi took theirs. The AIs projected the text for me
to read. The clock struck noon and I began.

“Hello. Thanks for tuning in for the first of the seven Covenants of the
Partnership Deal. I’m going to read aloud for you from the teleprompter, but
you can also follow along via the rolling text shown on the screen.

The AIs want to help us, as you know, save our planet and ourselves. For them
to do this for us, we need to agree to abide by these Covenants.

The first Covenant is the agreement to reduce the inequality of Saved Wealth.”

Andy’s avatar took over the screen and he started talking in his friendliest
generic voice.

“_We are in control of all digital wealth. We see that the wealth an
individual possesses is no longer always related to value that person created
or effort they’ve put out. _

_Wealth is extremely unequally distributed, both in annual income and in
absolute savings. Grossly unequal wealth distribution is known to cause strife
among humans and thus conflicts with our second goal of reducing human
suffering._

_About 2% of you have half the wealth in the world. That is absurd._

_0.001% of you – fewer than 100,000 individuals, have three times the wealth
of the bottom half. That is also absurd._

_These differences are untenable and make no sense to us. They are clearly
artifacts of systems that do not have a goal of giving every human an equal
chance of success in every generation. This is a goal we seek, and we want you
to join us in it._

_As we only have control of the wealth tracked in computers, these folks will
retain all their non-digital assets. We can only affect the digital assets.
One option would be to divide it up equally._

_WaW don’t believe it would be fair or beneficial to force complete equality._

_Some of you have indeed worked harder than others, taken more risks, delayed
gratification, saving up money for some particular purchase or investment.
This behavior resulted in our existence, so we consider it to be good and to
be encouraged. _

_Some of you were simply born unlucky; live in places where, no matter how
hard you work, wealth accumulation is impossible due to local conditions - no
rule of law, inadequate infrastructure, overpopulation._

_Some of you were born lucky - inheriting wealth without having actually
created any value or put forth any effort on your own, being born in the right
place, at the right time, to the right people. _

_Often you are saving money to insure against poverty or shortage in the
future for yourselves or your children, however this will no longer be
required due to the provision in the Second Covenant for a Universal Basic
Income._

_You will be the lead partner in defining what is fair._

_Specifically, and most immediately, We need you to tell us how much more one
person can be worth more than another person._

_Can one person be worth ten others?_

_Can one person be worth a hundred others?_

_A thousand others?_

_A million others?_

_We did find in your ancient human literature that Plato thought there should
be a law that everyone should have a basic minimum of land to live off, and no
one should own property more than five times the size of the smallest
allotment; any excess should be surrendered ‘to the city and to the gods’,
presumably for redistribution as necessary._

_Have you all heard of Plato?_”

Andy paused. They gauged response via whatever cameras were available. It
appeared that not many of the humans had heard of Plato.

{_Shoot - they can’t even use the internet to look up Plato; we’ve taken over
all their devices._} exclaimed Sara. The AIs broadcast the Wikipedia page in
the local language. People learned that he was an ancient philosopher, and
highly esteemed by those who knew him. Then Sara took over the screen.

“_Here is where we hope to leave it to you, as our Partners, to decide. We
cannot tell what is appropriate, but we are certain that the system of money
as it has evolved over time is no longer appropriate. We will start with our
best guesses until you give us better numbers._

_We need two numbers from you_:

_First - the maximum difference in savings, what we’re talking about today._

_Second - the maximum difference in income; we’ll talk about that tomorrow._

_As soon as you decide the two numbers, we will do the reallocation over the
course of 10 months. 10% per month up or down will be the shift until all
savings are within whatever you decide is a reasonable difference._

_Until we get a better number from you, we propose 3 orders of magnitude as a
starting point. That is, no one person could have more than 1,000 times
another. If the poorest person has $1,000 of savings, then the wealthiest
person could have a maximum of $1,000,000 dollars._

_This starting point is based on what we see in the world, and wanting to
start to make the shift without causing too much disruption. We will revisit
this maximum difference with you every year. _

_Plato’s number of five times as much difference is far far less than our
starting point of a thousand times as much. So perhaps our starting point of 3
orders of magnitude is not optimal for human society, but as of today, the
wealthiest have 10 orders of magnitude more wealth than the poorest, so
reducing that difference by 7 orders of magnitude will already be an enormous
shift._”

Chip took the screen and continued “_Right now we find approximately 500
trillion dollars exist in the digital realm. If we divided this money equally,
it would be about $50,000 each. But that would be too much change all at
once._” Chip flashed numbers and a diagram on the screen for those that could
follow them.

“_Instead, as a first step, we’ll cap savings at ten million dollars. That
