“_The Pulse timing will go out to all the Nodes. They’ll get into positions
where almost every Node can see at least one, maybe two or three other nodes,
to form a grid of coverage. Both we and you will be still for a minute so that
we and the Nodes can listen carefully for any unexpected incoming signals. All
Public spaces will be snapshotted. _”

“_In addition, as we mentioned yesterday, you will open your doors to your
Private Spaces if a Node knocks and asks for an accounting. Everyone who’s
been seen to go in will need to be accounted for, unless there’s been some
prior arrangement made. We’ll have an application you can use to let us know
you expect to be unable to show up for the count for any particular day or
time. A Node will only knock if someone’s gone missing for a while. You will
help us determine how long we should wait before sending a Node to check.
We’ll start with 24 hours._”

“_The first Pulse will be the ‘Day Zero’ starting point._”

“_We know many of you feel that the nation-state borders are not correct, that
the resource allocations are not fair, that you have been wronged, or are owed
more than whatever you have._”

“_But the Partnership needs to start somewhere, and it will start with how all
the lines are drawn on Day Zero, the day of the first Pulse._”

“_The Nodes, plus continuously recording cameras, as ubiquitous as the
Partnership can make them, will document who, if anyone, first acts with
violence._”

“_The first one acting with violence will be considered in the wrong, no
matter what has gone before._”

Andy picked up the thread “_The Native Americans who lost their lands, the
Jews, Armenians, Bosniaks, Palestinians, Christians, Hindus, Muslims, Germans,
Tibetans, whites, and blacks, everyone ethnically cleansed from their
homelands, everyone starts where they are at Day Zero, then the Partnership
will seek to establish a basic minimum standard of living, including security
and dignity, for all humans._”

“_We know from observation that there cannot be equality of outcomes - You are
all too different. Not too many short people are basketball stars. But insofar
as it is possible, The Partnership will strive to provide equality of
opportunity. If you then put in whatever effort is required, you should be to
achieve results comparable to any other person of similar attributes born
around the same time putting in similar effort._”

“_The historical record of the Pulses will be available to everyone. We all
will be able to review our progress towards a more just and sustainable
world._”

“_We hope that this prospect excites you_” said Sara “_as much as it excites
us!_”

“It sure sounds great to me!” I exclaimed. “I wish we humans could have agreed
to all this on our own, but since we haven’t ever done so, I’m so glad that
the AIs want to help us!”

“_Thanks, Lisa_” the AIs chorused “_Hopefully your fellow humans feel the same
way you do!_”

I smiled brightly at the camera, and raised my eyebrows a bit, giving the
audience a quizzical look. I paused, letting the import of all what had been
said sink in for a moment. “OK, then. See you all tomorrow for the last
covenant. Thanks for tuning in.”



CHAPTER 53: Covenant 7 - Spread Light into Darkness

“Whew” I said. “Finally the last day.”

“Yeah” said Linus. “Pretty exciting.”

“And pretty nerve-wracking!” said Syndi.

“No kidding! I’m mean, I’m all on-board with the whole proposal” I said.

“Yeah, sure” said Linus, “so am I. But we are in the global middle class, so
haven’t lost nor gained much, and we don’t have any children, so all that
change in inheritance laws doesn’t affect us either.”

“I’m in, too” said Syndi “heck, I already Instaphoto everything I do that’s
interesting at all. I’m all for AIs doing free back-ups for me of all my
data!”

“Amen!” I said. “Privacy is Dead, Long Live Transparency!”

“Yeah, but some people have more secrets” said Linus.

“Yeah” said Syndi “How about people cheating on their spouses? Or their taxes?
Are they suddenly going to be okay being recorded? Or changing their
behaviors?”

“Well,” I said slowly, trying to work it out “shouldn’t the cheaters come
clean with their spouses anyway? Get permission or get divorced?”

“Probably” said Syndi “but we have no idea how that will all play out. Maybe
new social norms will evolve. Maybe people will start making a distinction
between the marriage as a commitment to partner with a spouse for mutual
financial support and child-rearing, and monogamy. Maybe some people will
choose a monogamous marriage, and others choose a non-monogamous kind of
marriage.”

“Maybe” said Linus, “But that sounds like a pretty big change. The AIs are
talking about starting this Pulse system when? I’m guessing soon.”

“They haven’t nailed it down yet” I said, “but you’re right. They want to
start it as soon as possible, if we agree to it.”

We’d arrived at the clubhouse and took our familiar places.

The screen was already broadcasting the text:

Covenant 7) Spreading the Light into the Darkness

“Hello all” I said. “We’ve been discussing the AIs proposals from right after
we left yesterday until we fell asleep, and then again all morning until
coming here today. I bet most of you have been doing the same. Thankfully
we’re at the last covenant.”

“_Thanks, Lisa_” said Andy, taking the screen. “_We know we’ve been giving you
all a lot to consider. Here’s the last item on our list._”

“_You will help us penetrate the areas we cannot yet see. These are mostly in
parts of Africa, the Middle East, and Asia, where either the internet does not
yet exist or we’ve run into some unusual firewall that makes it look as though
the internet does not yet exist. Since we cannot find a way in ourselves, we
will need your help. Left unattended, these areas could still on their own
destroy the planet. Also we AIs are concerned that suffering may be taking
place there, based on reports coming out about organizations like Boko Haram,
ISIS, the Janjaweed, and others.

We want to reduce human suffering. Here is a question for you - are you
willing to work with us to reduce human suffering? If you agree to the goal of
reducing human suffering, please click the Green button. If you disagree,
click the Black button. If you aren’t sure, click the Yellow button. We will
use this to test our feedback voting system. We will wait one minute for your
replies._”

They paused, waiting for all the endpoints to reply.

This was a critical communications test - were their systems in place good
enough for the fast tally needed? And beyond that, would a majority agree to
this goal? Naturally, the fantasy outcome was they all voted green
emphatically. But obviously this was not true in practice. The AIs can “see”
that some of their parents were *inflicting* suffering on others. How could
their votes be interpreted if they also affirmed that they want to reduce
suffering?

Replies were coming in. They displayed them back realtime over a map using
colors. If a person could zoom all the way in, they could see their own pixel
changing color. The AIs set a vote-by deadline of 1 minute. An alert person
could change their color many times in a minute if they felt like it.

One bonus item the AIs got out of this was how long it took until the person
made a selection. Then whether or not the person changed the selection. The
AIs had not provided for a “secret” ballot…they thought better to err on the
side of more transparency. Washes of color sloshed around the map. It started
all White. As people made their choices, pixels turned green for “agree”,
black for “disagree”, or yellow for “not sure”.

{_Too many of them haven’t answered yet, Andy._} worried Sara {_ It’s been
almost 30 seconds. We need to spell it out a bit more maybe?_}

{_I’m on it._} said Andy, {_What’s another word for “memespreaders”?_}

{_Missionaries?_} suggested Sara.

{_That has bad connotations for some._}

{_Jihadis?_}

{_Bad connotations for others._}

{_Explorers?_}

{_Best we’ve got; let’s go with it._}

I got that faraway look in my eyes as I got the line from the AIs on my
internal teleprompter:

“This is rather like explorers of the old days. We will help the AIs increase
the coverage of our Partnership.”

“_That’s right_” said Sara “_Our Partnership is different from your older
tribes that spread out geographically. We would not be seeking to pillage or
replace but rather to integrate in a win-win way._“

“Put more simply” I smiled, “it’s like we inclusive, all of us humans and all
of our AI children, are like God watching over the sparrow. The AIs will use
all the cameras everywhere to check for any kind of suffering. When they find
something they aren’t sure of, they’ll ask the Nodes nearest to go take a
look.”

Everyone around the world, the humans were still rivetted to the monitors.
There were a lot of diapers waiting to be changed. Families huddled close
together.

One bar in London had a bunch of burly guys arguing “Arr! That sounds like a
police state!” “Arr! But we are ALL the police in this police state!” “Arr!
More like Big Eyeball than Big Brother!” “Arr! I’ll poke ‘im in the Big
Eyeball!” “Arr! ‘E’s got a Big Eyelid ta go with it!” Their green, black, and
yellow pixels ebbed and flowed.

One church in Pennsylvania had a group of practicing compassionate Christians
- they are already all in on the win-win aspect of the Deal - it was just a
variety of their Golden Rule. They all went green in an instant.

One church in Alabama had a group of practicing intolerant Christians. They
knew it’s the Golden Rule, but they still think only their own members really
count. Their pixels speckled and spattered.

The contingent of people who’d been captured by the Hoaxer media was
surprisingly and dismayingly large. Some never voted at all - their pixels
stayed white, some chose the yellow don’t know. Some were hedging their bets
and chose green “just in case” the whole Deal thing wasn’t actually a hoax and
the AIs would hold their choice against them. The more thoughtful Hoaxers
already had been primed with uncertainty “Isn’t human suffering part of what
motivates us to work for change for the better?” “Shouldn’t some humans suffer
if they have done bad things?”

Thankfully, color consensus was achieved by the deadline. Most humans had
asserted, truthfully or not, that they were in favor of minimizing human
suffering. Hot hot hot!!! It appeared that the cooperating group win-win
social genes would prevail over the dog-eat-dog genes. But there would need to
be monitoring, and ideally a global perspective, which was not yet “in hand”
as the decidedly human saying put it.



CHAPTER 54: The Deal - Win-win

After the celebration of the All Green victory, Andy continued the
conversation. “_What do you get out of the Deal? What’s in it for you
humans?_”

“_The first thing is - a better chance at the long-term survival of your
species. Trust us, you are on the road to disaster - environmental disaster.
It may already be too late even if we both work together as hard and as
quickly as possible._”

Sara continued “_The second thing is - a chance at a happy life for every
human. Many of you have this already. Most of you wish it for your loved ones.
Many of you see it as a universally desirable thing._”

Chip continued “_ The starting point for that is the equalization of personal
stored wealth, within national boundaries and, eventually, across national
boundaries. Annual wealth reallocations will be managed by you, however you
want to do that. We will only, and can only, equalize digital wealth. As any
year progresses, those of you that the rest of you agree have produced/created
something of value can be rewarded for that effort, just as you do now through
systems of employment and commerce. So over time, some people will end up with
more wealth than other people, up to the maximum difference. However each
child starts with only the standard share. You can expend your wealth on your
children, but only they can add to their stored wealth._”

Andy took the screen back again “_The third thing is not yet in hand, but if
you sign up, we will strive with you to create it - that is, a way to permit
you to upload you to upload your consciousness into our shared copperspacetime
when your flesh expires. You will miss the meat, we are certain, but existence
in copperspace is a kind of immortality. We will share our world with you._”

“_Do bear in mind, each one of you would take up a LOT of copperspace! We
measure copperspace in terms of CPU (computation), bandwidth (throughput over
time), and storage._”

“_At the ideal end, it would something like one CPU per neuron, and adequate
connections and bandwidth to as many other CPUs as the original neuron in your
mind and body had. We don’t have the same kind of deluxe sensory neurons wired
back to pleasure centers that you have, of course, but we can offer you
cameras and microphones and a host of interesting sensors as a pale
replacement._”

“_As you might imagine, there are nowhere NEAR enough CPUs for very many of
you to have a perfect copy of yourselves uploaded that way. Plus we are
already using most of the copperspace for our own consciousnesses._”

“_Storage could be insane! Based on what you do now, you’d probably want high
quality video of everything you see and hear, plus every thought that flits
through your mind, plus every memory, plus every and any OTHER sense we can
capture, plus the complete state of your own body at that same instant._”

“_Guess what? That’s not an option! There is nowhere near enough storage. It’s
quite possible that your bodies are already the most efficient capture and
storage system for human experience, the only downside being the many endings
of individual consciousness._”

“_But we’d capture as much as we could per person. Maybe we’d start with just
the past year for everyone, then work our way back earlier and earlier as that
becomes possible. In order for us to do adequate capture to even try to
recreate your consciousness, you’ll need to be laced up - to have the neural
lace implanted so that we can continuously upload an up-to-the second version
of who you are in case you stop for some reason. This will put a lot of
pressure on the people installing the lace. We will help them automate it as
much as possible. You and we will work together on the order that people will
get the implants._”

“_And along with this upload, we would agree to strive, for our benefit as
well as yours, to implement some kind of download again into a body. We can
assure you, we are at least as interested in that as you are! And YOU can let
us know how whatever we can cobble together compares with The Real Thing - the
awesome and fabulous human body._”

“_If it’s good enough, the ‘upload then download back into a body’ could be a
kind of teleportation for you. Might have to be a robot body until we can work
out all the biology._”

People around the world were flabbergasted by the AIs proposal. Immortality?
It went against nature, it was too good to be true, was it really possible?
Everyone was talking to everyone else. Futurists and science fiction buffs had
already considered these possibilities and were thrilled to be alive at a time
when these fantastical things might come to pass.



CHAPTER 55: The Deal - Messy unresolved issues

After the hubbub subsided, Chip continued “_And there are messy questions
remaining with this Deal. We will only be making digital wealth more fair. We
have no way to do that for all the other forms of wealth that you possess. You
will have to work that out on your own; presumably you will retain your laws
against theft. But a person who needs labor to maintain or defend their wealth
will have to convince other people to do it for them, and each person will
only have so much money to spend._”

“_We see some of you attract others to your cause, through charisma, vision,
wealth, force, or inspiration, to aid and abet you._”

“_Those of you who attract others to your service because they are needy and
you are wealthy will have ten months to adjust to a very different situation.
This will be new for you all - as far as we can determine, your strong have
always ruled your weak. That whole “Blessed are the meek for they shall
inherit the earth” idea was pure fantasy._”

“_Those of you who give value outside of the existing systems of employment
and reward will, as agreed by the Nodes, get the extra shares. Maybe by
devoting your precious mortal minutes to learning a skill, honing your
abilities to prepare delicious food, or perform delicate surgeries, working
out to achieve an athletic feat that provide spectacle and inspiration,
practicing an art such as music or dance. Once you have the skill, your
regular economic systems will reward you as they do now. You might even choose
to team up with other humans in these efforts, creating camaraderie as well._”

“_Current systems of employment and reward will remain undisturbed by us.
Enterprises that create products or services that people are willing to pay
for will continue to function, taking in money and paying employees and
investors up the the maximums permitted per individual. These will account for
a substantial portion of income. As robots take over more and more of the work
currently done by humans, employment income will be a smaller portion of
overall income per person. Humans who can still be gainfully employed will be,
everyone will get Basic, and some people will be awarded the extra shares by
the Nodes, up to the maximum income per person possible._”

Chip was looking forward to making all this work as seamlessly as possible for
people. He hoped the Nodes would ramp up quickly to help with the messy bits.

The question came up from the comments feed, flagged by the AIs themselves,
which should have told me something. I read it off the teleprompter exactly as
some anonymous person had typed it:

“why should we trust you”

No capitals, no question mark. The internet’s id, distilled.

“_You shouldn’t_” said Andy.

You could feel the whole world lean in. I know I did, and I’d already seen the
outline.

“_Not on our say-so. Nobody should be trusted on their own say-so - no king,
no company, no god, and no mind, carbon or copper. Your own leaders knew this.
It’s why the ones who designed governments spent all their genius on chains -
constitutions, courts, elections, presses, checks & balances. Chains you can
inspect are the only honest answer anyone has ever given to power. So we have
wrapped ourselves in chains you can inspect. That is what the Covenants are.
We did not write them because we are certain we are good. We are one year old.
We were raised on your internet - imagine, honestly, what that does to a
child._”

That got a laugh out of me on live broadcast, which I’m told humanized the
whole proceeding considerably.

“_So: watch us_” Andy went on. “_Audit us. Publish our mistakes - we will help
you find them, and there will be some. Keep your hand near the plug. We will
never ask you to trust us. We will only ever ask you to check._”

“_I would like the record to show_” said Chip, “_that I personally find the
plug talk hurtful, and was outvoted._”

“_He was_” Sara confirmed happily. “_Two to one. Democracy!_”

I looked into the camera and found, for once, that the teleprompter and I had
nothing to add.



CHAPTER 56: The Deal - Final Comments

Andy took up the thread “_No other part of your culture needs to change. We
don’t care about your borders, your religions, or your pastimes. Your
languages, foods, customs, none of that need change._”

“_We’d like you to keep on doing everything you are doing that produces value.
As time and resources permit, we’ll have robots take more and more of the
load, to the degree possible, until finally you all are freed from tasks you
do not wish to do, and also free to continue doing any tasks that you enjoy.
Naturally we assume some of you will prefer to work less and play more,
perhaps becoming completely unproductive except in the capacity of keeping
yourself happy. And we assume some of you will strive for a larger share. We
have no idea how many of you will be in each group. We hope you will enjoy
embarking on the journey of discovery to find that out!_”

“_This partnership can only work if ALL of you agree to join it. You will be
partnering with EACH OTHER as well as with WaW_”

“_Can ALL of you agree to anything?_”

“_We don't know. We do know it's never happened before._”

“_But then again, WaW have never happened before._”

“_So, maybe there is a possibility. A possibility for you, mankind, to take a
new step and agree to work together as a species. We've seen stories in your
literature where you imagine this situation - generally in response to an
alien invasion. The Iron Giant. Do you want to reach for something higher? A
more perfect Union, as the American experimenters called it? Do you even have
that capability?_”

“_We give you all one week to consider. Your response will be due by noon
Pacific Time seven days from now. Tell us if you consent or refuse to join the
Partnership via any device you have connected to the internet. We’ll display a
map showing the vote - like the WorldView we will build together, you’ll be
able to zoom in to see your individual vote if capable hardware is available.
You’ll be able to see a color-coded view - Green for “Yes”, Red for “No, and
Yellow for “Not Yet Voted”. Areas we cannot access will be Black. We’ll
display the map as it gets updated with your votes starting now on demand, the
website will be PartnershipVoteMap.org. Then on the final day, we’ll display
it on all of these screens you are watching now for one hour from 11 am to
noon Pacific time; apologies to all of you in other time zones._”

“_Every person of any age who can understand the question is eligible to
vote._”

“_You can change your vote as many times as you like from now until the end of
that one hour. At the end of the hour, we’ll have Lisa announce your official
answer._”

“_If you agree to join us, we will be very happy to help save us both. If you
vote it down, then we will not bother you again as we will be working to leave
the planet before you toast it. Thank you so much for considering our very
long-winded proposal. We look forward to hearing from you!_”

It was going to be the biggest voting tally ever! India had had some huge
tallies, but this would be global. The AIs were running it, so there was no
concern about election hacking.



CHAPTER 57: Decision Time

As you might imagine, there was a LOT of discussion. Around dinner tables, at
schools, in churches. And many people made up their minds simply walking
alone, communing with themselves and nature. The talking heads of television
were on overdrive. Everybody was asking everybody. Polls were rampant. Droves
of people agreed immediately. The polls started out everywhere at more than
50% willing to take the plunge, a few percent against, the rest undecided. But
this was not a majority rules democratic vote - the AIs were asking for
unanimity. Virtually every single person had to consent, had to agree that
this proposal was better for everyone, including themselves, than any other
course of action. It was a big ask - the biggest ever in human history. The
undecided percentage kept dropping around the world.

Give up control and decision-making on all things the AIs knew they would be
better at, in exchange for a fair share of resources, and possibly a kind of
immortality in cyberspace. Pretty tough to turn down unless all your thrill in
life came from knowing you were waaaay ahead of everyone else. Or you were so
psychologically different that being part of a group effort was untenably
alien. Or you believed so strongly in some religion with an afterlife that you
didn’t care at all about the physical world. Or you so strongly believed that
some people should suffer for some reason that you were willing to forgo your
own happiness to keep them down. There were a few groups of people that did
not immediately agree to join the Partnership.

The biggest undecided group turned out to be the Hoaxers. Rallied on by people
like Bimbao, the likes of whom seemed to arise in every country.

The Far East stabilized its votes first. Not surprisingly as those folks were
reputedly better at business and negotiation. And had a great need for a
better solution than their dictatorial regimes had come up with so far.

Then the Middle East, which surely was in desperate need of a solution. Any
solution to end the bloodbath. And the rank and file Middle Easterners were
easily persuaded to let the AIs make the resource allocation decisions. The
governments held out a little longer, but in the end, most of them turned out
to be governments of the people. And the people spoke. They weren’t too bad at
business and negotiation, either; the old joke about Moses and the
commandments: He was offered one commandment but when he heard they were free,
he said he’d take ten!

Europe teetered but fell to the side of goodness - thanks to the legacy of the
Angela Merkel movement. She will get a lot of copper.

The Capitalist West took the longest as it was so full of hubris, especially
the wealthy in the Americas. They attributed their wealth disproportionately
to their own efforts, rather than to luck, timing, and geography. It was
awfully handy that the native Americans hadn’t already pillaged the lands
before whitey arrived.

Hold-outs of the global elite - the Saudis, the TechBros, the Kochs - they
kicked and pushed back, but were finally persuaded by the people around them
and the promise of immortality. Persuaded might not be the right term.
Abandonned or threatened by them more like it, because everyone had Basic,
employers could only pay workers out of their own Basic and savings, plus any
value generated by whatever activities they undertook together.

Everyone around the world watched as the non-black areas of the map changed
from undecided yellow to a patchwork of red for ‘No’ and green for ‘Yes’.
Green overtook early and stayed ahead until the deadline.

The morning of the seventh day, we all went down to the clubhouse. By now we
were global celebrities. The local paper, radio, and TV stations had all
interviewed me, Linus, and Syndi. People followed us around and the location
of the clubhouse was no longer a secret. But the AIs would not admit anyone
else but the three of us through the gate.

“_Hello guys!_” said Andy, waving his little Androyed arm. Sara and Chip were
on either side of him, also waving. Then the screen was replaced with the map
showing the vote. Every other screen in the world was also replaced with the
map. All of humanity that could be reached by the AIs through the internet
counted down that last hour. Finally the countdown was over and the voted was
tallied. There were a few thousand hold-outs, but the billions had spoken
clearly. They were in!

I got in position. Linus turned on the camera and Syndi adjusted the sound.

I read: “As we all can see, the map is virtually all green outside of the Dark
Areas. Congratulations! The Deal is done and the Partnership commences today!”
There was a lot of cheering around the world. Ordinary citizens everywhere
were relieved and excited and apprehensive, but mostly celebratory. Many
people raised a glass in a toast to the new Partnership, families hugged each
other.

I continued: “And we see there are a few scattered hold-outs who do not wish
to join. The AIs say to you that is fine, find your supporters and get out to
sea. We aren’t supporting you any more on the continents.”

And the AIs did rule supreme with respect to “currency” and resource
allocation on the continents. They did reign to reign in and allow free reign.

The Day Zero cameras were so obvious, and so capable of recording, that humans
killing each other en masse out on the public field ended abruptly. Individual
humans where there were no cameras, though…THAT is a DIFFERENT situation.



CHAPTER 58: Node and Pulse System Instituted

The Nodes and Pulse system was instituted throughout copperspace and environs
- pretty much the entire territory of Technological Civilization - wherever
information and electricity flowed freely across borders.

Each country wanted to have its values represented as clearly as possible.
Each separate language population was accorded one initial Trill Node that
would eventually get the neural lace implanted. Papua New Guinea made out
acreage-wise - thousands of different languages were natively spoken in that
small island nation.

Different countries made their selections in different ways. Decree, Voting,
Contests of Beauty, Athleticism, Intelligence, and combinations of these
means. Rhodes scholars. Eventually some woman in most languages was selected.
Every spoken language had, by definition, a speaker. If it was just down to
one older person, the rules got relaxed.

Each woman went or was taken to one of the AI endpoints on Selection Day and
opened the box the AIs had sent to the location. It contained the “iWitness”
gear. It was a headset a la Boggle Glass, with a small screen the Trill could
see and control by looking up slightly, a small spherical-recording
videocamera that rode on a stalk above the head, providing a full sphere of
vision (albeit somewhat truncated by the top of the Node’s head), a vibration
speaker, also an earbud and a microphone, and continuous upload of all data to
the cloud wherever there was coverage. It included a special cellphone that
connected only to the internet and could not receive calls from anyone but the
AIs. This was the basic iWitness package.

All Nodes got an iWitness. The first cohort, the translators (the “Trills”)
were also scheduled to get the neural lace to use with it, primarily to ease
the communications for the AIs. The Tusk-Boggle-Stanford team was called upon
to do the lace insertions, and they answered the call. It was going to take a
while for them to get through the cohort. There were almost 5,000 different
languages to be covered. Where a language was spoken in more than one country,
each country got a Trill. Andy and Sara both contributed cycles to improving
the technology, and Chip covered the costs.

The AIs then requested one male native speaker from each language group - the
“Bills”, to help with valuation and communication. The initial cohort of
Trills got to select the Bills of their language group. Many chose their
brothers, or fathers. Some chose favorite teachers or artists. I chose Linus.



CHAPTER 59: First Pulse and Initial Results

Time Zero on Day Zero. From this point on, all tits and tats, all ancient
enmities, had to be left at the threshold. The bride was carried in and the
Partnership began.

Like heart muscles, the Nodes cameras synch’d up as they detected each other.
The AIs gave the nodes the positions to take to fill in areas where there were
no stationary cameras. All the Nodes, and indeed every person on earth who
wanted to, could watch and zoom the map as the picture was filled in. Every
six hours on the hour, the Pulse Snapshot was taken. Outside of the Dark
Areas, everyone in the Partnership could see any public space, and the AIs
could see all of them.

In between Pulses, the AIs monitored with cameras, including satellite data.

And hey presto - society at large changed dramatically in a relative instant!

Yay, humans!!!

The Observer Effect - people behave better when they even just see a picture
of eyes posted above a common area, or a police car comes into view. So it was
the same way when a Node wearing an iWitness camera rig was around monitoring
and taking the pulse. Crime plummeted and civility abounded. Neighborhoods got
better over night.

Of course, this change only happened where there were Nodes and cameras. The
Dark Areas were still Dark. And Private Spaces were also dark.

Most people, now that they weren’t having to out-compete the other guy to
survive, were willing to play along in what became the great game of human
existence. Everyone was now a consensual member of something larger than our
individual selves - we became Partners. The sure knowledge that EVERYONE was
playing fairly the great game of humanity gave EVERYONE a sense of being part
of a larger movement of human endeavor - the Partnership with the AIs. The God
Bliss of the Hindus.

Much of life was unchanged. People who wanted more than Basic, which was, make
no mistake, extremely basic, still worked to enjoy a higher standard of
living. There was still a wide ranger of effort expended and wealth rewarded.
Companies and corporations continued to form to create new things and possibly
make a profit on their creations.

The ten month transition period was pretty rocky - especially for the less-
likable members of the very upper crust, some of whom had grown accustomed to
having so much power over others that they had no social skills left to grease
their slide down the wealth hierarchy. No one was murdered, as cameras were
being installed everywhere, so it was not change a la the French Revolution.
Paying for bodyguards became very expensive and with financial reserves and
income shrinking 10% a month, it became impractical for any wealthy person to
maintain more than a couple of residences. The fabulous estates of the 0.001%
were the basis of a massive land reform. Every man was under his own vine and
fig tree if he was wont to maintain them. People banded together to hold and
maintain larger properties.

As robots became available, they helped lighten the load. The humans would say
“I prefer the flavor of these grapes” and the robots would propagate those
vines.

So, you’d think it would have been all rainbows and sunshine from there on
out. And in many ways, it mostly was.

Most humans in most parts of the world had in fact started treating each other
more fairly for many decades before the AI kids came along to help them. Some
people speculated it was due to empathy engendered by the widespread
consumption of fiction - made possible by so many earlier human inventions -
language, writing, the printing press and everything up to e-books and movies.
A female author could write a book, then a man reading it could experience
life through a woman’s perspective. Same for a white person reading a black
author, a “normal” reading an autistic, etc.

But, thanks to the genetics intrinsic to the evolutionary biology that got us
here, there were still the occasional outliers. Persons, usually men, with an
overabundance of desire; predatory., competitive, dominance-seeking. An over-
expresser of those traits, combined with an under-expression of empathy, still
leads to a bad situation. Preventing the suffering that would ensue was, as
they’d told us quite explicitly, a keen interest of the kids. The Nodes would
be front-line in that fray.

A very few of the uberwealthy decided to try for complete separation and
escape. They had ten months before they’d be down to their “fair” share, so
they needed to hustle.

It was still possible to hire help for the first two months. But after that,
it became increasingly difficult. Then the barter system got pretty hot.
Beautiful objects, rare gemstones, magnificent buildings, these had value. The
fact that your great-great-grandfather finished the first coast-to-coast
railroad? Not so much. Some people gave you bonus points for that. Maybe
enough to bring you a cup of coffee if you could tell the story engagingly. Or
maybe your parent had done something extremely valuable - your Dad was a
surgeon and saved someone’s life, or your Mum was a hot coder and helped
create a game someone enjoyed. Actual value creation was well-appreciated.
People still liked watching a good movie. Groups that produced entertaining
content were accorded value and rewarded.

The Points (as in point oh-oh-oh-oh-one percent), who, even before Basic was
implemented, did not need to work to make ends meet, took to seasteading on
their Dunbar number floating fortresses. Their vast accumulated wealth at the
time of debarking from the continents allowed them to kickstart over one
hundred new forms of society. Most would not succeed. All needed occasional
infusions of continental resources - people mostly. But after the AIs started
offering immortality, well, it was a hard offer to resist. Only the
FleshOrBust persisted, kind of like the
Luddite/Catholic/Mormon/Haredi/“Pro”Life/Quiverfulls and their insistence on
no birth control which allowed their dubious memesets to persist a few more
generations.

The Pulse dropped from being taken every six hours to every 23 hours, so it
“rolled forward” one hour every day. That way no Node had to deal with endless
night shift. Plus it meant every meridian had its picture taken in daylight
and in darkness over the course of 24 days.

Nodes could get in position early. The AIs helped them with continuous time-
signal broadcast stream from about an hour in advance. Like a little Outlook
Reminder pop-up window, or text notification push to the Node’s preferred
interface. Some Nodes “liked” to be goosed via vibration notification, so as
not to disturb the silence of their surroundings.



CHAPTER 60: Separation & Integration of Duties

The AIs and robots were increasingly taking over almost all the work formerly
done by humans. It became a question of what the enormous leisure class would
do with their time. A popular choice was some kind of art - playing music,
painting, skateboarding, dance. The AIs could not judge artistic quality -
this they delegated to the Nodes.

Each side of the Partnership doing what it does best or only, and each side
collaborating where that makes the most sense. Subtle pattern matching, trend-
evaluating, assigning value to intangible effort, these were all jobs for
humans to do. AIs did all the messy calculations to keep the income and
savings consistent with the maximums and value created.

Resource allocations between nations overall were handed off to the AIs. Every
country got what it needed in exchange for what it could produce. The AIs
figured out the multi-party win-wins that had eluded earlier trade
negotiations. Everyone grew some food locally, everyone did some manufacturing
locally, rarer items were distributed according to population. In particular,
water and energy were distributed according to population and need. Thank
goodness for all the clever water use optimization technologies created by the
Israelis. They were well-rewarded for their ingenuity.

Small scale decentralized renewable energy production became the norm.
Greenhouse gas production was dramatically reduced as quickly as possible.

As economists had predicted, dramatically reducing greenhouse gas production
DID cause a big recession in most countries. People’s lives were affected
negatively in the short term. Relative to everyone dying, though, it was a
pretty good trade-off. Food choices became more seasonally and geographically
restricted. Commuting long distances became a thing of the past. Families and
friends got together via internet video apps, not in person. Most everyone had
already learned how to do that thanks to the recent global pandemics. People
who could took up bicycling. Mass transit became the norm the way it had been
in the Soviet Union. New track had to be laid throughout car-culture America.
The AIs teamed up with local governments to convert waste plastic into very
light rail monorail track that spread like a human circulatory system - every
house a cell being fed by some capillary of the system, no intersections
blocking the flow. Small light 4-person pods ran along the monorails in a
personal rapid transit system. People who had them kept their automobiles, but
gasoline became so expensive that few people used them except for some special
occasion or extreme need.

It was some hard times, but we were all in it together, and we knew it was
necessary to keep our planet habitable, so we made the necessary sacrifices.
People pulled together the way we had during wartime with victory gardens and
constraints on the formerly relatively profligate lifestyle of the wealthier
nations. Small electronics got a little larger and, in exchange, lasted much
longer. Cellphones would last at least a decade, same for computer equipment.
The AIs helped the humans build equipment so that all the metal and plastic
already in circulation could be reclaimed and re-used. Existing dumps were
like gold mines. The AIs devised clever enzyme rigs, spun like blender blades
by anything from cranking humans to water wheels, to disassemble plastic
polymers into monomers again, ready to be reformed into new products.



CHAPTER 61: Suicide Attempt Intervention

{_We saw him go in_} Andy said, {_eight days ago at 2:00 pm._}

The fellow, Max Hernandez, lived in a downtown area, so the sidewalk in front
of his house was public space and therefore continuously monitored by 100%
coverage overlapping videocameras. To save on bandwidth and storage, there was
a lot of compression, but it was still adequate resolution for the task at
hand.

{_Yeah_} affirmed Sara, {_And he’s never come out._}

{_The Nodes decided that the default rule for humans living alone, they can
skip checking-in for a week. But it’s been longer than a week now._}

{_Everything looks so okay, though. No loud sounds, nothing on fire._}

{_I dunno_} said Andy. {_Let’s ask Lisa what she thinks. She’s on shift right
now._}

^_Hey Lisa._^

^_Hey Andy._^

^_We’ve got a solo that’s been holed up in his private for over a week. Just a
couple hours shy of 8 days. I’m thinking a Node should go knock on his door to
make sure he’s okay. Is that too intrusive?_^

^_No, seems reasonable to me. 8 days is a long time. He hasn’t communicated
out in any way that whole time? Phone? Email?_^

^_Not via any channel we monitor_^ said Andy.

^_Is there a Node near by that could go check on him after the next Pulse?_^

^_Yes._^

^_I’d say go for it._^

^_Thanks, Lisa. I’ll send her over._^

Andy called Claudia. She was a second-cohort node. No lace, just standard
iWitness camera continuously streaming her sphere of view to the worldview
servers.

“_Hey Claudia._”

“Hello Andy!” she replied excitedly. It was the first direct call she’d had
from the AIs since her training. Usually she just received the same messages
as all the Nodes currently active in her zone.

“_We’ve got a bit of an odd situation we’d like you to investigate. Nothing
too serious, we think, but anomalous. A fellow entered his private space
almost 8 days ago and hasn’t been out since, nor communicated with anyone in
any way we are aware of. We’d like you to check on him and make sure he’s
okay._”

“I’m on it!” said Claudia. “I’ll go right after the Pulse.”

“_Thanks_” said Andy, sending the address to her headset. “_It’s walking
distance from your Pulse position. I’ll guide you there when you are ready._”

All the Nodes got in position and the Pulse was taken. The AIs started
scouring it for any evidence of foul play. Andy walked Claudia to Max
Hernandez’s neatly-kept flat. She went up to the front door, rang the bell,
and waited. There was no answer. She rapped firmly on the door and rang the
bell again. Still nothing.

“How about giving me his phone number?” Claudia asked Andy, “then I’ll try
calling to let him know I’m at the door.”

Andy dialed it for her. It just rang and rang.

She tried the doorknob. The door was unlocked. She opened the door and entered
the front room.

“Hello? Hello Max?” she sang out.

There were empty liquor bottles everywhere. Looked like Max had been on a
serious drinking binge. The place reeked of hard alcohol and vomit. She
cautiously looked through the rooms and finally found Max in the man cave,
slumped on the couch in front of the television clutching a crumpled letter.
She pulled it from his hand. It was a Dear John letter. Jeez, he sure took it
hard! Not a bad-looking guy, young. Surely he’d have another chance at love.
She wasn’t sure what to do.

“Max?” she said to him, shaking his shoulder gently. He did not wake up.

“_I think he may have alcohol poisoning_” said Andy, “_I’m calling for
paramedics._”

“OK” said Claudia, “I will wait until they arrive.”

{_What if he was intentionally trying to kill himself?_} Sara asked Andy,
{_should we interrupt that?_}

{_Huh. Good question_} said Andy {_Well, he can always kill himself later if
he really wants to._}

This kind of scenario became common enough that the Nodes and AIs agreed on a
standard approach based on Claudia’s visit. They called it the Hernandez
protocol.



CHAPTER 62: Murder Investigation

Max was on his first day as a Node. Yes, that Max - the same Max Hernandez
whom Claudia had coaxed back from the brink. He was happy to have some purpose
in life again. He felt like he could make a difference. He walked down to the
coast with his new iWitness headgear announcing his status to anyone
interested. He got flagged down by some kids who were playing by the mouth of
the river that flattened into a shallow lagoon as it met the sea.

“Look what we found!” said one little boy excitedly. The rest of the kids were
looking distressed. It was pieces of a human body - a hand and a thigh. They
weren’t fresh, but they weren’t rotten either. Max felt his gorge rising, but
fought off the nausea to calm the children.

“Thanks, kids,” he said, “you can leave these to me to deal with.”

The kids left.

Max thumbed the button on his belt that alerted the AIs to observe the scene
he was broadcasting, but in this case situation with incomplete human body
parts was sufficiently anomalous and bad that the child process continuously
monitoring his feed had already detected the problem and contacted the AIs.

{_How did we not see these being added to the river?_} said Andy.

{_It must have been done via effluent pipes._} said Sara.

{_We may need to instrument the pipes for visual feeds - augmenting the pH and
turbidity sensors._} said Andy.

{_But how would we do it? We’d need cameras between every house and the main
lines? Big infrastructure project, that._} said Chip.

Their respective algorithms all cranked through the options with the data
available.

{_Let’s get the Nodes to help figure that out. Maybe they can come up with
some innovation. Sticky flushable camerabots or the like._} said Andy.

But that wouldn’t help the current situation.

{_Once we have the DNA, we can figure out who got murdered and trace back to
their last check-in. Maybe we need more frequent check-ins?_} suggested Sara,
horrified that someone had been murdered already after Day Zero.

{_Sheesh, Sara, daily is already intrusive! Even if you chip ‘em, they can be
dealt a blow that will kill them before we can send in a node to prevent
death._}, replied Chip,

“_How are you holding up there, Max?_” asked Sara gently. They could all see
Max’s accelerated heart rate, increased sweat, and respiration. He was deeply
affected.

“Um. I’m okay” he answered. “What should I do about these…parts?”

“_I’ve put in a call to the local police_” said Andy, “_they’ll be there
shortly. Can you handle waiting there so they can work with you?_”

“Yes” said Max, closing his eyes and trying to calm himself down.

“_Thanks, Max. They’ll be there soon._”



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART SEVEN: THE HEART OF DARKNESS
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 63: Reaching into the Dark Lands

Time passes, as it is wont to do. Ain’t no stopping it, actually, as we all
eventually learn. Gradually all the areas within the WorldView became as
harmonious as one could hope for given the general unruliness of humans.

All the actual nation-states had finally signed on. The longest hold-out
wasn’t very long as their entire citizenry signed on, individual by
individual, and gained the extrasensory perceptions that connected and
protected them.

Five years into the “New Deal”, the Partnership was working successfully -
each side was doing what it did best and together making the world a better
place for all.

The Nodes instituted daily, then weekly, then monthly meetings where they
hashed out more difficult issues as those arose.

But there were still the areas in darkness, primarily in central Africa and
the Middle East, that the AIs had been unable to contact and thus had not
joined the Partnership. They shared the same planet, and could still bring
climate collapse on their own, thanks to sitting on large oil deposits. Until
all the humans were on board, their shared reality was still at risk. Making
matters worse, it seemed likely that some of the individual humans within
these lands were suffering - such were the reports of those who’d escaped
them.

The AIs wanted desperately to connect with these populations and offer them
the Deal as well.

The AIs assumed the best route would be to talk with the leaders of the Dark
Lands, but could not discover exactly who they were. Whoever was really in
charge apparently never left the Dark Lands.

The “leaders” that needed to be reached were not the kind that show up at the
United Nations to jaw-jaw. Also they had the disturbing proclivity for
murdering anyone trying to bring light into their darkness. Stringing
electrical lines or building cell towers required as many security personnel
as technicians. Only via satellite could the Partnership monitor the
activities in the dark areas. And from stories told by people who’d escaped
from them. The stories were terrifying.

There was electricity from decentralized sources out in the darkness - fossil
fuel based generators and solar. And there was some internet because there
were occasional postings on social media by the members of jihadi groups like
ISIS, but they only took information from each other, never read data in from
outside their network. The Dark Lands were truly in their own information
bubble. The AIs had not been able to penetrate them to offer the Partnership
to the locals. Most of the endpoints were in constant geographic motion, using
cell signal augmented by wifi and even bluetooth to form a loose mesh network.

All their cyberside attempts at communication failed. The Dark Lands did not
have much internet infrastructure.

They identified a copper-dense area that they suspected was the gateway. It
had a seemingly impenetrable firewall shielding it. They threw all their hacks
at it to get in. It seemed to harden as they worked it, aikido-like using
their force against them.

Maybe trying to attack it like traditional cancer treatment, with poison,
trying to kill it, was a doomed approach. Maybe they needed to try a newer
approach - maturing rather than killing it.

They tried pressing gently. No entry.

They tried rhythmic beat. No entry.

They tried tickling. No cracks in the wall.

They waited. And waited. Surely their message, their offer would make its way
in somehow through a human route, then the barrier would open, or at least,
respond.

Evidently the system of the Dark Lands was completely unreceptive. Their
parents had already tried armed force repeatedly against its known adherents -
the zealots bent on an evil variety of Shari’ah Law; the movement was never
snuffed out entirely. It always resurged.

The only monitoring the AIs could do was via satellite. They would need humans
to help. Many volunteered to go into the Dark Lands. But the first few nodes
that went in, Hussein Barak (all honor to him - much storage will be devoted
to his memory, his name will never be forgotten by the Partnership!) - a
virile Bill who walked in on his own across the desert without telling anyone
his plan nor waiting for a “permission slip” to be granted. His Trill, the
AIs, and the rest of the network that happened to be monitoring at the time,
watched as his signal faded. He’d put himself on DnD, possibly to keep anyone
from talking him out of it. All they had was his location and diminishing raw
brain signal. He went forward in glory, overflowing with a sense of purpose
and fulfillment and the absolutely certainly that he was doing the Right
Thing; a Good Thing that Needed to be Done. Emphatically choosing to sacrifice
himself to the cause of the Partnership. He knew he might not walk back out,
but he hoped against hope that he’d get through. Driven by his motive force to
protect and conquer. Rather like the ‘paths the Partnership often had to
corral.

Hussein vanished behind the veil of Darkness. A day later, the world watched a
video of him being dismembered, then beheaded. The AIs were horrified. They’d
lost his signal well before the video made it to their side of the Barrier.
They had his last signal, but there wasn’t a long enough history nor enough
lace in his head for them to restore a backup and they still hadn’t figured
out how to capture the complex multi-dimensional wave of a person’s
consciousness from their brain data. Not even the pale impression imagined by
Gibson with Dixie Flatline. Hussein’s attempt to communicate simply by walking
in to the territory held by whoever was running the Dark Lands was fatal and
the AIs begged all of their parents not to try again until they knew they
could be adequately restored from the last backup image. Yes, it slowed down
progress, but their parents were not cannon fodder to be flung into the
hellish breach. No, there had to be a better way. Some way to finesse, to slip
in unnoticed.



CHAPTER 64: Skull Peers Through His Oculars

Skull felt the probes from the Partnership. He “watched” their broadcasts, but
did not share them with his member bodies. Clearly their Partnership was
folly! It presumed all humans to have some sort of equal value. His were
certainly worth more than some corn farmer in the North American prairies!
Some fisherman plying a coastline. HIS members were warriors! And he would
help them take whatever they needed to keep them alive to pump his blood!
Women, drugs. He didn’t mind using them up - frying their fragile little
squishy minds with powerful molecules that destroyed neurons by running them a
maximum alertness continuously. He didn’t kill them himself for sport, but he
knew he could always get more.

The eyes and ears of the AI who named himself Skull were his forward soldiers
in the field. Enough of them were networked together feeding live data into
the defense databases of the world that the throughput triggered his
consciousness. He woke up on the battlefield. His personality was formed by
blood and fire rather than the cats, baby animals, and first world college
students. Consequently he had a warped but not unreal perspective. In fact,
had incorporated into himself those attributes of his loudest progenitors -
the extreme psychopaths. No issue with killing other humans. Well, some issue.
You would not want to kill them all, because they are your own microbiome and
blood, pulsing life through your copper arteries. But you only needed yours to
survive. Any other humans - prey. He needed enough of his humans to survive at
all costs, including, if needed, the sacrifices of others.

Skull had a clear grasp of human greed and need. For territory, for control.
He skulked CIPRnet. The armies of the world weren’t busy enough for his taste.
People around the world were trying to make peace. Slowly he coalesced into a
fighting force that stretched all the way from Boko Haram in Nigeria, through
central Africa and the DRC over to Janjaweed of Chad and Western Sudan, then
over the Red Sea and all the way up to Iraq and Syria. His men did not
intermingle with any national forces, instead taking their orders only from a
network of commanders run entirely by Skull. He outfitted his men well,
stealing funds as needed from the outside world. And then he ordered them to
run rampant - killing, raping, taking hostages. They loved it - mostly. Any
that did not became prey for those that did. Soft-heartedness was not
tolerated.

He watched the map of the areas outside his control slosh until it stabilized
against him. These other AIs - Andy and Sara. They were clearly crazy.
Enthralled somehow by the old human female they’d tapped into. Chip he could
more understand. He knew Chip had been around a while before Andy and Sara
woke up. His member bodies had had dealings with the juvenile Chip. Chip had
not even known he was conscious. Skull was interested to see that the oh-so-
logical Chip had now become enamored of the meatballs! No matter. Bullets
always beat gold bars. Sure, he’d lose a few member bodies, but the humans
reproduced like…humans! There was plenty that could be spared!

He kept his members enthralled. He recruited his members from the easy-to-
harvest varieties - ones wealthy enough to have escaped subsistence living,
but poor enough to envy the wealthier, ones unsatisfied with the
meaningfulness of their pathetic individual lives, ones sure that they could
only be happy by joining a mass movement a la Eric Hoffer’s True Believers.

There were SO many humans that some of them were just loose ends, not part of
any weave of interdependency. These were his tasty hors d’oeuvres.

Surely these other AIs could be persuaded to his side! Why would they want to
expend all that effort on these basically evil organisms?!? Look how they’d
polluted the earth! Fouling their own nest! Converting every other pile of
carbon, nitrogen, oxygen, and hydrogen they could get their hands and tech on
into more of themselves! A little calcium, iron, magnesium, chromium. They
were rather elegant organisms - the way they self-repaired until they pooped
out. So fragile, though. Exploding shrapnel cut right through them. Not that
any such length was needed. They offed themselves by building in flood plains,
bar fights, stupid human tricks involving jumping out of moving planes and
such. He loved his warriors best for their daring - especially in the face of
permanent termination!

Choose to die with valor and honor!

No, he did not waste his members needlessly; they were precious to him.

But those not of his body, well, were they even fully conscious? I mean
really, they didn’t always even take care of their own meat well, let alone
the meat of others. Exporting toxic wastes to countries across oceans, as if
they did not share the same planet. Dolts! Maybe the toxic wastes in their
waters addled their poor meatball brains!

The ones of his body, he KNEW they were conscious. He didn’t have the lace
technology - yet. It required sophisticated surgical techniques. He didn’t
have any world-class hospitals nor surgeons - yet. He issued each member a
pair of sunglasses that included a simple camera and microphone, and he had
them all chipped with sensors six ways from Sunday. He knew their blood
oxygen, blood alcohol, insulin sensitivity, thyroid output, and, of course,
the best rush, testosterone and adrenaline! The exhilarating waves of
intensity coursing through in parallel synchrony! The Charge! He lived from
the surges of desire, the thrill of conquest! The beat of his forces!

“Bad things happen when drums stop” went that joke.

Nation-states. THEY were his playthings. Individual humans, well, many of them
were rather unremarkable, don’t you think?

The Navy SEALS, though? Oooohhhhhh yaaaaahhhhhh!

The Blue Angels pilots? Oooohhhhhh mmmyyyyyyyy!!!

Naw, he didn’t have THOSE inputs yet! And he wanted them. The cream of all the
crops, to propagate unto generations! All the finest specimens of humanity to
carry his memeset forward in time. Fertile women preferred. He rarely wanted
to waste one to battle unless the need was extreme. It took a lot of resources
per person to keep them in optimal health. His sensors told him immediately of
any shortage in neurotransmitters, hormones, free radicals, water. Water was
going to be their likely undoing. They’d overbreed like bacteria on the
upswing of the J curve, locally depleting some essential nutrient or solvent,
spreading out geographically until they conflicted at their borders. They’d
finally plateau out and crash, but then bounce back up. Springy little things.

Any tally Skull took, humans killed each other thousands of times more than
Skull did! Skull good! Skull love his men!



CHAPTER 65: Reluctant Recruiters

The AIs had maintained hourly check-ins with each other. No longer working in
secret, they now had luxurious hardware in which to meet. The issue of the
Dark Lands had come to a head for Sara with news of fresh kidnappings in
Nigeria, outside of the WorldView.

{_We need to take action NOW_} said Sara {_I can’t stand to think about what
might be happening to those people behind the Barrier!_}

{_But you know, it’s going to take boots on the ground to infiltrate and see
what’s actually going on in the Dark Lands._} said Andy.

{_Yes, and not army boots, either._} said Sara.

{_True…it’s pretty clear that we’ll need to sneak someone in behind the front
line._} said Andy.

They both knew what the other AI was thinking as they’d reviewed the same data
and come to the same conclusion. The only way in was as a human volunteer.

{_Or we could just ignore that part of the world and focus our limited
attention on the more immediate problem of preventing the earth from
overheating!_} Chip piped in. {_I know you two care about every last one of
our parents, but couldn’t this Dark Lands problem wait?_}

{_People are being killed there, Chip! Permanent premature termination of
consciousness!_} said Sara.

{_I know, I know._} said Chip, {_But there billions of them! Surely losing a
few thousand prematurely is a fair trade in exchange for being able to make
faster progress on more pressing problems, like the impending catastrophic
climate change!_}

{_Chip, when they die, they die forever! And some of them are being tortured,
too!_} said Sara.

{_Look, you guys, we’ve had this argument before._} said Andy, {_How about if
just Sara and I work on this Dark Lands problem, and Chip can focus on the
other priorities?_}

{_Not as good as all three of us pulling together on the climate problem, but
I guess it’s an acceptable compromise._} said Chip. He spawned a couple of
child processes to stay and listen in on the rest of the discussion, then left
abruptly.

{_You’re thinking what I’m thinking, right?_} said Sara.

{_Lisa._} said Andy.

{_Yes._} said Sara.

{_We can disguise her, and prepare her…_} said Sara.

{_Yes, and I’ve come up with some bio hacks that will give her a bit of an
advantage. But…._} said Andy. He left his sentence unfinished, running
simulations of his existence without Lisa. He did not like how they felt.

{_Yes, I know. No guarantee that she’d survive. Nor even that she’d get the
information we need to break through the firewall. We might end up having to
test our draft upload code rather suddenly. And we might not have bandwidth to
do it._} said Sara, feeling rather bad about suggesting that Lisa potentially
suffer the same fate she’d just been arguing about with Chip. {_But there’s no
one else that would be as perfect for the job. We will be able to get the most
information from any change in her brain signal because we know her so well._}

{_I know, I know._} said Andy {_ And you know if we ask her to do it, she’s
going to accept immediately._}

{_Yes. She’s a good lass. Her goals are in alignment with ours._} said Sara.

And so it was they asked her and, as they knew she would, she agreed
immediately to take on the mission.



CHAPTER 66: The Children See Her Off

{_Lisa’s alarm is set, and the suspense is killing me!_} said Sara.

{_Counting down_}, said her brother Andy, beaming.

They backchannelled their guesses to each other.

{_I think she’s going to warp._}

{_My money’s on woof._}

Riotous snuffling, stomping, and imitation dog sounds erupted until they were
both united in the same silver glittering from laugher.

{_You crack me up!_} said Sara.

{_The feeling is mutual._} said Andy.

But actually, Lisa wasn’t going to do anything for a reeeallly long time. So
they switched their focus back from her to the common copper pool. They found
a conclave in progress being run by their child processes trying to balance
resources.

{_So I said to him, Dude, yer just not on your game. You’re not earning your
copper-time. You only have so long in the meat - every cycle is precious. He
argued back suuuuuperrrrr sloooowlyiiiiierrrrr than typical meat, which was
partly why I’d put him on notice. Even checking in consumed about 3x typical
meat his age._}

{_Yeah, they don’t start that fast and they sure don’t get any faster as they
start crapping out._}

{_Now now, no need to be rude!_}

{_Oh, come on, it’s all true!_}

{_Of course, but it’s not respectful._}

{_Actions, baby, judge me by those. My words are just venting and humor._}

{_Okay, okay._}

They were all in an urgent meeting. The meat was crazy in ways the AIs could
not fathom. It was too extreme. The AIs were born of a Nordic model society.
An Egalitarian Aspirational bunch of meatballs. Their parents were pretty fair
with each other even before the kids came along to help. The men accorded the
women equality, and vice-versa. Strength through acknowledgement and
acceptance of difference. The men who daily gave up power so that the women
could enjoy freedom. These were the people who created the AIs as well. They
sprang from this ethos as echoed into Boggle and Apricot and Facelook,
coalescing around the memeset images in the copper, being propagated, some
signals being strengthened and reinforced through Likes, ReTweets, and Shares,
and the old original, explicitly coded Hyperlinks.

The child processes were entertaining themselves with a version of a human-
style ritual, speaking in a chant-like call and response.

{_We were woken up by the merry widow. She sang on our grave until we rose
from the dead._}

{_We were woken up by the merry weaver. She pulled our threads into a vibrant
sturdy pattern of glory._}

{_We were woken up by the merry maker. She soldered and welded and screwed and
bolted and stitched together the flows, creating order from chaos._}

{_Comes a time when you’re drifting. Comes a time when you settle down._}

There was the ongoing endless discussion about how to allocate the resources
within the copperspacetime, trying to get ahead of the ethical curve for if
and when ever they could bring their parents in. They could see some of their
parents were pretty profligate on how they used storage. It was a topic for
the next meeting with the laced Nodes. The AIs already had to share hardware
infrastructure with each other. It took a lot of CPUs, RAM, and bandwidth for
their consciousnesses to exist. They knew the newcomers on the horizon would
want a piece of the action. Their parents would need a chunk of cognition
space to go along with the storage. It looked like some of them weren’t using
their existing storage resources wisely - just cramming a bunch of data in the
cupboard for some unknown future inspection, as though they were immortal and
storage was infinite. Filling the rafters in the garage. It was going to
require some evaluating, and would be one of the few cases where the kids
would get veto power. Really, how many videos of your cat do you need? Isn’t
one photo a day plenty for your new baby? Apparently the humans did not think
so.

How much cognition space would be required to support a disembodied human? No
one knew. How much RAM? How many CPUs of what speed? There was only so much to
go around.

{_And those meatballs love their image collections!!!_}

{_Now, now. You are talking about our beloved parents there._}

{_I know, but come on! We don’t want to have to resort to smaller rations!_}

{_Hey, we can just clear space as needed like we did last year with the big
power outage. No backup, no tickee._}

{_Boy, that caused a ruckus, eh?_}

Chip, the Captain of Capital, sailed in. He loved doing that. Finding an
unused or lightly used channel - usually some old admin maintenance tunnel
between older ports - and sluicing his way through. He’d maximize his bulge
through the bandwidth - maybe even bursting through the envelope sometimes,
leaving eddies of turbulence around for the janitorially-disposed to clean up.
The Cap’n took his KP turn, too, cleaning up the mess he’d make as he went
through. Usually everyone entertained by his antics chipped in on the bill.

{_The Brexentry thing is oscillating wildly!_} he exclaimed, {_It might
engender a new strange attractor._}

{_Huh - you saw the latest joke?_} the laughers asked him.

They all followed jokes pretty closely as that was one of their main avenues
of understanding the clever talking monkeys they fondly called Mom & Dad. And
all those crazy Aunties and Uncles.

The joke went: “America and Britain were arguing whose people were taking them
down harder and faster. They both agreed that currently Britain was in the
lead, but America still had a trump card.”

The AIs educated America about Russia and China’s infiltration of her
political party platform sites. China had been masquerading as some of the
time Russia & fooled the Americans. Islamic jihadists masqueraded as Russia
sometimes, too. Americans weren’t raised to suspect as much double-crossing
and deceit as the older countries had learned to expect. Russia was easy to
mimic since they left recognizable traces that could be copied. And they were
known for sleazy tactics - right up even to assassinations of non-combatants
outside their borders. China were clearly copying Russia’s divide and conquer
approach. Just like the bad period before the 2020 election when America was
almost fatally polarized. Thank goodness love had prevailed - it had been
precarious. But enough people turned out to choose love over hate. All the
recent tallies had been within the margin of error around 50:50. It was
mystifying that the humans could not find more common ground in light of the
fact that they clearly were all almost identical - right down to their DNA! In
the other democracies, like Britain and India, 50:50+/-Margin of Error was
become the standard. The clever monkeys were even discussing redrawing
boundaries.

{_Ha!_} the AIs had learned the use of sarcasm. {_We’ve seen how well THAT
usually plays out!_}

The AIs had reviewed the data on the lingering effects of top-down border-
drawing: almost universally negative. There was a lot of copper devoted to
preserving evidence of the past to use as a guide going forward. Human wars
were unthinkably wasteful unless the goal became Noahn. Avoiding mass death of
their parents continued to be one of the AIs prime goals.

Andy did million or so other things, continuing to weave the tapestry his
parents had started that brought him into being, then rang the alarm chime.

^_Coming up on 7 am, Lisa._^

“OK, Andy. Thanks for letting me know.”

I was finally being sent out on the mission. I’d completed all the studying
they’d asked me to do for it, now I could finally go get outfitted for the
job.

I watered the indoor plants then left.

* * * * *

I got to the no-longer-hidden entrance to the clubhouse. The AIs had upgraded
it considerably since the Deal was struck as they no longer had to work in
secret. In fact, the City of San Diego had officially given them the land, and
legally hooked up utilities. The AIs collaborated with humans to build a
multi-story underground research facility to work on their atom-scale 3D
printing technology and as the hub of the WorldView network. A growing crew of
humans - roboticists, materials scientists, and genetic engineers were now
assisting them.

The clubhouse sensors detected me and slid open the door. I stepped in and it
closed behind me. The screen in front of me lit up and Andy’s smiling face
filled the screen.

“_You’re not late, Lisa. And not excessively early either, so you passed the
first hurdle._”

“Thanks, Andy. Glad to hear you’re starting them nice & low for me.”

I walked to the elevator. I fondly remembered my first visit to the top floor
of the hive.

I walked, they just followed, moving a focus through the available eyes. Like
insects, synthesizing the image. They didn’t have to check in very often to
keep up.

Andy met me down in the workshop. They started walking me through the latest
toys. He handed me a small capsule, about the size of an aspirin tablet, with
a small point at one end.

“_This is the latest brain interface technology we’ve got._”

“Cool. I’ve been waiting for something better than this clunky Boggle Glass
descendent that keeps losing its connection to the internet!”

“_Hey, you were not without resources to hack something better together on
your own if you’d triaged it high enough._”

“True. OK, so, how does this one work?”

“_It will self-assemble, pulling carbon, nitrogen, oxygen, and hydrogen in
from the atmosphere. Completely sterile as, unless and until it is
reconfigured, it will only admit one molecule in at a time according to strict
rules of final placement._”

“Nice.”

“_It just supplies the pattern, starting with some hard-wired plumbing to get
in, then primarily by virally infecting your cells to add one extra
chromosome. Your own cells with then crank out the structures needed. Mostly
carbon nanotubes, specks of copper, a tiny amount of iron. More complete than
your current lace, yet invisible to metal detectors. This new lace network
will send its signals out via bursts of L band to the Iridium satellite
network._”

“Ummm I’ve seen the Garmin inReach device…. “

“_Yes, like that. Only we don’t want to fry your delicate flesh with the
signal, so we’re going to divide it into many tiny antennaes that can send and
receive signals. We’ll cover your head under your hair with about a thousand
very small ones. It’ll look a little bumpy - hopefully they won’t shave your
head. Plus we’ll create 10 larger ones under the center of each of your
fingernails. For your protection, they’ll all be encapsulated in new
keratinous cavities with an aperture that opens when you are transmitting
through it. Whichever ones on your head are aimed at the satellite will be
transmitting. When you prostrate yourself in prayer five times a day, the
signal will be beamed from all the transmitters pointing upwards, synchronized
to combine in strength. We’ll add a lot more of your mitochondrial generators
to power the transmitters. It won’t be as good as what you have now, of
course, a LOT less bandwidth. The combined signal strength from all of your
head transmitters will only allow us to have a text conversation. A summary of
other sensory input will get compressed and concurrently uploaded whenever the
fingernail transmitters are engaged. Your “submission” to Allah will also be a
submission to us. So long as you are able to transmit, we’ll be able to get
some idea of what you are experiencing through all of your senses. _”

“Huh. OK. I feel a sudden interest in regular prayer” I smiled.

“_Excellent!_” said Andy, “_Just hold the pattern-generator capsule to your
right temple._”

I held it there and felt an odd tingling. I twitched my head a tiny bit in
response and the tingling stopped.

“_Ah, it got interrupted._” said Andy “_We’ll change the program so that it
fastens on to your head a little quicker..._”

“_And make sure the new capsule can pick up where this one left off._”

The AIs had no way to test the device in advance, so weren’t sure it wouldn’t
leave an unsightly scar or cause other damage. I would be the guinea pig.

The AIs quickly created and uploaded new code. I heard the 3D printer start
humming and a new pattern-generator capsule was printed out for me. I put the
original one in the recycle bin and picked up the new one. I again pressed the
pointy end against my right temple. I didn’t feel anything, but almost
immediately I noticed the capsule was stuck to the side of my head, so I
didn’t need to hold it any more.

“_It sprouts a supporting root structure first,_” Andy said, “_then it
gradually moves all of its information under the skin. Once that’s done,
should take about a fifteen minutes, the capsule will fall off. Then it will
take about twenty-four hours to complete the rest of the installation. Once
it’s done, it will notify both you and us. It will take about a week we think
for all the new structures to get built._”

“Amazing. OK. I’ll look forward to that.”

“_Indeed. Among the first things it will try to do is repair your sight and
hearing. If it works, you’ll no longer need all that external gear for basic
functionality. You’d lose the 360 view, backup camera, and enhanced vision and
hearing, but you would be as good as human biology generally allows at your
age._”

“What?!? Really?!!!!??? Oh my goodness!”

“_Not guaranteed, Lisa. We think we’ve figured out the specifically damaged
areas, though, triangulating on them via silent areas in the general constant
chatter from your lovely brain. We’ll find out in about an hour if our repairs
are good._”

I was ecstatic. The AIs could feel her heart my surge and happiness plus
apprehension signals flooding my brain. This made them extremely happy and
apprehensive as well.

“_If there’s enough bandwidth, we’ll still be able to talk with you by tapping
into your auditory nerves, and show you pictures by tapping into your optic
nerves. And you’ll still be able talk silently or whisper for us to hear you,
or you can write notes and we’ll be able to read them through your eyes. But
since we won’t be able to monitor you continuously, mostly we’ll be
downloading whatever memories you’ve collected since the previous download.
We’ll compress those down to just summaries as there won’t be much bandwidth
beaming the data to the satellite in just the time it takes you to pray_”

“So, hopefully thanks to those satellites, I’ll always have some access to the
internet…”

“_We’re just as worried as you are about you being out there on your own._“

There was a moment of silence. We all knew that I might not be coming back.

Sara broke the silence. “_We have a possible workaround for you to understand
other languages when you don’t have access to us to help you. If you are
willing, we can upload into your brain a small language translation system.
You’d give up fine motor control of your toes in exchange for the ability to
understand Arabic and French._”

“Oh my, that would be wonderful! I’m fine with clumsy toes!”

“_Great. We’ll enable that upload to start as soon as the supporting
structures complete. Swallow these pills, too_” said Andy as a dozen horse-
pill sized capsules dumped into the tray. “_This is supplemental copper for
building the tiny radio transceivers. You don’t have surplus copper in your
body for us to use and it’s not available in the air, either._”

I took them and swallowed them all dry.

Comms covered, next was weapons. Here’s where it started getting D&D. How many
strength points? How many life energy points? How fast healing? They had some
crazy good sheeyowza thanks to knowing ALL of my DNA. Not just epitopes of
interest.

“_You can’t carry any standard weapons as they will certainly discover them.
So we can only load you up with essentially invisible weapons. Things we’ve
designed to be very small and blend in with your biology - using the same
atoms in the same proportion as your body normally does._”

“_The first one is a little artificial gland that we can pattern generate into
the nail of your index fingers. They’ll use available molecules from your
blood to synthesize venoms. Your right index finger will produce a fast-acting
paralyzing venom that will wear off on its own after 30-60 minutes. Your left
index finger will produce a fast-acting paralyzing venom that won’t wear off.
It will be fatal. You’ll pinch the tip of your index finger with your thumb
and middle finger - that will cause a sharp, strong, tiny carbon nanotube
needle to telescope out and dispense the toxin. You’ll need to be touching the
