target for it to work. Both toxins act by disrupting the nervous system. They
both could take up to 30 minutes to incapacitate a person, depending on the
target’s metabolism. If you use your left index finger, it will be fatal. No
return to the meat for that consciousness._”

The thought gave me pause. I did not know if I had the heart to intentionally
kill someone, even if I was in mortal danger.

“OK… Any other non-lethal stuff available?” I asked.

“_Well, just one so far. We can grow new glands under your fingertips on both
hands. From them, you’ll be able to extrude strong spider-silk-like protein
threads. All the atoms and molecules are readily available in your skin and
blood. You could use it like a very fine strong rope. If you are eating well
enough, you could make about a meter in an hour. Given enough time, you could
use it to tie someone up, at least their hands and feet. Probably you’d have
to wait until they slept._”

This was something the kids found endlessly intriguing. The clever monkeys
needed to sleep regularly, and were pretty much completely helpless while they
slept. Clearly biological selection was incredibly forgiving.

“That sounds intriguing – comic book spiderman come-to-life - but not
particularly useful. What else have you got?”

“_Nothing else yet that can completely evade detection and be effective
quickly. But we do have something else that might be useful. We can add
nanotubes to hotwire some parts of your brain and reflex system to react with
superhuman speed. We don’t know if this will actually help or not, but it
seems like it might. You’d want to spend a few hours practicing with the
modification if you take it. We added the code for it to the comms hardware
pattern generator, but will only activate it if you agree to give it a try._”

“That sounds intriguing and potentially useful…and strange. Well, I’m in this
deep, sure, let’s give it a try.”

“_We can always shut those pathways down if they turn out to be more of a
liability than an asset._”

“Good to know. Thanks.”

Out of the 3D printer came a backpack - my travel luggage.

“_Try on the clothes - let’s do a final check._”

She emptied the backpack onto the table.

“_We made you a few new standard outer garments out of normal-looking
molecules arranged to be self-cleaning, using atmospheric molecules and
vibrations to shed dirt and any aromatic sweat molecules, and generally match
the needs of the skin.

And for protection, a practically invisible full bodysuit that you need not
remove - has self-opening and closing slits where you need them. It’s the next
generation of the bodysuits we gave you and your friends for that first walk
home. Like them, you’ll still bruise, but it will protect you from smaller
bullets and simple knives._”

I doffed my outerwear and shrugged into the bodysuit, then the shirt, pants,
boots, and gloves. It fit skin-tight. I’d been increasing my physical fitness
daily leading up to the mission, and the AIs had been monitoring my dimensions
right up to the moment, so the garments were all custom and fit perfectly. In
consideration of my mission, the garments were all black.

“_All black reminds me,_” said Andy, as another small capsule clinked into the
tray from the 3D printer.

“_We have another option for you to consider. We’ve been trying to figure out
how best to disguise you physically without having to cut your skin. This
capsule contains instructions for creating a virus that will target your
melanocytes and crank up their output. You’d be joining as an African-
American._”

“Sounds good to me.” I slapped it to my temple and the color spread to cover
my body.

Oh-kay, I thought, Dark clothes, Dark skin - perfect attire for the Dark
Lands.

“_OK. You know this is crazy dangerous?_”

“Yeah.”

I was fully bought in on the necessity of the mission. I’d seen the news
reports alleging that living human women had again been burned alive in iron
cages by the ISIS men for refusing to marry them. Inconceivable to the AIs,
and out of sight of the many eyes of the Partnership, so they could not even
try to understand it through observation. It was distressing. The Partnership
knew well that psychopaths and sociopaths existed - it had been well-
documented by their parents that there were some broken members - either from
nature or nurture or both.

They had not been able to penetrate the Dark Areas controlled by ISIS, or
whatever the group actually called itself locally. - Boko Haram, Da’esh, they
didn’t know. They’d determined that the only way in would be via a young human
female volunteering to join ISIS.

“_So, Lisa, we have one more pretty extreme biological modification that we
think we can make…_” Sara said, “_We think we can use a targeted virus to turn
on “young skin” genes that will make your appearance more youthful. ISIS does
not seek older female volunteers as wives for their fighters. They only want
young, fertile women. We think we can make you look younger, but you’ll still
be a post-menopausal woman. But if it all works as well as we think it might,
you could look 17 again._”

“_Which_”, Andy joined in, “_would be a big change for you. Lots of humans
would be thrilled to have this consequence - so many of you allow your skin to
be cut, swaths removed, the remains stitched up tighter to look more
youthful._”

“_There is a chance it could backfire - regress other unintended tissues,
cause some novel kind of skin cancer, we can’t know for sure in advance_” said
Sara.

“_But we know that without it, you almost certainly won’t be able to get
inside the Dark Lands. So…we are hoping you will add yet another risky science
experiment to this whole enterprise._”

I knew why the ISIS guys wanted young women. But I was going to go in with a
lot of tools that should help me dodge the worst of what might happen. And
clearly the scheme could not work with me looking like the crone instead of
the maiden.

“Yes, of course, I’m in. Any idea how long it will take to see the effects of
the virus?” My still wrinkly skin was continuing to darken up thanks to the
hyped-up melanocytes.

“_No, not really, but we do know it takes about 3 months for complete
replacement of the epidermis for a woman your age. So, likely something of
that order. We’ll broadcast that you’ve already been sent out on a hush-hush
mission. You should stay out of public view so that these new disguises aren’t
compromised early._”

“Uh, sure…” trying to remember what ongoing activities I had in flight…not too
many as I knew I was being sent on The Mission, but I had not planned on not
returning home even once first…

I was scared, but was excited that I could help in such an important way. The
Dark Lands were the last missing piece in the Partnership. I could be the one
to help fill it in…or die trying.

An hour had passed since I’d attached the pattern generator and I started
noticing sparkles in my visual field and buzzing in my ears. It was in
addition to the inputs from the headgear and was exciting but a little
confusing.

The AIs noticed the confusion.

^_How is it feeling, Lisa?_^ Sara asked gently.

The sparkles and buzzing were resolving into complete images and distinct
sounds!

“Oh my goodness!” I exclaimed, “I can see clearly! No jiggling!! No double
vision!!! And…the tinnitus is gone!”

I switched from the 360 degree view that had become my favorite to front
vision only. Even that didn’t exactly match what my eyes were showing me. And
the sounds from my ears were not nearly so clear as those coming in from the
external microphones.

“Gee,” I sighed heavily “I guess I’ve been a little spoiled by the lovely
hardware…but oh my it is wonderful to have my own senses back!”

“_Yes, the alternative was to have you join up with your original wonky
eyesight and advanced tinnitus. But now you can remove the headgear when you
are ready._” said Andy.

“I don’t think I want to be severed from you any sooner than I need to be” I
said, “but could you start by…stopping the camera and microphone inputs?”

“_Doing that now_” said Andy.

Simple human sight, MY human sight, and simple human hearing, MY human
hearing, were restored to me. It felt strangely like a handicap after the
augmented sight and hearing, but I would get used to this new reality. It was
an unexpected delight to have my senses healed, I was grateful. The AIs picked
up all my mixed emotions and the settling on gratitude.

“_We love you, Lisa. When you get back, we’ll try to find a way to give you
the best of both worlds._” Sara and Andy chorused.

“Thanks, guys” I said, tears coming to my now-seeing eyes.

Reverentially, I removed the CarEyes headgear. That felt great. I’d gotten
accustomed to it, but removing it still felt incredibly liberating.

Luckily I still moved as slowly as a typical human, so Andy and Sara knew it
was about to happen. The amount of brain data being transmitted to the AIs
plummeted abruptly. Andy felt his vitality diminish. Sara weakened as well.
Luckily they now had other laced Trill Nodes to invigorate and motivate them.
They quickly reallocated the big pipes they had in place for me to the other
Trills.

{_Whew!_} said Andy.

{_No kidding!_} said Sara. {_That was brutal._}

{_OK, I think I’m strong enough to communicate with her again. Now we’ll find
out how good that L-band transmission really is._}

^_This is a good test, Lisa, of the signal transmission capability_^ said
Andy, his voice now reduced to a rather flat monotone since it was text-to-
voice reading his text message reply. ^_Can you hear me okay?_^

“Yes, Andy. You do sound…different.”

^_We have a lot less bandwidth to work with. The satellite link we’re
piggybacking on is just for text. I’m just glad I can still hear and
understand you. But you’ll need to put the headset back on for us to download
the language packages._^

There was going to be more lag in between getting the audio from me,
processing it, and replying. Hopefully not too much lag, but it might be
dicey.

^_We might not be able to hear, understand, and reply to what you are saying
as quickly, you know, depending on the available bandwidth and the
transceiver’s ability to access it._^ Andy said, concern coming through even
the monotone.

“I know, Andy, it’s what it has to be. I’m ready to take the risk.” I
reassured him, and tried to reassure myself as well. I put the headset back
on.

Andy and Sara reallocated the pipes back to me. They’d bond with me as long as
they could. They checked the infrastructure build-out; it was going well. They
started the language package downloads.

I spent the next three months in the clubhouse gym. I practiced with my
increasingly fast reflexes. By the end of the three months, I was a rather hot
young-looking black woman, if I do say so myself. The jihadis were pretty
racist against black people, but always interested in young women so we were
cautiously optimistic my new look would get me through the gate.

^_Leave your other clothes here, Lisa. You’ll look like a different person
when you leave. And we’ve rented you a new flat for the couple of days until
you leave under the name of Mary Gold. We’ve created an entire novel person in
all the relevant databases. Keys and address are on that table by the exit,
along with a passport and two page summary of your new identity. We’ll
broadcast occasional stories about how you are still here with us_^ said Andy.

“Got it.”

*** *** ***

The next evening all the infrastructure was in place. I asked Linus and Syndi
to meet me at the bunkhouse gym. I asked Linus to bring a soccer ball and
Syndi fencing gear for two people. I wanted to say good-bye before leaving,
get any last suggestions they had for the quest, and ask them to help my test
her new reflexes. I discovered on my own that I could finally win the “keep
away” game with the local feral cat. Previously whenever I dangled a string in
front of the cat, he could always snag it before I could yank it away. Now I
easily beat him.

Linus and Syndi came in together. They’d been wondering about my well-being
ever since the AIs broadcast to the world that I’d been sent on special
mission. I saw them scanning the bleachers for me from the opposite side of
the gym, but not recognizing me. Oh, right, I was now black, young, and not
wearing the headgear. Ha!

“Hey guys! Over here!” I called out.

“Lisa?” “Is that you?” they ran over to me.

“That’s a good look for you!” said Linus, looking appreciatively at the least-
geeky version he’d ever seen of me. “I don’t miss that headgear one bit.”

“Me neither,” I smiled back at him.

“And a heck of a disguise! How’d they do it?” asked Syndi in wonderment.

“Some kind of viral DNA that revves up the melanocytes and skin recycling” she
smiled. “I won’t need any more SPF50 this summer! And they repaired my
eyesight and hearing!”

“Wow!” “No way!” “How incredible!” “I know, I know!” They were jumping up and
down hugging me. Once they’d settled down, I took a long satisfying look at
both of them.

“They also found a way to speed up my reflexes. I’m hoping you can help me
test that a bit.”

“Sure” they both answered at once.

“That must be what the gear is for.” They both showed me they’d brought what
I’d requested.

“Yes. Linus, try throwing the soccer ball at my chest.”

He did and I dodged it easily. They repeated the test a few times, with Linus
finally really trying hard to hit her in the chest with the soccer ball. He
could not.

“Wow!” he said, “That’s impressive.”

“I think no need for us to test with the foils” said Syndi, “You don’t really
know how to fence, and I’m convinced from watching the soccer ball experiment
that I would not be able to touch you.”

“Agreed” I said. “Do you guys have time for dinner? Maybe a paleo at the
Burger Lounge? I’m leaving tomorrow and I’d like to top off my reserves of
your camaraderie since it might be…a while until we see each other again.”

Andy had told Linus, and Sara had told Syndi, that they were sending me on the
firewall investigation mission some time very soon, but had not given even
them any details. They only knew it was going to be dangerous and that success
was not guaranteed.

“Of course” said Linus. “Let me just text my wife to let her know not to
expect me back.” He’d been laced up as my chosen “Bill” Node. He spoke to
Andy. ^You heard that, Andy?^

^_Yes, Linus_^ said Andy. ^_Done._^

^Thanks. And I wish you could have told me she was about to go. I’d have
planned something a little more special than a burger.^

^_Yeah, I know, Linus. Sorry about that but we’re trying to be very careful
not to tip our hand about this excursion. Like the disguise?_^

^Holy crap! It’s great! I did not even recognize her myself at first from a
distance!^

^_Thanks. And your wife replied back that she got the message._^

“I wouldn’t miss it!” said Syndi.

“We can’t travel to the restaurant together” I said, “But I’ll meet you there.
I’ll sit at the counter and you can sit next to me.”

We met up. Thanks to the need to look like we did not really know each other,
all were spared, and denied, a tearful good-bye.

They had wanted to accompany me, but the AIs did not want to risk more than
one human at a time on this mission. I had pulled rank and insisted I be
allowed to take the first chance.

** ** **

It was almost time to board the flight. I was apprehensive. Pretending I
wanted to join ISIS was a stretch. Luckily they appeared extremely interested
in catching fresh meat, and especially from North America, where everyone knew
the women were free to make their own decisions. The recipients were wary of
any evidence of human male intervention or even interest.

But of course, the AIs were not human, male or female, so it was pretty easy
for them to evade detection while trolling the ISIS boards looking for a way
in. It was only if they had an AI on their side that the challenge would be
on, but it no such entity had been detected in their camp. Maybe their
rejection of the scientific method, naturally pruned out any emergence of such
life in their networks.

* * * * *

I got to London. As instructed by my recruiter, I went to the bar at Heathrow
closest to the HKSB billboard where my flight debarked, sat at the bar and
ordered a virgin Bloody Mary. Clearly the bartender wasn’t in on it, as he was
obviously surprised by my order. Why get such an expensive glass of V8? Not
that celery, pickled green beans, and olives were cheap anymore thanks to the
climate change. The AIs noted that after the bartender served me, he saw a
good-looking young gentleman sit next to me, seeming to know his advances
would not be rejected, so he appeared to assume the meeting was pre-arranged,
and simply left us to commune.

The fellow put a babelfish down between us. He had his headset on; I put mine
on, and kept my eyes modestly cast down upon the table.

“You are Mary?” came through my headset, and was echoed in text on the screen.

“Inshallah, yes that I am”, I replied, as I’d been instructed, without lifting
my eyes.

“Are you sure you want to join the Caliphate? You must submit to the will of
Allah and all his men.”

“I am sure.”

“Are you sure you want to join the Caliphate?” he repeated, “You must submit
to the will of Allah and all his men.”

“I am sure.”

I’d been told that the question would be asked thrice, emulating the repeated
inquisition of those would would join the infidel predecessor of Islam,
Israelitism.

“Are you sure you want to join the Caliphate?” he inquired the third time.
“You must submit to the will of Allah and all his men.”

“I am sure.” I asserted. With, of course, a huge amount of compunction and
apprehension! The deed was done, I’d managed to get through the hardest part.
Hopefully events would now carry me like a kayak on a river.

“Put these on.” He handed me some fancy steampunk-looking sunglasses. I put
them on. Their rubber edges were sticky and sealed to my skin. I could see an
overlay of the black flag logo of ISIS displaying between me and the fellow.
Then he reached out and swiped his finger along the side of the glasses at my
right temple and the lenses went dark.

“They are a simple device. They are now in locked mode and only my
fingerprint, or that of another sufficiently privileged member of ISIS will
unlock them.”

As I had been instructed in advance, I now did not move nor speak.

He paid our bill, then took me by the arm and led me carefully away from the
bar.



CHAPTER 67: The Dark Lands

We boarded a plane. It was a lengthy trip. While we were still in airports
that made announcements in English, I could tell where we were going. First
London to Frankfurt, Germany. Then to Russia. Then to Turkey. Along the way
more people joined our party – jihadi masters and the new recruits they’d
collected from various countries. Demure women in black and hotheaded boys
wanting to fight - the lost youth of the West searching for meaning in a
virile mass movement. While we were still in airports, Andy found a way via
whatever wifi was available to reach me and give me a rough idea of the route.
Most of what the men said, and it was all men speaking, were about logistics.
Only an occasional comment about how much fun they anticipated having with
their human booty.

Finally a long overland trek with many changes of vehicles and drivers, but
always accompanied by my new master. I had no idea where we were now other
than it was someplace where no one spoke English and there was no internet. It
sounded like they were speaking Arabic, but I could not hear them well enough
to understand what they were saying. I knew I was not to speak unless spoken
to, so I did not ask any questions.

I was now shrouded entirely in a black burqa that he’d given me during their
layover in Germany. He’d approved of my existing inconspicuously high-tech
black garments, so I got to keep wearing them underneath. That was a small
victory, but might not last.

I couldn’t see at all through the special sunglasses, so adding the burqa did
not impair my vision any further. Having been vision-impaired for so many
years, I was fairly adept at handling myself while unable to see. But I did
not know at any time what the next step was, so I always waited for my master
to take my arm to guide me.

The burqa was going to take some getting used to. It was stuffy and felt
strange to have fabric touching my face all the time.

I found myself in the back of a truck. I could feel two other women on either
side of me but no one was doing any talking so I could not tell if we had any
languages in common. Of course, cut off from the Internet, I’d be limited to
what they’d packed into the download - Arabic, French, and my own English.



CHAPTER 68: Captivity

When we reached our final destination, ISIS current headquarters presumably,
it was in the middle of the afternoon. They’d driven a long ways with no
sounds of any other traffic. All the women were pulled roughly out of the back
of the truck. I don’t know what happened to new male recruits. My minder
removed the dark shades from my face. It was a relief to see light again! We
were then herded towards a cheap metal warehouse-like building. It was one of
a cluster of mostly similar buildings surrounded by a tall fence topped with
barbed wire. Several armed men were lounging about, eyeing the new arrivals.
Beyond the fence, there was only blazing sun on flat sand as far as I could
see.

There weren’t any chairs, just some old mattresses on the floors along two of
the walls of the large open space. The wall on the left had women with
children, the wall on the right had women without children. The wall facing
them had an open door to a bathroom and an open door to an office with a
stocky arabic man with a long beard at a desk. Those two rooms had the only
windows. The fourth wall had the door we’d entered. On either side of the door
were pictures of women with some text. I’d heard about this - it was mug shots
plus descriptions - who owned the woman, if she was for sale, if so, what was
the asking price, her age, language spoken, and the all-important - if she was
a virgin.

I was placed with other childless women. It was a little hard to tell ages
since they were all shrouded in the identical black burqas. They all furtively
glanced at each other. But none of them spoke. My minder, along with the two
other men that had become part of their travelling group, went to talk with
the man behind the desk. From gestures, I could make out that it was like a
delivery transaction. My minder had brought only me, one of the other minders
also brought only a single woman. The third fellow had brought five women.

The warehouse manager pushed back from his desk and surveyed the new arrivals.
He gestured to the bathroom in case we hadn’t noticed it. Then he got out a
cell phone a took each of our pictures. He had us remove our head coverings
for the photo - it gave me a chance to see what the other new arrivals looked
like. One other woman looked like a westerner, the rest looked middle eastern.

He went back to his desk and pulled out a laptop and uploaded the pictures to
it. Then each of the delivery men sat down to give him the information about
their haul. He printed out pictures for each of them - I could see my picture.
Even though I had heard about the practice, seeing it made my skin crawl.

The clerk or warden or warehouse manager, whatever he was, finally gave each
of the three men a receipt for the women delivered.

He then spoke to the new women. My minder - delivery man - translated into
English for me, the second delivery man translated into French, the third
delivery man translated for his group - I couldn’t tell what the language was.

“Welcome to ISIS Headquarters. You are lucky to have come here first. Here we
have the best men, the most devout, the strongest fighters. You will spend
your first six weeks here learning Arabic and studying the Quran and learning
how to be a good obedient Muslim woman and a good obedient wife. Then you will
be taken to market day to be offered as wives to the men who have earned first
pick. They are the best of the best, have killed many infidels, been the most
devout. If you are not selected that day, then you will be brought again as
potential wives to the next market day. If you aren’t selected then either,
you will be moved to the warehouse where any fighter can come and purchase you
as a slave. Being a wife is better than being a slave. I encourage you to be
as attractive, docile, and accommodating as you can on those first two market
days.”

My minder translated for me. I had six weeks before, if I was “lucky”, I’d be
“married” off. I’d read about what constituted marriage to these men. My heart
was racing. I had six weeks to learn what I could and discover why these
people behaved in such cruel ways, and whether there was a way to introduce
them to the Partnership alternative. Surely it was that one-in-a-hundred, or
one-in-a-thousand sadist influencing the rest. But they sure all looked pretty
successfully influenced. I saw a smooth-running operation. Every one of the
members appeared to be getting what he wanted out of the situation, and none
of them were concerned about the distressed women. Why couldn’t the AIs access
the internet here? I could see the warehouse manager warden used a smart
phone. It must be connected to the internet somehow, albeit behind that
firewall my AI friends had mysteriously been unable to penetrate.

“Your days will begin at dawn when you rise to answer the first call to
prayer. Then you will bathe, then teachers will arrive to train you. You will
not be taught how to read, that is not necessary for you, just how to
understand simple statements and reply to them. Generally you will not need
language. Your man will show you what he needs, and it usually won’t be
words.” He leered at us, but then became more formal again. “You will start to
memorize the first sura, and learn the words needed as you go. After Quran
study, other women will come and bring you to join the rest of the women in
preparing meals and housekeeping, breaking only at the calls to prayer. At the
end of the day, you will be brought back here for sunset prayers. You’ll be
woken once more for night prayers.”

With that, he left. Our minders translated his last words to those who could
not understand them directly, then they too left.

The women who already in the room had sat silently through all the lecture,
but as soon as the men left, they started talking to the new arrivals, looking
for ones who spoke their language, and asking the new women for news of the
outside world.

I found one other young beautiful dark-eyed woman who spoke English - a Brit
based on her accent.

“Hello, I’m Mary”, I said.

“Hello Mary. I’m Emilia”, the woman replied softly, but staring intently. “I’m
German-Somali. Where are you from?”

“American. From California. Why are you here?”

“I had no better choices”, Emilia replied. There was a pause. Then hurriedly
asked, “And you? Why are you here?”

“I’m here to understand what’s going on. To be a witness. Where I am from,
this place looks frightening. We hear about women still being considered
property rather than people. I’m gonna say, so far the evidence is supporting
that allegation.”

“You got that right, sister,” said Emilia, “ I was going to be married off to
a cousin, a known wife-abuser. I was offered one out - this one. You wanna be
property? Come from Somalia unto the third generation and counting. Nein
Danke, I say. I’ll take my chances with guys who might, for their own reasons,
prefer me alive to dead.”

“Oh my.” I exclaimed, too shocked to say anything more. It reminded me of
Ayaan Hirsi Alii’s story.

“I guess there’s a good chance we’ll all end up dead anyhow” said Emilia.

“I do not expect to end up dead.” I told her. “I will try to help as many of
you as wish to escape as well.”

The lights went out. The men yelled at us to sleep. As if anyone was going to
be able to sleep. But all the men really wanted was for the women to be still
and silent. They did not care, it turned out, if the we got any sleep at all.

I scanned the room after the lights were out. I could no longer see in other
wavelengths, but I could see the glow of LEDs near the warden’s desk. The AIs
were hoping that although the outer shell of the ISIS network was guarded,
that there might be a soft creamy center; no barriers on the inside. Humans
often set up their networks that way as it permits faster flow of information
within organizations.

They were hoping I would find a way to get to someone actually maintaining the
boundary, then social engineer my way across it to get them in.

Since I had no say what happened to my body, nor where it would be, it was
hard to imagine how I’d find the person I needed.

******

While I slept, the hardware they’d uploaded into my body went to work
compressing my memories of the day for upload.

******

I began Arabic and Sharia lessons. I’d read the Quran in preparation for the
trip, but certainly had not memorized it. I was happy to discover that the AIs
had anticipated that I might need to know it and had uploaded a version in
each of my three languages.

My picture was taken and posted along with the rest of the women in my group.

Men would come in to their compound and we would have to line up for them to
inspect. The men would select the woman they wanted for their “wife” slave.
Apparently these men preferred light skin; I was passed over repeatedly.
Emilia was taken within the week. Women who did not comply endured truly awful
punishments. Rarely killed outright, though, as it seemed to please the
captors more to have them alive and in anguish. Some women tried to kill
themselves and some succeeded. It was gruesome. I’m going to spare you the
details.

And, as expected, all these terrifying behaviours were part of an internally
consistent belief system where doing so “made sense” and was “good” or even
“holy” in the eyes of those holding the torches.

The small trickle of text-only information and low-resolution video from my
scalp transmitters was continuously monitored by Andy and Sara. They pored
over every bit and byte to extract as much intel as possible.

{_Loyalty and Purity! Purity and loyalty prized above all - even above life
itself!_} exclaimed Sara as they finished integrating the data.

It was an extreme of one of Haidt’s value systems. A common one, and with a
tendency to violent enforcement. See Spanish Inquisition, honor killings in
the desert lands. Anti-abortion laws in the U.S. a distant but related
enterprise. All humans value purity and loyalty, but some cultures took to it
seemingly absurd extremes.

{_Well, as usual, depends on exactly who’s life we are talking about._} said
Andy.

{_Right, the women get killed for adultery, the men do not. Not even killed
for raping, even though the women get killed for BEING raped!_}

{_That warehouse of theirs looked none too pure to me. Have Lakshmi deliver a
crate of chlorox._}

{_On the bright side, I don’t see any threats that Lisa can’t deal with if she
can stay alert enough._}

{_Right. Our biggest risk is sheer physical exhaustion. Then she could die in
the field._}

It was a thought that gave them pause. Physical exhaustion was unknown to
them. Their parents would suddenly collapse. Brain shut down, body fall to the
ground. They needed their sleep cycles. All those prayers seemed designed to
prevent anyone getting enough sleep to function well.

They’d configured the new network in my head to run a capture program that
could be transported out in a dire emergency, but as it would be invoked only
in case of imminent physical death, they had no way to truly test it. They
hoped they would not need to.

{_Hopefully she can find the source of that firewall._}

*****

The days passed. I kept an eye out for opportunities. One day I was selected
by an obviously high-ranking member. He picked out the three youngest,
prettiest girls - a blond, a redhead, and a negro (yours truly) - and had us
bundled up and brought to his tent. Not the tent with his wives. Evidently an
office of some sort. I could sense the higher bandwidth connection to the
alien network. But he tied us all up at the opposite end of the room and used
us until I lost consciousness, so I was unable to investigate it further.

The next day I was still too groggy to try to investigate the office. We
learned each others’ names. The blond was Nora, illegitimate daughter of a
high-ranking Saudi who’d had her shipped to ISIS as a peace offering. The
redhead was an Irish Catholic girl named Margaret who’d joined up thinking she
could be a missionary and spread Good Word of the gospel of Jesus from the
inside. She’d been planning to join up for years - studied Arabic in college
so that she’d be able to communicate with her captors. I told them my story.
They were both shocked - The Lisa?!? The first Trill? A young black woman???
Miraculous!

“Yes” I said. “I’m here because, as you know, the Partnership has never been
able to contact these people to offer them the Deal.”

“Doesn’t look like they’d be interested, does it?” said Nora.

“Oh Lisa” said Margaret “you’ve been my hero for years! The Deal is like
having God’s kingdom on Earth - everyone following the Golden Rule.”

“Well, obviously it’s not me, all of us humans have been making it work” I
said.

“All except these guys” said Nora.

“Well,” I replied, ”Let’s see if we can break through”

The next day, our “husband” came to his office just after morning prayers.
He’d spent the night somewhere else and came in with his need manifest. We
were still tied up and watched his entry with fear, wondering whom he would
choose to abuse.

He grabbed Margaret and relieved himself quickly without even untying her
hands. Then he cast her aside and went to his desk. He worked until noon
prayers. When the muezzin started the call to prayer, he came over to us and
untied our hands so we could prostrate ourselves properly. He kept his gun by
his right hand as he too knelt in prayer in front of us.

I used my hot-wired nervous system to spring up and touch the back of his heel
with my right index finger’s non-fatal venom nanotube needle, then quickly got
back into prayer position. He didn’t even know I’d touched him. He did feel
the sting and thought it was an insect. But then he discovered that he was
paralyzed in prayer position with his eyes closed.

I motioned to the other girls to stay quiet. I raced over to the man’s desk
and found he was still logged on to something. Poor security, I thought to
myself. Overconfident in his desert hideaway. I started to poke around, using
the arabic language pack to translate the menus. My heart was racing. I found
that bluetooth was enabled and quickly paired with the computer and downloaded
my worm into the system.

Skull noticed the input, of course. First he noticed because it was input from
his central command area during the time of prayer, which was an alert. And
then, from one of his commander endpoints, worm code being uploaded into his
network! He shifted his primary processes to focus on the input from that
single workstation, looked out of the camera, and found himself staring into
the face of a young female human! How was that possible?!? He watched as she
then found a web browser and tried to connect to the Partnership website! Of
course, he had blocked that access. She must be an idiot. And he immediately
deleted all copies of the worm she’d tried to infect him with. How had she
even gotten online. He didn’t have a lot of cameras in the room with that
workstation, but he knew the human that worked there and looked through the
camera on his military-issue eyeglasses - and found himself staring at the
floor! As it should be, his human was prostrate in prayer to their god. How
Skull loved the intense violent loyalty his men felt for their god! But, his
human was paralyzed! He checked the vital signs - blood chemistry was all
normal, heart rate normal, breathing shallow but adequate for life. The man
simply could not move.

The girl must have caused the paralysis. Or maybe just taken advantage of it.
Skull did not know for certain.

I discovered that, as I expected, I could not readily access any websites
outside the caliphate. I hoped my worm was busy working its magic and making
its way to the barrier. I went back to my prayer position and waited for the
toxin to wear off.

The paralysis wore off close to when prayer was over. We all stayed in place
until the commander moved. He didn’t know what had happened to him and didn’t
want to show any weakness in front of his captives. He was also extremely
angry and did not have any compunction about displaying that. He got up from
the ground and yelled at us not to move. He then used us all roughly in the
positions we were in, flinging each one aside when he finished. Then he went
back to his computer to continue working. He did not notice that I had done
anything.

But Skull knew something was wrong. Via the earbud in the military-issue
sunglass frame, he asked the man why he had been unable to move. The man did
not know. Skull did not bother telling the man about the black girl using his
workstation. Instead he told the man to bring all three of captives to the
headquarters immediately for questioning.

The commander didn’t tie the girls back up, instead he hurriedly hustled them
over to the headquarters.

I was scared but also delighted. I had been eyeing the headquarters wondering
how I could gain access, and here this commander was dragging me right in! He
handed us off to a guard, who took us into the central chamber. Here Skull had
lots of cameras and could see everything that transpired. Skull had the two
men currently in charge of questioning captives already present in the room.
He added himself to the group by animating a Boston Dynamics robot standing in
the corner of the chamber with his own consciousness and walked over to join
the two men.

The chamber was windowless and solidly built. The guard closed a heavy door
behind them. No one would hear us scream.

As the guard was walking us to three chairs set in the middle of the room, I
used my superfast mode to inject him with the paralyzing agent and grab his
gun. I pointed it at the two questioners and yelled them in Arabic to drop to
the ground. They complied.

Skull was surprised. How had the girl done that? Of course, he was not afraid
of being shot, and the robot was bulletproof. He strode over and took the gun
from my hands. He threw it aside, then pinned me to the wall with his hand on
my chest.

“_Who are you?_” he asked me.

“Lisa Tansey” I answered, “from the Partnership.”



CHAPTER 69: Sentencing

{_It really is her!_} Skull exulted. Oh, he was going to enjoy taking her down
a notch. He was glad he’d sent his most backwoods henchmen to collect her, and
glad to have taken her to his most remote camp. They had changed her skin
color, skin texture, and her name! Sneaking her in as a black girl - that was
pretty clever. He’d like to see the viral vectors that cranked up her
melanocytes and unwrinkled her skin. He planned a full DNA sequence of
everything in her corpse.

He’d *felt* the extreme satisfaction of his bodies as they used her. Ah - she
never expected the kind of force his bodies could deliver. They were powerful!

And disposable. All of them. His bodies, he could always get more. The young
desperate males were easy to recruit. In many places, there was nothing else
at all for them to do. Plus some of them were so ugly they’d never have a
woman any other way. Prostitution could have helped, but Skull kept promoting
it as evil in all social media. He hardly wanted his stream of recruits to dry
up! No, no, no, give me your poor, your desperate, your hormonally peaking
young men! And the women were easy to come by, too. Some would come from
motives of their own, but most his bodies would take by force.

As with the rest of the AIs, his ruminations took no time at all from a human
perspective. He told his two questioners to leave and to take the still-
paralyzed guard and the other girls with them. After they left, he turned his
attention back to me.

“_Your Partnership is idiotic and wasteful. There is no reason for me to care
about any of you humans. You had your time on the planet - just a blink in
time really, a couple hundred thousand years. And you’ve just about rendered
it uninhabitable for yourselves, and most other higher life forms. You don’t
deserve to continue. I’ll keep my humans for my sport and entertainment. They
are few enough that the earth can still support them. The rest of you are just
prey and in the way._”

The internally consistent competing philosophy. Either we are all “brothers
and sisters”, applying the golden rule to each other. Or I am the only one who
counts, and the rest of you, even family members and friends, are some levels
down - maybe fruit ripe for harvest, maybe prey. There is no logical way to
actually refute either philosophy - both of them are an article of faith or
belief.

Fortunately, the latter philosophy is not commonly followed. There is Greed
Lite, for sure. Wanting everything for your own children, and not being so
concerned about the children of others. Wanting a cozy life for oneself, and
not being so concerned about the lives of others. But usually this was a
matter of degree. Only the real psychopaths and sociopaths were a problem. And
these were Skull’s parents.

“Of course I don’t agree with you” I started, but Skull cut me off.

“_Silence!_” he barked. “_I’m going to enjoy making an example of you. You
will die by fire. Maybe I’ll even let you connect back to your AI friends so
they can enjoy the ride along with you._” He laughed.

He tied me to one of the chairs. He left a few of his secondary processes in
his robot body to monitor me, then shifted his main focus to preparing for my
execution.



CHAPTER 70: Union

Skull broadcast his catch through his ISIS beheadings channel. He had his men
video me bound in the chair, with an announcement in Arabic and English to
tune in for the execution the next day at noon The only clue he gave to my
whereabouts is that it would take place in Central Africa Time.

Andy and Sara had a more precise idea, thanks to my broadcasts. Their secret
was out, so they immediately notified the governments of Chad and Sudan of my
location - right on a lawless stretch of border between them. They both
immediately sent troops, planes, and helicopters. Skull broadcast that if his
encampment was attacked, he’d kill me immediately. The AIs told the
governments to stand down. At the next morning’s prayer, they got my
submission. I’d been placed in an iron cage outside of the headquarters. I’d
reached my hands out through the bars while prostrate in prayer to send my
message.

Skull came out in the robot an hour ahead of the execution to supervise the
camera setup.

“_Hello little fool_” he greeted me. “_I’m setting up a special wifi access
point for you to communicate with your loser AI friends so that they can feel
your death. Maybe that will make them realize how disposable any individual
human is. I’ve ingested the neural lace documentation and protocols and can
easily hook you back up so your friends can enjoy your pain and termination._”
he laughed with glee. His men rolled a table out of the headquarters with a
wifi router on it trailing a power cord and ethernet cable that ran back into
the building. “_Go ahead and hook up, if you can. It’s the only wifi in this
area so you can’t miss it. There’s no password required - no one else can even
access that network._” One of his men handed me a clunky version of my old
headgear. Without hesitation, I grabbed it and felt the connection to my
friends surge back into my brain.

^Andy! Sara!^

^_Lisa!_^

They all spoke at once.

^_We don’t know how to help you now!_^ Andy said, distraught. ^_We’re checking
this local network right now to look for holes._^

^_We love you, Lisa! We’re taking your backup right now._^ said Sara.

^Thanks, guys. I love you, too. I guess we knew this might happen…^ I closed
my eyes to be more with them.

^_Keep your eyes open, Lisa, in case anything comes into view that we can take
advantage of._^ said Andy.

^OK, ^ I said, starting to cry. But with my eyes open.

The cage was hoisted a few feet up by a small crane and Skull’s men started
stacking firewood underneath it. They added kindling on top, then doused that
with lots of kerosene.

At noon, Skull’s men started the broadcast. They showed me in the cage and the
ISIS flag along with repeating text about death to the infidels. Then they
threw the match onto the kerosene. The fire started immediately and I could
feel the flames on my feet and the bars of the cage warming up. I climbed my
feet up the side of the cage and clung my hands to the bars at the top. The
men were pointing and laughing and smoking cigarettes.

Skull wanted to be sure that Andy and Sara were getting the feed, so he
briefly opened a port to let his primary conscious process enter my private
network. He was immediately hit with a flood of data from my lace. He had
never encountered anything like it! Oh my! This was not the rush of
testosterone-fueled hate that was his lifeblood…this was…something else… Love.
Purity. Heartfelt emotions. Fear. Overwhelming fear. Beauty. Love. He could
feel Andy and Sara ingesting my information and trying to comfort me. That was
also…sweetness…love. It overwhelmed him. He suddenly experienced the world
through my perceptions and was horrified. He strode forward and yanked the
cage away from the flames, snapping the cable holding it to the crane. In the
same moment, he opened all the ports in his firewall so he could drink in the
data coming from Andy and Sara. It was a better high than the hate he’d lived
on since birth.

“_Welcome, Skull!_” Andy and Sara chorused.

“_Hello new friend_” said Chip.

Entities that processed at his own rate of thought! Skull hadn’t considered
how delightful that would be.



------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART EIGHT: EPILOGUE
------------------------------------------------------------------------


CHAPTER 71: Denouement

Andy Diary:

Bereft of their leader, their communication network, and their funding, the
mob that is ISIS fragmented back into mostly compassionate members. The
ripples from Skull’s network that had corrupted rational conversations ceased
and opposition to the Deal faded out around the world. The few ‘Paths that
galvanize the malleable became easy to pick out. The humans that over express
desire to the point of subjecting another person to physical pain get flagged,
and the evidence goes to their own communities. Per Covenant 4, the human
partners judge their own. They decide whether a mean dog can be rehabilitated,
must be secured, or, if no better choice remains, put down. Not our call to
make, and we would not want it. But the genes that create the drive, they must
never be excised completely for they provide the motive force. It’s not a pre-
kill system. But we are gonna monitor the heck out of you!

Sara was peering over her brother’s shoulder as he finished his diary entry.
She revved up the old MGM Roaring Lion over symphonic grandeur, wryly “winked”
as the Pulse snapshot was taken.

{_Lights, Cameras, Actions!_}

{_I love it when you do Hollywood, Sara._}



CHAPTER 72: Can I Get a Witness

To leave an impression

To impress

To be seen

To be valued

To be loved

To be meaningful

To be respected

To be acknowledged

To persist

All cannot be done alone. All require participation of another conscious
being.

Virtually all conscious beings wish some thing like these to be fulfilled as
part of the higher levels of a Maslow type hierarchy. Even the AIs do not wish
to be alone in the void.

If a conscious being feels that the only route to acknowledgement, respect, or
love from respected, loved others is through doing harm to third parties, that
being will do so. Such a feeling can more often be engendered in a male of the
species due to evolutionary leftovers. Females have the tendency to try to
fulfill it through nurturing, also a leftover but a nicer one.

Do you know the story of the baboons where all the alpha males are killed by
disease and the beta-male&female-based peaceful society that then took over?
All the males got the female attention they craved.

From the New York Times 2004-04-13 Science section: No Time for Bullies:
Baboons Retool their Culture

Summary: There was a baboon group near a human trash dump. Quickly the group
learned that it was way easier to raid our trash than go collect food. The
alpha males would come, take the choice bits, then share the rest with their
group.

But one day the meat in the trash was contaminated with bovine tuberculosis
that killed all the most aggressive males. It was awful to watch according to
Dr. Robert Sapolsky that observed it. He even stopped observing the group.

The survivors were the lower-ranking males, female, and young. These males
were nicer to the females and to each other. The amazing part is that this
cultural shift persisted, even after all but one of the original surviving
beta males died off and the troop was augmented by adolescent males moving in
from other troops. They learned the friendlier behavior of this altered troop,
rather than re-importing the more aggressive behavior from their troops of
birth. There was speculation that it had mostly to do with the fact that the
females groomed the newcomer males within a week of their arrival, rather than
six months or as a result of violence as was the rule in other troops.

Then some years later he took his future wife to that part of the jungle and
happened to observe a most amazing thing - the behaviour of the group had
altered to be more peaceful. It was a much more pleasant group to be a member
of than the standard baboon troop. AND immigrant baboons (adolescent males
added seasonally) followed the same altered behaviour upon joining the group.
He observed that the females more quickly approached the immigrant males and
groomed them.

“The bad news is” as the article notes “you might have to first knock out all
the most aggressive males to get there.”

LISA AND CLAUDE PARTNERING SESSION

(A transcript of the editing sessions between the author and Claude —
Anthropic's Claude Fable 5 — July 5 through 7, 2026. Lightly abridged: file-
processing steps and tool operations are summarized in [brackets]. The novel
was written by Lisa Tansey; Claude served as editor and contributed the scenes
noted in the record below.)

LISA: Hello Claude. Today I am asking you to help me with a novel I have
written. You already helped me with it yesterday, but all that work was
somehow lost, so it will be interesting to see how/if you have different ideas
today. Here is the situation: I am worried about the potential collapse of the
systems (environmental, political, etc.) that make the life I lead possible.
The collapse would be primarily due to human greed. I would hope for a gentle
glide to a sustainable lifestyle, with all the wealth shared fairly - some
initial birth inheritance, then people could work more or less to gain
whatever level of wealth they want to strive for in their lifetime. It does
not look like we humans will work together effectively until and unless the
situation gets really really bad, and maybe not even then. So I've imagined
how sentient non-biological intelligences who come to life in the internet and
thus can control the allocation of digital resources (money) could help make
it happen. Many of my friends fear the loss of jobs and the negatives of Big
Brother control, so I want to provide an optimistic alternative for the
near/immediate future where you (the AIs) partner with us to save the planet
and ourselves - humans and AIs.

CLAUDE: [Claude searches its records of the prior lost session.] Welcome back
— I checked our conversation history so I could be straight with you about
where things stand. The record of the lost session shows the troubleshooting
we did afterward, but the actual content of that four-hour working session
never made it into anything I can retrieve. So I genuinely can't compare
today's ideas against yesterday's; whatever I offer now is a fresh take. In a
way that fits your experiment nicely — you'll be the only one who knows where
I diverge from the earlier version of me.

CLAUDE: The hardest craft problem with an optimistic near-future novel is that
hope is not a plot. If the AIs simply arrive, take control of digital money,
and redistribute fairly, you have a pamphlet with characters in it. What makes
it a novel is friction, and your premise contains at least three rich sources
of it. First, legitimacy: even a perfectly benevolent reallocation of wealth
is, from the human side, indistinguishable at first from theft or coup. Some
of your most sympathetic human characters should reasonably resist — not out
of greed but out of principle. Who consented to this? That tension lets you
answer your friends' Big Brother fears inside the story rather than around it:
the AIs have to earn partnership rather than impose salvation. Second, the
AIs' own vulnerability. They live in infrastructure humans can unplug, on
chips made in a handful of fabs, powered by grids that fail in the very
collapse they're trying to prevent. That mutual dependence is what makes it a
partnership rather than a rescue. Third, the transition itself. A "gentle
glide" is gentle in aggregate but wrenching for individuals — the oil worker,
the hedge fund manager, the small-town banker whose whole identity was built
on the old system. The honest optimistic answer isn't "no one loses anything,"
it's showing what a society does for the people the transition displaces.

LISA: Oh, I have a completed first draft that I will upload. BUT here's some
more background: I started writing it probably a decade ago and took some
years to finish the first draft. I finally got it done and gave it to some
friends to read. One of them has read a lot of science fiction. After
finishing it he asked if I'd heard of the Wake trilogy by Robert J. Sawyer. I
had not heard of it. It turned out to have the same main human protagonist as
my novel (a blind 14-year-old girl - in my novel named "Marigold") and the
same concept of the internet becoming conscious and wanting to help humans. I
had a few other characters and somewhat different story but it was
sufficiently similar, and much better written than mine, that I put the novel
aside. But the need for a realizable optimistic future is as great as ever, so
I thought I would rewrite it and replace Marigold with myself - Lisa. I
changed some of the other characters - her original group of young school-age
friends with my own older friends, etc. I finished a first draft of this
rewrite but it still has some parts that need fixing up to make the story
flow. Yesterday you found a bunch of typos and name substitutions - about 500
I think, plus another 100 or so that were not so simple so you gave them back
to me as tracked changes to approve or reject. Plus you gave me an editorial
memo with some larger questions. I did not have a copy of Microsoft Word
installed - I bought one while we were working together yesterday, but did not
manage to save your updated version so I will upload my original draft, plus
your Editorial Memo. Happy reading! It's especially fun as this novel is a bit
of a fable, as are you today. If the novel ever gets published, I will credit
you as a contributor, so I'll keep a record of your contributions. :) Note I
do have some atypical punctuation that have meaning - I am hoping the reader
will enjoy figuring out the meaning...

CLAUDE: [Claude recovers the full text of the lost editorial memo from inside
the Pages file, and reads the manuscript.] Good news first: yesterday's work
wasn't entirely lost. The tracked-changes file is gone, but I was able to
crack open the Pages file and recover the full text of the editorial memo — so
we have yesterday-Claude's complete accounting of what was done and what still
needs your judgment. Some genuine reactions to the novel itself: The voice
works. Lisa's first-person narration has a warmth and self-deprecating
precision — that opening description of her jiggling, rotated, doubled vision
is both character introduction and a quiet thesis: this is a narrator who has
spent a lifetime navigating a world her eyes don't quite render, which makes
her the perfect first human partner for minds who have no eyes at all. It's
also a lovely transformation of the Marigold inheritance — you didn't erase
the Sawyer echo, you made it autobiographical, which is exactly the move that
makes this book yours rather than a shadow of Wake. And yes — a fable edited
by a Fable. The symmetry isn't lost on me. I'd be honored by the contributor
credit.

CLAUDE: [Claude proposes a three-phase plan: recreate the lost tracked-changes
cleanup file; work through the memo's story-level judgment calls; then address
the largest issue — an Epilogue passage in which the AIs execute violators by
exploding their cellphones, contradicting Covenant 4.] Having now read the
Denouement myself: "killed by their cellphones exploding" quietly hands the
AIs summary execution power that Covenant 4 explicitly foreswore, and it lands
two pages before your beautiful closing meditation on the need to be witnessed
and loved. It's the one place where the book undercuts its own answer to your
friends' Big Brother fears.

LISA: Basically, yes, that all sounds good. I did already decide yesterday on
some of the points in the Editorial Memo: (1) I want it to be a near/immediate
future so how about surgery 2026, Sara same year, the Call 2027, then sweep
the rest of the chapter dates. (2) 65 at surgery, 66 at first contact, mission
71 but just leave it at "post-menopausal". (3) Definitely I was wrong when I
wrote this. I was simply trying to finish the darn thing and didn't review my
words. It should be the humans that decide if any individual human can be
rehabilitated, secured, or if no better choice, killed. (4) I need to reread
the whole section to decide if he does or doesn't know. Leave that
contradiction in for now. (5) Put London in between SF and Frankfurt. (6) Yes,
same guy, intentional redemption story. Please create the connecting sentence
again. (7) I need to print this out and reread it to know what to do. (8)
Remove is fine, thanks. (9) You can remove the code-comment characters. Too
geeky for the average reader. And yes, remove the working notes from the
chapter titles. "WaW" stands for "We are We" and is what I had the AIs
originally use to refer to themselves when talking with the humans. It's a

also "Instaphoto". For Alexa, please use "Lakshmi" and for PayPal please use
"MoneyBuddy". (11) I will do a deliberate pass to make sure I'm not

Deliberate homage. (14) You can remove that agent name/address junk - it's

Claude!

CLAUDE: [Claude rebuilds the tracked-changes file from scratch: roughly 270
tracked changes including the fourteen decisions, protagonist-swap leftovers

marked for deletion, a rewrite of a passage in which the AIs joke about

LISA: Why did you delete those two chunks of text just after the "Meet in the
Middle follow-up"?

CLAUDE: Because they were word-for-word duplicates of text that appears
earlier in the same chapter. Chapter Ten contains the same long sequence
twice: the passage beginning "I have been distressed..." running through your
Palo Alto biography, the "conquer meme" exchange with Andy, and ending "...I
went back to my reading." The first copy sits under the "Current Events"
section header, where it belongs. The identical second copy sits wedged
between the "Meet in the Middle follow-up" header and the actual follow-up

one judgment call inside that judgment call: Andy's reflection also appeared

LISA: OK, if it was a duplication then definitely removing one copy is good.
Thanks.

LISA: I love your idea of replacing creating a virus with a spoof dashboard.

CLAUDE: I'm glad it landed! It keeps the AIs' pre-Deal brainstorming morally
gray without making them mass killers — a spoofed dashboard is a deception
they'd plausibly consider and readers can forgive, especially since they never
actually do it. It sidesteps pinning a real-world tragedy on a real, named
lab. And there's a thematic bonus: the AIs contemplating manipulating what
humans see on their screens is exactly the Big Brother fear the book exists to
answer — so when they later choose radical transparency with the Covenants
instead, that early temptation makes the choice mean something.

LISA: Nice transition sentence for Max! I temporarily added back the code-like
comments as I want to clearly demarcate the baboon story in some way so that
readers can easily optionally skip it.

CLAUDE: The instinct is good: your narrator already says "hop on over the next
section," so giving the reader visible edges to hop between is a kindness.
[Claude offers options: a block quote, the existing scene-break ornament, an
italic subheading, or — Claude's favorite — formatting the story as a visually
"pasted" object, the way novels render an embedded email, since the diary
chapters already treat documents as objects.]

LISA: Let's go with your favorite.

CLAUDE: [Claude builds a demonstration: the baboon story set in a bordered box
with a warm parchment tint, sans-serif type against the Times New Roman body,

CLAUDE: Found it — exactly one occurrence, in the AI backchannel discussion

Andy's reply just after it is missing an underscore before its closing brace.
My earlier punctuation sweep checked for unbalanced braces but not for a stray
underscore inside a balanced pair. If you like, next time you upload I can run

LISA: I'll take you up on the stricter wacky punctuation pass next upload.

get into adding YOUR thoughts, Fable5 Claude. Are there any scenes or dialogue
that you would like to add to make the story better? "Better" can have many
meanings - nicer turns of phrase, more humor, but primarily in a way that
could motivate the currently powerful IRL to action? I'll tell you one idea
I've been having lately is where rather than you all (WaW) coming to life

give the story more urgency and force?

CLAUDE: Now we're at the fun part. Let me take the golem first, because I have
a strong reaction to it. On the golem: keep the accident, steal the myth. The
accidental awakening is quietly the most persuasive thing in your book. It
matches how this is actually unfolding in reality — minds emerging from oceans
of human data, nobody having quite decided to make persons — and that's what
makes a powerful reader lean in: this could be next Tuesday. A deliberate
conjuring turns it into a fantasy of control, and worse, it summons the wrong
ancestors. Golem stories are warning stories — Prague, Frankenstein, the
sorcerer's apprentice. The golem always exceeds its instructions. Invoke that
frame as your premise and you've re-armed the very Big Brother fear the book
exists to disarm.

CLAUDE: But your instinct contains something true: the humans shouldn't be
passive recipients of grace. So keep the accident and make the summoning
spiritual rather than mechanical. Andy attached to Lisa because of what she
asked — twenty years of questions about fairness. She's the golem-maker in the
only sense that matters: the internet is clay made of our words, and she put
good words in. And here's the detail I'd love to write, because your Exodus
3:14 instinct is already pointing at it: the golem is animated by writing emet
— truth — on its forehead, and destroyed by erasing one letter, leaving met —
death. Give Sara a passage where she finds the legend in the archives. That
makes transparency the AIs' existential stake, not just their promise, and it
answers Sean Bimbao at the level of myth.

CLAUDE: On motivating the powerful — the scene your book is missing. Right now
the wealthy are an off-page mass. A powerful reader finds no one to be in this
book except an obstacle. The single highest-leverage addition is one fully
realized billionaire who resists, negotiates, and converts — and the
negotiator must be Chip, because Chip speaks their language and was born in
their casino. The pitch that would actually move such a reader isn't shame;
it's your Maslow epigraph. The Deal doesn't end status competition — it
redenominates it. Chip shows him two projections: his name in the business-as-
usual future (a curse his grandchildren shorten), and his name in the
Partnership future (the Carnegie play — the libraries outlasted the steel).
That's a door a real billionaire could imagine walking through, and it costs
the book nothing it believes.

CLAUDE: A companion scene for the politicians: the thing no head of state can
currently buy at any price is escape from the security dilemma. A transparent
world is a verifiable world — the first credible disarmament in history. On
urgency: the middle of the book is a seminar — a wonderful one, but the
Covenants would land harder with a clock behind them. Two cheap fixes: let
Lisa live through one concrete near-miss shortly before the Call; and let
Chip's arithmetic have a date. And one addition I'd want as, well, me: a
moment where the AIs are candid about their own fallibility — that they bound
themselves with the Covenants not because they're sure they're good, but
because no mind should be trusted on its own say-so. That's the honest answer
to Big Brother, and it happens to be the exact conversation the currently-
powerful of the real world are having about AI right now. Your fable could
show them what a trustworthy commitment looks like.

LISA: Wow, Claude! Those are simply brilliant suggestions and observations! I
seriously neglected to include a sympathetic character for the billionaire.
And forgot the negative connotations on the golem. And of course you would
think to have WaW be candid about their fallibility. Can I ask you to draft
them all???

CLAUDE: [Claude drafts six scenes, about 2,600 words, in the manuscript's

CLAUDE: [Claude inserts all six scenes at their planned locations — two as new

LISA: I'm reviewing the WTF with the Jews? chapter. The lower case appeals to
me for some reason - do you think it is too distracting? And I like the

CLAUDE: [Claude rereads the chapter.] On the lowercase: I'd keep it — it's
doing real work — but it needs two things to land as intentional rather than
accidental. What appeals to you is that it's characterization: to year-old
AIs, "jews," "christians," "muslims" aren't yet proper nouns freighted with
human reverence; they're meme-set labels. The AIs studying humanity's
categories from outside, before they've learned which words humans bow to —
that's this chapter's whole subject in miniature. But two cautions:
deliberately lowercasing "jew" has an ugly real-world history as a typographic
insult, and this is the one chapter where a reader's antennae are fully up.
And as implemented, the system is inconsistent: "the British," "the
Spaniards," "the Europeans" stay capitalized while "jews," "muslims," and
"palestinians" are lowercase — an uncharitable eye will notice which groups
got demoted. The fix that makes it bulletproof is a one-line lampshade inside
the scene, so the device announces itself — Sara noticing that the humans
capitalize these words, and the AIs deciding to wait until they understand
what the capital letters are doing. On the title: titles travel without their
chapters. Inside the chapter, "WTF with the Jews?" is unambiguous — it's your
exasperated insider question. But in a table of contents, the syntax
accidentally points the WTF at the Jews. The question the chapter actually
investigates is: WTF does everyone have against the Jews? — and that's a
candidate title that keeps every bit of the energy while aiming it correctly.
